For the stochastic term, (
17b
) and the theorem’s scaling condition give

\widetilde{\delta}_{3,t}^{\dagger}\leq 2\eta\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,\delta)\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2\to\infty}.

This is the only place in the contraction step where the worst-direction row-wise noise level
\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,\delta)
is used.

It remains to bound the deterministic mean term
\widetilde{\delta}_{4,t}^{\dagger}
. The calculation is the same as for
\delta_{3,t}
in Step 2 of Theorem
1
: apply the Lipschitz bound for
\bar{\mathcal{G}}
to
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{Z}_{t}^{\dagger}(s))-\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{Z}^{*})
, expand
\bm{Z}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)\bm{Z}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)^{\intercal}-\bm{Z}^{*}(\bm{Z}^{*})^{\intercal}=(\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\widetilde{\bm{Z}}^{\intercal}-\bm{Z}^{*}(\bm{Z}^{*})^{\intercal})+s(\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}^{\intercal}+\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\widetilde{\bm{Z}}^{\intercal})+s^{2}\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}^{\intercal}
,
and then use (
C.39
), (
C.43
), and (
C.42
) to get

\widetilde{\delta}_{4,t}^{\dagger}\leq\frac{1}{16}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2\to\infty}+C\eta\alpha\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{n\kappa}}\,\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{2\to\infty}\,\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}},

after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary.

Combining the above bounds, and using
\alpha\leq\beta
and
\kappa\geq 1
, we obtain

\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t+1}^{(t)}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\Bigl(1-\frac{5}{16}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Bigr)\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2\to\infty}+C\eta\beta\sqrt{\frac{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}{n}}\,\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{2\to\infty}\,\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

Using (
C.43
), the induction hypothesis, and (
C.42
), we know
\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho^{t}\phi_{n}^{\dagger}\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\rho^{t}\phi_{n}^{\dagger}\sqrt{nr\kappa\sigma_{\min}},
, and consequently

C\beta\sqrt{\frac{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}{n}}\,\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{2\to\infty}\,\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\beta\kappa\sqrt{r}\,\sigma_{\min}\,\phi_{n}^{\dagger}\,\rho^{t}\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{2\to\infty}.

Since
\kappa\geq 1
, (
C.40
) implies

C\beta\kappa\sqrt{r}\,\phi_{n}^{\dagger}\leq C\alpha\left\{\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\kappa^{3/2}\sqrt{r}\frac{\phi_{n}^{\dagger}}{\psi_{n}^{\dagger}}\right\}\psi_{n}^{\dagger}\leq\frac{\alpha}{32}\psi_{n}^{\dagger}

after shrinking
c_{0}
. Therefore, we arrive at

\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t+1}^{(t)}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\Bigl(1-\frac{5}{16}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Bigr)\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2\to\infty}+\frac{1}{32}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\rho^{t}\psi_{n}^{\dagger}\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{2\to\infty}.

Invoking the induction hypothesis
\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho^{t}\psi_{n}^{\dagger}\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}
and
\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq(1+c_{0})\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}
, one can obtain

\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t+1}^{(t)}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\Bigl(1-\frac{9}{32}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Bigr)\rho^{t}\psi_{n}^{\dagger}\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{2\to\infty}.

It remains to bound the rotation drift in (
C.44
).
By (
C.43
) and (
C.42
), we note that

\frac{\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t+1}^{(t)}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sigma_{r}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}})}\leq C\rho^{t+1}\phi_{n}^{\dagger}\frac{\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sqrt{n\sigma_{\min}}}\leq C\rho^{t+1}\sqrt{r\kappa}\,\phi_{n}^{\dagger}\leq Cc_{0}<0.01

for sufficiently small
c_{0}
. Hence Lemma
6
applies and gives

\big\|(\bm{R}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-1}\bm{R}_{t+1}^{\dagger}-\bm{I}_{r}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{C}{\sigma_{r}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}})}\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t+1}^{(t)}\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

Therefore, using
\|\bm{Z}^{t+1}\bm{R}_{t}^{\dagger}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t+1}^{(t)}\|_{2\to\infty}
, it follows that

\displaystyle dist_{\infty}(\bm{Z}^{t+1},\widetilde{\bm{Z}})

\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t+1}^{(t)}\|_{2\to\infty}+C\frac{\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t+1}^{(t)}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sigma_{r}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}})}\Bigl(\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t+1}^{(t)}\|_{2\to\infty}\Bigr).

Because
\phi_{n}^{\dagger}/\psi_{n}^{\dagger}
scales similarly to
\phi_{n}/\psi_{n}
in Theorem
1
, following a similar procedure in establishing (
C.13
) in Section
C.2
, one can obtain

dist_{\infty}(\bm{Z}^{t+1},\widetilde{\bm{Z}})\leq\rho^{t+1}\psi_{n}^{\dagger}\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}.

(C.45)

Equations (
C.43
) and (
C.45
) establish the induction (
C.41
) for all
t\geq 0
.

Finally, since
\widetilde{\bm{Z}}=\widehat{\bm{Z}}\widehat{\bm{R}}
with probability at least
1-\delta
by Step 2, we have
dist_{2}(\bm{Z}^{t},\widehat{\bm{Z}}\widehat{\bm{R}})=dist_{2}(\bm{Z}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{Z}})
and
dist_{\infty}(\bm{Z}^{t},\widehat{\bm{Z}}\widehat{\bm{R}})=dist_{\infty}(\bm{Z}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{Z}})
.
Using (
C.41
), (
C.39
),
\rho^{t}\phi_{n}^{\dagger}\leq\rho^{t}\phi_{n}+C\tfrac{\Delta_{2}(n,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}
, and
\rho^{t}\psi_{n}^{\dagger}\leq\rho^{t}\psi_{n}+C\tfrac{\Delta_{\infty}(n,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}
,
we conclude that

dist_{2}(\bm{Z}^{t},\bm{Z}^{*})\leq\rho^{t}\phi_{n}\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+C\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}},

and

dist_{\infty}(\bm{Z}^{t},\bm{Z}^{*})\leq\rho^{t}\psi_{n}\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}+C\frac{\Delta_{\infty}(n,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}.

This combines the Step 3 contraction with the intermediate optimizer bound (
C.14
), and completes the proof of Theorem
3
.

C.4
Proof sketch for the
\ell_{2}
part of Theorems
1
and
3

We outline the common
\ell_{2}
argument under the larger local region
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{z}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
. Still, we take
\epsilon=c_{0}\alpha/(\kappa\sqrt{r})
for a sufficiently small constant
c_{0}
such that the initialization requirement (
8
) still holds. In the noisy case, we work on the event where (
17a
) holds with
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{z}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
, and assume Assumptions
1
and
2
hold with
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{z}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
and
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\cdot)
in place of
\mathcal{G}(\cdot)
. The noiseless case is recovered by setting
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\equiv 0
. Set

\varepsilon:=M\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}},\qquad\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(2)}(\varepsilon):=\big\{\bm{Z}:\|\bm{Z}-\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\varepsilon\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\big\},

where
M>0
is a sufficiently large universal constant. By the smallness condition on
\Delta_{2}(n,\delta)/(\alpha\sigma_{\min})
, after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary we have
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(2)}(\varepsilon)\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{z}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
. As in Step 1 of the proof of Theorem
3
, let

\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\bm{Z}\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(2)}(\varepsilon)}\|\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},\qquad h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z}):=\mathcal{L}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal})+p_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z}).

Since
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{Z}^{*})={\bm{0}}
,

\|\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z}^{*})\|_{\mathrm{F}}=2\|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{Z}^{*})\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 2n\,\Delta_{2}(n,\delta)\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

Repeating the mean-value expansion in Step 1, but keeping only the Frobenius terms, gives

\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}-\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+C\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}-\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+C\frac{L_{2}\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}n}\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}-\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

The stated smallness conditions allow the last two terms to be absorbed into the left-hand side, so

\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}-\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

With a similar argument in Step 1 in the proof of Theorem
3
, we can show that
h_{\alpha}^{*}(\cdot)
is strongly convex on
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(2)}(\varepsilon)
. Therefore the analogue of Lemma
7
with
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{F}}
in place of
\|\cdot\|_{2\to\infty}
yields
\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}})={\bm{0}}
, and
\widetilde{\bm{Z}}
is the unique minimizer of
h_{\alpha}^{*}(\cdot)
on
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(2)}(\varepsilon)
. The identification argument from Step 2 of Theorem
3
then yields
p_{\alpha}^{*}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}})=0
,
\nabla_{\bm{Z}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\widetilde{\bm{Z}}^{\intercal})={\bm{0}}
, and
\widetilde{\bm{Z}}=\widehat{\bm{Z}}\widehat{\bm{R}}
. This proves the optimizer bound.

For the iterates, only the
\ell_{2}
part of Step 3 in the proof of Theorem
3
is needed. Define

\bm{R}_{t}^{\dagger}:=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\bm{R}\in\mathcal{O}^{r}}\|\bm{Z}^{t}\bm{R}-\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{\mathrm{F}},\qquad\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}:=\bm{Z}^{t}\bm{R}_{t}^{\dagger}-\widetilde{\bm{Z}}.

Repeating the
\ell_{2}
-contraction calculation in Step 3 gives

dist_{2}(\bm{Z}^{t+1},\widetilde{\bm{Z}})\leq\widetilde{\gamma}_{1,t}^{\dagger}+\widetilde{\gamma}_{2,t}^{\dagger}+\widetilde{\gamma}_{3,t}^{\dagger},

where the same bounds as in Theorem
3
yield

\widetilde{\gamma}_{1,t}^{\dagger}\leq\Bigl(1-\frac{7}{8}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Bigr)\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}},\qquad\widetilde{\gamma}_{2,t}^{\dagger}\leq\frac{1}{8}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}},\qquad\widetilde{\gamma}_{3,t}^{\dagger}\leq\frac{1}{4}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

Therefore,

dist_{2}(\bm{Z}^{t+1},\widetilde{\bm{Z}})\leq\rho\,dist_{2}(\bm{Z}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{Z}}),\qquad\rho=1-\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}/4.

Combining this with the initialization bound and the estimate for
\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}-\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
gives the stated
\ell_{2}
contraction toward
\bm{Z}^{*}
.

Appendix D
Proof of Results under Asymmetric Model

This section proves the main deterministic and noisy results for the asymmetric model, namely Theorems
2
and
4
. The organization parallels that of Section
C
. Specifically, Section
D.1
provides the notation and technical lemmas used throughout this section. Sections
D.2
and
D.3
establish both
\ell_{2}
and
\ell_{\infty}
error contractions under the stronger localization
\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
in the deterministic and noisy settings, respectively. Section
D.4
then proves the
\ell_{2}
error contraction under localization
\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
.

The proofs follow the same broad strategy as in the symmetric model, outlined at the beginning of Section
C
, but require additional control of the non-orthogonal alignment
\bm{G}_{t}^{*}
. Specifically, for the
noiseless
case, we first use the asymmetric benign regularizer to construct the aligned update, which mirrors one on a locally strongly convex objective, up to the perturbation caused by
\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*}-\bm{I}_{r}
, as in (
12
). We then use this property to establish the
\ell_{2}
and
\ell_{\infty}
error bounds for
\bm{U}^{t+1}\bm{G}_{t}^{*}-\bm{U}^{*}
and
\bm{V}^{t+1}(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}^{*}
. The third step differs from the symmetric case. Instead of quantifying the one-step alignment drift
\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*}-\bm{G}_{t}^{*}
, we must control
\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*}-\bm{R}^{0}
, which is necessary to keep
\bm{\Lambda}_{t+1}^{*}-\bm{I}_{r}=(\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*}-\bm{I}_{r}
small along the trajectory. We obtain it through a telescoping argument that sums the increments
\bm{G}_{s+1}^{*}-\bm{G}_{s}^{*}
over
s=0,1,\dots,t
to obtain this.

For the
noisy
setting, again we study the empirical target
(\widehat{\bm{U}},\widehat{\bm{V}})
. Following the strategy of the noisy proof in Section
C
, we establish the first-order condition for
(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{G}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{G}}^{-{\intercal}})
where

\widehat{\bm{G}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\bm{G}\in GL(r)}n^{-1}\|\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{G}}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+q^{-1}\|\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{G}}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

This makes
(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{G}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{G}}^{-{\intercal}})
a valid contraction target for the algorithm. We then re-center the analysis at this empirical target and repeat the deterministic contraction argument, with the alignment now taken relative to
(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{G}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{G}}^{-{\intercal}})
. The use of
(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{G}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{G}}^{-{\intercal}})
as the contraction target is again the key technical tool in the proof, as it ensures that, when telescoping the alignment drift, the non-decaying statistical error is absorbed into the empirical target rather than accumulated along the trajectory.

D.1
Preliminaries

We use
\{\bm{e}_{i}^{(n)}\}
to denote the canonical basis in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and we omit the superscript
n
when the dimension is clear from the context. For a vector
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and a subset
{\mathcal{S}}\subseteq[n]
, write
\bm{x}_{{\mathcal{S}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{|{\mathcal{S}}|}
for the sub-vector indexed by
{\mathcal{S}}
. For a matrix
\bm{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}
and index sets
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\subseteq[n]
,
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}\subseteq[m]
, write
\bm{M}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{S}}_{2}}\in\mathbb{R}^{|{\mathcal{S}}_{1}|\times|{\mathcal{S}}_{2}|}
for the corresponding submatrix. In particular,
\bm{M}_{i,\cdot}
and
\bm{M}_{\cdot,j}
denote the
i
th row and
j
th column, while
\bm{M}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1},}
and
\bm{M}_{,{\mathcal{S}}_{2}}
denote row- and column-restricted submatrices.
Write
\bm{z}=\mathrm{vec}(\bm{Z}^{\intercal})
, so that
\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}
denotes the Hessian with respect to the vectorized variable; later,
\nabla_{\bm{z}_{i}\bm{z}_{j}}^{2}
denotes its
(i,j)
block.

Let
\bm{M}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=n^{-1}(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}-q^{-1}\bm{V}^{\intercal}(\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})
. We use the same notation for the Fréchet derivative as in Section
C
. Also, throughout the proof, all constants
C,c>0
are universal and may change from line to line. We typically use
c_{0}
as a constant that can be sufficiently small. Let
\rho=1-\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}/4
.

Define the weighted Frobenius and
2\to\infty
norms by
\|(\bm{A},\bm{B})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}:=\big(n^{-1}\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+q^{-1}\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big)^{1/2}
and
\|(\bm{A},\bm{B})\|_{2,\infty}:=\max\big\{\|\bm{A}\|_{2\to\infty},\|\bm{B}\|_{2\to\infty}\big\}
.
Throughout the asymmetric proofs, we slightly abuse the notation to denote

\displaystyle\bm{Z}:=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{U}\\
\bm{V}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\bm{S}_{Z}:=\begin{pmatrix}q^{-1/2}\bm{I}_{n}&{\bm{0}}\\
{\bm{0}}&n^{-1/2}\bm{I}_{q}\end{pmatrix},\qquad

\displaystyle\bm{z}:=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{vec}(\bm{U}^{\intercal})\\
\mathrm{vec}(\bm{V}^{\intercal})\end{pmatrix},\qquad\bm{S}_{z}:=\begin{pmatrix}q^{-1/2}\bm{I}_{nr}&{\bm{0}}\\
{\bm{0}}&n^{-1/2}\bm{I}_{qr}\end{pmatrix}.

We also use the rectangular penalized objective
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V}):=\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})+p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
. Recall that we have defined

\tau_{*}^{2}:=\frac{n^{-1}\|\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+q^{-1}\|\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{2},\qquad\omega_{*}:=\|\bm{U}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\vee\|\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}.

For the block score, define the extended score matrix

\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U},\bm{V}):=\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{0}}&\mathcal{G}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\otimes\bm{I}_{r}\\
\mathcal{G}(\bm{U},\bm{V})^{\intercal}\otimes\bm{I}_{r}&{\bm{0}}\end{pmatrix}.

We will repeatedly use the simple consequences of the current theorem scaling,
\tau_{*}^{2}\leq r\kappa\sigma_{\min}
,
\beta\geq\alpha
, and
\kappa\geq 1
:

\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}\leq c_{0}\alpha\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}/\kappa}.

We introduce the following technical lemmas. The first lemma is the asymmetric analogue of Lemma
2
.

Lemma 8
.

Under Assumption
3
,
we have

\displaystyle\min_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}}\lambda_{\min}\!\Big[\bm{S}_{z}\big\{\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})-

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big\}\bm{S}_{z}\Big]\geq{\alpha}\sigma_{\min}

(D.1)

\displaystyle-\max_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}}\alpha\Big(\frac{\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{n}+\frac{\|\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{q}\Big),

where
\sigma_{\min}=\sigma_{r}(\bm{\Sigma}^{*})
.
Moreover, for
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})+p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
with
p_{\alpha}^{\natural}={\alpha}nq\|\bm{M}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}/4
, we have

\min_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}}\lambda_{\min}\!\Big[\bm{S}_{z}\big\{\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})-\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big\}\bm{S}_{z}\Big]\geq(1-c_{0}){\alpha\sigma_{\min}},

given that
\mathcal{D}\subseteq\Big\{(\bm{U},\bm{V}):{n^{-1}\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}+q^{-1}{\|\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\leq c_{0}\sigma_{\min}/\kappa\Big\}
with sufficiently small
c_{0}>0
.

Proof.

See Section
F.3
.
∎

The next lemma is the asymmetric analogue of Lemma
3
.

Lemma 9
.

Under Assumption
3
, for every
(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}
and every
i\in[n]
,

\alpha\,\lambda_{\min}(\bm{V}^{\intercal}\bm{V})\leq\lambda_{\min}\!\big\{\nabla^{2}_{\bm{u}_{i}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})\big\}\leq\lambda_{\max}\!\big\{\nabla^{2}_{\bm{u}_{i}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})\big\}\leq\beta\,\lambda_{\max}(\bm{V}^{\intercal}\bm{V}),

and for every
\ell\in[q]
,

\alpha\,\lambda_{\min}(\bm{U}^{\intercal}\bm{U})\leq\lambda_{\min}\!\big\{\nabla^{2}_{\bm{v}_{\ell}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})\big\}\leq\lambda_{\max}\!\big\{\nabla^{2}_{\bm{v}_{\ell}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})\big\}\leq\beta\,\lambda_{\max}(\bm{U}^{\intercal}\bm{U}).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\min_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}}\min_{i\in[n]}\lambda_{\min}\!\big\{\nabla^{2}_{\bm{u}_{i}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})\big\}

\displaystyle\geq\alpha q\sigma_{r}(q^{-1/2}\bm{V}^{*})-\alpha\max_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}}\big\|\bm{V}^{\intercal}\bm{V}-(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{V}^{*}\big\|,

\displaystyle\max_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}}\max_{i\in[n]}\lambda_{\max}\!\big\{\nabla^{2}_{\bm{u}_{i}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\beta q\kappa\sigma_{r}(q^{-1/2}\bm{V}^{*})+\beta\max_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}}\big\|\bm{V}^{\intercal}\bm{V}-(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{V}^{*}\big\|,

\displaystyle\min_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}}\min_{\ell\in[q]}\lambda_{\min}\!\big\{\nabla^{2}_{\bm{v}_{\ell}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})\big\}

\displaystyle\geq\alpha n\sigma_{r}(n^{-1/2}\bm{U}^{*})-\alpha\max_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}}\big\|\bm{U}^{\intercal}\bm{U}-(\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\big\|,

\displaystyle\max_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}}\max_{\ell\in[q]}\lambda_{\max}\!\big\{\nabla^{2}_{\bm{v}_{\ell}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\beta n\kappa\sigma_{r}(n^{-1/2}\bm{U}^{*})+\beta\max_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}}\big\|\bm{U}^{\intercal}\bm{U}-(\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\big\|.

Proof.

The proof follows from the same argument as in Lemma
8
by restricting the perturbation to the form
\bm{W}=\bm{e}_{i}\bm{a}^{\intercal}
for
\bm{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
. We omit the routine details. Different from Lemma
3
,
\mathcal{G}
is not involved here as it appears only in the off-diagonal parts in
\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
.∎

Unlike the symmetric case, the alignment in the asymmetric model is taken over
GL(r)
rather than
\mathcal{O}^{r}
. The next lemma records the first-order optimality condition for the alignment.

Lemma 10
.

For any
\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
and
\bm{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times r}
, define

\Phi(\bm{G}):=\|(\bm{U}\bm{G}-\bm{U}^{*},\,\bm{V}\bm{G}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2},\qquad\bm{G}\in GL(r).

If
\bm{I}_{r}
is a local minimizer of
\Phi(\cdot)
, then
\bm{M}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=0
. Equivalently,

p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\frac{\alpha nq}{4}\big\|n^{-1}(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}-q^{-1}\bm{V}^{\intercal}(\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=0.

(D.2)

Proof.

See Section
F.4
.∎

The following lemma shows that if an iterate is already close to the truth after a well-conditioned linear transform, then the optimal alignment exists and remains close to the reference rotation. This result will be repeatedly invoked to justify the existence of
\bm{G}_{t}^{*}
and to transfer estimates for the one-step aligned iterate to the optimally aligned iterate.

Lemma 11
.

Fix
\bm{Z}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{U}\\
\bm{V}\end{array}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{(n+q)\times r}
. Suppose that there exists a matrix
\bm{P}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}

with
2/3\leq\sigma_{r}(\bm{P})\leq\sigma_{1}(\bm{P})\leq 3/2
such that

\max\big\{n^{-1/2}\|\bm{U}\bm{P}-\bm{U}^{\ast}\|_{\mathrm{F}},q^{-1/2}\|\bm{V}\bm{P}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}^{\ast}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\big\}\leq\delta\leq\frac{1}{80}\big(\sigma_{r}(n^{-1/2}\bm{U}^{\ast})\wedge\sigma_{r}(q^{-1/2}\bm{V}^{\ast})\big).

(D.3)

Then the optimal alignment matrix
\bm{Q}^{\ast}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
between
\bm{Z}
and
\bm{Z}_{\ast}
exists. In addition, the matrix
\bm{Q}^{\ast}
satisfies

\|\bm{P}-\bm{Q}^{\ast}\|\leq\|\bm{P}-\bm{Q}^{\ast}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{5\delta}{\sigma_{r}(\bm{U}^{*}/\sqrt{n})},\;\|\bm{P}^{-{\intercal}}-{\bm{Q}^{\ast}}^{-{\intercal}}\|\leq\|\bm{P}^{-{\intercal}}-{\bm{Q}^{\ast}}^{-{\intercal}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{5\delta}{\sigma_{r}(\bm{V}^{*}/\sqrt{q})}.

Proof.

See Section
F.5
.
∎

D.2
Proof of Theorem
2

We prove in this subsection the stronger contraction statement under the localization
\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
. Accordingly, throughout the proof we invoke Assumptions
3
,
4
,
B.7
, and
B.8
with
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
. The
\ell_{2}
-only part of Theorem
2
, where only Assumptions
3
–
4
are imposed with
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
, is proved later in Section
D.4
by the same argument after removing the row-wise estimates.
Throughout this proof,
\eta^{t}\equiv\eta=\{10(\alpha+\beta)\kappa\sigma_{\min}\}^{-1}
. Besides the error bounds, we also track the existence of the optimal invertible alignment and its proximity to the reference orthogonal matrix
\bm{R}^{0}
. In particular, for each
t\geq 0
, define the optimal invertible alignment,

\bm{G}_{t}^{*}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\bm{G}\in GL(r)}\|(\bm{U}^{t}\bm{G}-\bm{U}^{*},\,\bm{V}^{t}\bm{G}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}},\qquad\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*}:=(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{G}_{t}^{*},

whenever the minimum is attained. Let

\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}:=\bm{U}^{t}\bm{G}_{t}^{*},\qquad\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}:=\bm{V}^{t}(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}.

We also write

\bm{E}_{U}^{t}:=\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}-\bm{U}^{*},\qquad\bm{E}_{V}^{t}:=\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\bm{V}^{*},\qquad\bm{e}_{t}:=\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{vec}\big((\bm{E}_{U}^{t})^{\intercal}\big)\\
\mathrm{vec}\big((\bm{E}_{V}^{t})^{\intercal}\big)\end{bmatrix}.

Then, one can easily see

\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}=(nq)^{-1/2}\|\bm{S}_{z}^{-1}\bm{e}_{t}\|,\qquad\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}=\|\bm{E}_{U}^{t}\|_{2\to\infty}\vee\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{2\to\infty}.

Let
\iota_{0}\in(0,1/30)
be a sufficiently small constant. We prove by induction on
t
that, for all
0\leq s\leq t
, the following hold:

(1)

the optimal alignment matrix
\bm{G}_{s}^{*}
exists;

(2)

\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{s},\bm{E}_{V}^{s})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho^{s}\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}
;

(3)

\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{s},\bm{E}_{V}^{s})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\rho^{s}\psi_{nq}\omega_{*}
;

(4)

\|\bm{G}_{s}^{*}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{s}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\leq\iota_{0}{\alpha}/(\beta{\kappa})
.

Here
\bm{R}^{0}\in\mathcal{O}^{r}
is the orthogonal matrix in (
13
).
Since
\bm{R}^{0}\in\mathcal{O}^{r}
, we may equivalently replace
(\bm{U},\bm{V})
by
(\bm{U}\bm{R}^{0},\bm{V}(\bm{R}^{0})^{-{\intercal}})=(\bm{U}\bm{R}^{0},\bm{V}\bm{R}^{0})
. Thus one may work with
\bm{R}^{0}=\bm{I}_{r}
throughout. We keep
\bm{R}^{0}
only to make the alignment argument transparent.

Similar to the proof in the symmetric model, define the interpolation segment parameterized by
s\in[0,1]
as

\bm{U}_{t}(s):=\bm{U}^{*}+s\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\qquad\bm{V}_{t}(s):=\bm{V}^{*}+s\bm{E}_{V}^{t}.

By the induction hypotheses,

\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\phi_{nq}\tau_{*},\qquad\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\psi_{nq}\omega_{*}.

Hence, one can check that, for every
s\in[0,1]
,

dist_{2}\{(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s)),(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\}\leq s\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\phi_{nq}\tau_{*},

and similarly

dist_{\infty}\{(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s)),(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\}\leq s\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\psi_{nq}\omega_{*}.

Since the current theorem assumes
\phi_{nq}\leq\epsilon/2
and
\psi_{nq}\leq\epsilon/2
, the whole segment
\{(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s)):0\leq s\leq 1\}
lies in
\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
. Therefore every assumption invoked below is available uniformly along this path.

Next, recall that in the asymmetric model, we can equivalently formulate the nonconvex procedure approximately into a strongly convex one within the local region, after imposing the optimal alignment
\bm{G}_{t}^{*}
. Specifically, define the one-step iterate aligned by
\bm{G}_{t}^{*}
:

\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}:=\bm{U}^{t+1}\bm{G}_{t}^{*},\qquad\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}:=\bm{V}^{t+1}(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}.

Since
(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})
optimally aligns with
(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})
provided that
\bm{G}_{t}^{*}
exists according to the induction hypothesis
(1)
, Lemma
10
implies

n^{-1}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}-\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}=q^{-1}\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t{\intercal}}(\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\bm{V}^{*}),

and hence we know
\nabla_{\bm{U}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})={\bm{0}}
and
\nabla_{\bm{V}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})={\bm{0}}
.
Therefore the gradient update can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}-\frac{\eta}{q}\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})-\frac{\eta}{q}\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*}-\bm{I}_{r}),

(D.4)

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\frac{\eta}{n}\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})-\frac{\eta}{n}\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\big\{(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big\}.

(D.5)

Now we proceed to prove the induction hypotheses
(1)
–
(4)
, starting from
t=0
.

Initialization.

We first verify the auxiliary existence and rotation statements at
t=0
, and then deduce the error bounds. By (
13
),

\max\Big\{n^{-1/2}\|\bm{U}^{0}\bm{R}^{0}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}},\,q^{-1/2}\|\bm{V}^{0}(\bm{R}^{0})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\Big\}\leq\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}.

Since
\tau_{*}^{2}=(n^{-1}\|\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+q^{-1}\|\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2})/2\leq r\kappa\sigma_{\min}
, we have by the scaling condition
\phi_{nq}\leq c_{0}\alpha/(\beta^{2}\kappa\sqrt{r\kappa})
that

\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}\leq c_{0}\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{2}\kappa\sqrt{r\kappa}}\sqrt{r\kappa\sigma_{\min}}=c_{0}\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{2}\kappa}\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}.

Applying Lemma
11
with
\bm{P}=\bm{R}^{0}
, we see that
\bm{G}_{0}^{*}
exists and

\displaystyle\|\bm{G}_{0}^{*}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{0}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|

\displaystyle\leq 5\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}\Big(\frac{\sigma_{r}(\bm{U}^{*})}{\sqrt{n}}\wedge\frac{\sigma_{r}(\bm{V}^{*})}{\sqrt{q}}\Big)^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq 5c_{0}\frac{\alpha\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}{\beta{\kappa^{2}}\big(n^{-1/2}\sigma_{r}(\bm{U}^{*})\wedge q^{-1/2}\sigma_{r}(\bm{V}^{*})\big)}

\displaystyle\leq\iota_{0}\frac{\alpha}{\beta{\kappa}},

with
\iota_{0}<10^{-1}
after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary. This immediately proves
(1)
and
(4)
at
t=0
.
Since
\bm{G}_{0}^{*}
minimizes the weighted Frobenius distance, the initialization bound immediately yields the weighted
\ell_{2}
statement (
(2)
) at
t=0
. Under the additional row-wise initialization bound (
15
), bound for
\|\bm{G}_{0}^{*}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{0}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|
above gives

\|\bm{U}^{0}\bm{G}_{0}^{*}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|\bm{U}^{0}\bm{R}^{0}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|\bm{U}^{0}\|_{2\to\infty}\|\bm{G}_{0}^{*}-\bm{R}^{0}\|,

and similarly for the
V
-block. This yields (
(3)
) at
t=0
.
Now assume
(1)
–
(4)
hold for all
0\leq s\leq t
. We prove them for
t+1
.

Step 1: weighted
\ell_{2}
error contraction.

Let

\bar{\bm{e}}_{t+1}:=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{vec}\big((\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\big)\\
\mathrm{vec}\big((\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\big)\end{pmatrix},\qquad\bm{w}_{t}:=\bm{S}_{z}^{-1}\bm{e}_{t},\qquad\bar{\bm{w}}_{t+1}:=\bm{S}_{z}^{-1}\bar{\bm{e}}_{t+1},

and

\vartheta_{t}:=\|\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\vee\|(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\|.

Since
\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})=\nabla_{\bm{X}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{X}^{*})={\bm{0}}
in the noiseless case and

\nabla_{\bm{U}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})=\nabla_{\bm{V}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})=\nabla_{\bm{U}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})=\nabla_{\bm{V}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}},

by Lemma
10
, we have
\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}},
and

\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}.

Therefore, (
D.4
)–(
D.5
) lead to

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{w}}_{t+1}=

\displaystyle\,\bm{w}_{t}-\eta\bm{S}_{z}\mathrm{vec}\Big\{\big(\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})-\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\big)^{\intercal}\Big\}

\displaystyle\,-\eta\mathcal{R}_{t}\bm{S}_{z}\mathrm{vec}\Big\{\big(\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})-\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\big)^{\intercal}\Big\},

where we let

\mathcal{R}_{t}:=\begin{pmatrix}(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*}-\bm{I}_{r})\otimes\bm{I}_{n}&{\bm{0}}\\
{\bm{0}}&\big((\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big)\otimes\bm{I}_{q}\end{pmatrix}

By the fundamental theorem of calculus (Theorem 4.2 in
28
, Chapter XIII), we have the expansion

\mathrm{vec}\Big\{\big(\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})-\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\big)^{\intercal}\Big\}=\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s))\,ds\Big)\bm{e}_{t}.

Similar to the decomposition in the proof of Theorem
1
, define

\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}:=\bm{S}_{z}\int_{0}^{1}\big\{\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s))-\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s))\big\}\,ds\,\bm{S}_{z},

and

\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}:=\bm{S}_{z}\int_{0}^{1}\big\{\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s))-\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\big\}\,ds\,\bm{S}_{z}.

Since
\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
, we have
\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
, which therefore gives

\bm{S}_{z}\mathrm{vec}\Big\{\big(\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})-\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\big)^{\intercal}\Big\}=(\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}+\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t})\bm{w}_{t}.

Hence, the expansion becomes

\bar{\bm{w}}_{t+1}=(\bm{I}_{nr+qr}-\eta\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t})\bm{w}_{t}-\eta\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}\bm{w}_{t}-\eta\mathcal{R}_{t}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}+\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t})\bm{w}_{t}.

We next decompose the right-hand side into

\gamma_{1,t}:=\|(\bm{I}_{nr+qr}-\eta\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t})\bm{w}_{t}\|,\qquad\gamma_{2,t}:=\eta\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}\bm{w}_{t}\|,

\gamma_{3,t}:=\eta\|\mathcal{R}_{t}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}+\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t})\bm{w}_{t}\|\leq\eta\vartheta_{t}\big(\|\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}\bm{w}_{t}\|+\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}\bm{w}_{t}\|\big),

so that
\|\bar{\bm{w}}_{t+1}\|\leq\gamma_{1,t}+\gamma_{2,t}+\gamma_{3,t}
.
Here,
\gamma_{1,t}
–
\gamma_{3,t}
play roles analogous to those of the quantities denoted by the same notation in Step 1 of the proof of Theorem
1
in Section
C.2
.

We first bound
\gamma_{1,t}
. Note that we have shown that the interpolation segment lies in
\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
, so Lemma
8
applies with
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
. In addition, note that

n^{-1}\|\bm{E}_{U}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+q^{-1}\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\phi_{nq}^{2}\tau_{*}^{2}.

With the theorem’s scaling condition
\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}\leq c_{0}\alpha\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}/\kappa}
, we know this quantity is at most
c_{0}\sigma_{\min}/\kappa
after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary. Substituting this localization bound into Lemma
8
yields, after shrinking
c_{0}
,
\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t})\geq 7{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}/{8}
.
To control the maximal eigenvalue, we note that, with Weyl’s inequality, we know that

\frac{\|\bm{U}_{t}(s)\|^{2}}{n}\vee\frac{\|\bm{V}_{t}(s)\|^{2}}{q}\leq\frac{\|\bm{U}_{t}(s)\|^{2}+\|\bm{E}_{U}^{t}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}}{n}\vee\frac{\|\bm{V}_{t}(s)\|^{2}+\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}}{q}\leq 1.01\kappa\sigma_{\min},

The same argument used in proving Lemma
8
yields
\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t})\leq 1.01(\alpha+\beta)\kappa\sigma_{\min}
.
Since
\eta\leq\{10(\alpha+\beta)\kappa\sigma_{\min}\}^{-1}
, all eigenvalues of

\bm{I}_{nr+qr}-\eta\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}
lie in
[0,1)
, and we get

\gamma_{1,t}\leq\Big(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t})\Big)\|\bm{w}_{t}\|\leq\Big(1-\frac{7}{8}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Big)\|\bm{w}_{t}\|.

Next, we bound
\gamma_{2,t}
.
By the definition of
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}
,

\displaystyle\gamma_{2,t}\leq\;

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{nq}}\Big\|\int_{0}^{1}\big\{\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s))-\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\big\}\,ds\times\sqrt{n}\,\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\;+\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{nq}}\Big\|\int_{0}^{1}\big\{\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s))-\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\big\}^{\intercal}\,ds\times\sqrt{q}\,\bm{E}_{U}^{t}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

By Assumption
4
, we know

\|\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s))-\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\|\leq L_{2}\|\bm{U}_{t}(s)\bm{V}_{t}(s)^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

Also, by telescoping and
\|\cdot\|\leq\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{F}}
, one has

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}_{t}(s)\bm{V}_{t}(s)^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{E}_{U}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{V}^{*}\|+\|\bm{U}^{*}\|\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|\bm{E}_{U}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{nq}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}},

where in the last step we used
q^{-1}\|\bm{V}^{*}\|^{2}+n^{-1}\|\bm{U}^{*}\|^{2}\leq 2\kappa\sigma_{\min}
and

\frac{\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\sqrt{q}\,\|\bm{E}_{U}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sqrt{nq}}\leq C\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

We therefore conclude that

\gamma_{2,t}\leq C\eta\sqrt{nq}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

From the scaling condition, we know
\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}\leq c_{0}\alpha\sigma_{\min}/\beta
, Then by the induction hypothesis
(2)
, we know
\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}\leq c_{0}\alpha\sqrt{{\sigma_{\min}}/{\kappa}}
,
which in turn gives
\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq c_{0}\alpha\sigma_{\min}
. After shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary, we arrive at

\gamma_{2,t}\leq\frac{1}{16}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|\bm{w}_{t}\|.

For
\gamma_{3,t}
, we now substitute the operator bound on
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}
and the estimate for
\gamma_{2,t}
into the alignment-imbalance term, which gives

\gamma_{3,t}\leq\eta\vartheta_{t}\|\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}\bm{w}_{t}\|+\eta\vartheta_{t}\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}\bm{w}_{t}\|\leq\eta\vartheta_{t}\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t})\|\bm{w}_{t}\|+\vartheta_{t}\gamma_{2,t}.

One then has
\gamma_{3,t}\leq 1.01\vartheta_{t}(\alpha+\beta)\kappa\sigma_{\min}\eta\|\bm{w}_{t}\|+\vartheta_{t}\gamma_{2,t}
.
Next, by telescoping and
(\bm{R}^{0})^{\intercal}\bm{R}^{0}=\bm{R}^{0}(\bm{R}^{0})^{\intercal}=\bm{I}_{r}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*}-\bm{I}_{r}\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\bm{G}_{t}^{*}-\bm{R}^{0}\|+\|\bm{G}_{t}^{*}-\bm{R}^{0}\|^{2},

\displaystyle\|(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|+\|(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|^{2}.

Therefore, with the induction hypothesis
(4)
at
t
, we arrive at

\vartheta_{t}=\|\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\vee\|(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\leq\frac{\alpha}{10\beta\kappa},

(D.6)

after shrinking
\iota_{0}
if necessary. Therefore,

\gamma_{3,t}\leq\frac{5}{8}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|\bm{w}_{t}\|.

Combining the three estimates gives
\|\bar{\bm{w}}_{t+1}\|\leq\big(1-\frac{1}{4}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\big)\|\bm{w}_{t}\|=\rho\|\bm{w}_{t}\|
. Dividing by
\sqrt{nq}
, we obtain
\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\,\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
.
By the induction hypothesis, we finally arrive at

\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\,\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}.

(D.7)

Step 2: weighted
\ell_{\infty}
error contraction.

We now bound
\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}
. Recall that
\bm{U}_{t}(s):=\bm{U}^{*}+s\bm{E}_{U}^{t}
, and
\bm{V}_{t}(s):=\bm{V}^{*}+s\bm{E}_{V}^{t}
for
s\in[0,1]
. Since the penalty gradients vanish at both
(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})
and
(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})
, the gradient difference in the row-wise mean-value expansion is the same as that for
\mathcal{L}(\cdot)
. Hence, only the Hessian blocks of
\mathcal{L}(\cdot)
appear below.

To obtain a row-wise recursion, we now rewrite the mean-value expansion block by block. Specifically, for
i\in[n]
and
\ell\in[q]
, define

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ij,t}^{UU}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{q}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\bm{u}_{i}\bm{u}_{j}}^{2}\mathcal{L}\big(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s)\big)\,ds,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{i\ell,t}^{UV}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{q}\int_{0}^{1}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{u}_{i}\bm{v}_{\ell}}^{2}\mathcal{L}\big(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s)\big)-G_{i\ell}\big(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s)\big)\bm{I}_{r}\Big\}\,ds,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell i,t}^{VU}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{1}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{v}_{\ell}\bm{u}_{i}}^{2}\mathcal{L}\big(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s)\big)-G_{i\ell}\big(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s)\big)\bm{I}_{r}\Big\}\,ds,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell k,t}^{VV}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\bm{v}_{\ell}\bm{v}_{k}}^{2}\mathcal{L}\big(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s)\big)\,ds,

and

\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}:=\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{G}\big(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s)\big)\,ds=\int_{0}^{1}\Big\{\mathcal{G}\big(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s)\big)-\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\Big\}\,ds,

where the second equality uses
\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
. Here,
\bar{G}_{i\ell,t}
denotes the
(i,\ell)
entry of
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}
.

By (
D.4
)–(
D.5
), for each
i\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*})_{i,\cdot}=

\displaystyle\Big(\bm{I}_{r}-\eta\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ii,t}^{UU}\Big)(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{i,\cdot}-\eta\sum_{j\neq i}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ij,t}^{UU}(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{j,\cdot}-\eta\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{i\ell,t}^{UV}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{\ell,\cdot}

(D.8)

\displaystyle-\frac{\eta}{q}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\bar{G}_{i\ell,t}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{\ell,\cdot}-\frac{\eta}{q}\Big[\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*}-\bm{I}_{r})\Big]_{i,\cdot},

and for each
\ell\in[q]
,

\displaystyle(\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})_{\ell,\cdot}=

\displaystyle\Big(\bm{I}_{r}-\eta\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell\ell,t}^{VV}\Big)(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{\ell,\cdot}-\eta\sum_{k\neq\ell}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell k,t}^{VV}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{k,\cdot}-\eta\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell i,t}^{VU}(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{i,\cdot}

(D.9)

\displaystyle-\frac{\eta}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{G}_{i\ell,t}(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{i,\cdot}-\frac{\eta}{n}\Big[\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\big\{(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big\}\Big]_{\ell,\cdot}.

Accordingly, define

\delta_{m,t}:=\delta_{m,t}^{U}\vee\delta_{m,t}^{V},\text{ for }m=1,2,3,4,

where each
\delta_{m,t}^{U}
and
\delta_{m,t}^{V}
are given as

\displaystyle\delta_{1,t}^{U}

\displaystyle:=\max_{i\in[n]}\Big\|\big(\bm{I}_{r}-\eta\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ii,t}^{UU}\big)(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{i,\cdot}\Big\|,\qquad\quad\delta_{1,t}^{V}:=\max_{\ell\in[q]}\Big\|\big(\bm{I}_{r}-\eta\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell\ell,t}^{VV}\big)(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{\ell,\cdot}\Big\|,

\displaystyle\delta_{2,t}^{U}

\displaystyle:={\eta}\max_{i\in[n]}\Big\|\sum_{j\neq i}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ij,t}^{UU}(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{j,\cdot}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{i\ell,t}^{UV}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{\ell,\cdot}\Big\|,

\displaystyle\delta_{2,t}^{V}

\displaystyle:={\eta}\max_{\ell\in[q]}\Big\|\sum_{k\neq\ell}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell k,t}^{VV}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{k,\cdot}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell i,t}^{VU}(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{i,\cdot}\Big\|,

\displaystyle\delta_{3,t}^{U}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\eta}{q}\max_{i\in[n]}\Big\|\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\bar{G}_{i\ell,t}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{\ell,\cdot}\Big\|,\qquad\qquad\delta_{3,t}^{V}:=\frac{\eta}{n}\max_{\ell\in[q]}\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{G}_{i\ell,t}(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{i,\cdot}\Big\|,

\displaystyle\delta_{4,t}^{U}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\eta}{q}\Big\|\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*}-\bm{I}_{r})\Big\|_{2\to\infty},\quad\delta_{4,t}^{V}:=\frac{\eta}{n}\Big\|\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\big\{(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big\}\Big\|_{2\to\infty}.

Equivalently, (
D.8
) and (
D.9
) are expressed as

\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\delta_{1,t}+\delta_{2,t}+\delta_{3,t}+\delta_{4,t}.

(D.10)

We next bound the four terms.

For
\delta_{1,t}
, similar to the proof of Lemma
9
, one can check that, for every
s\in[0,1]
, we know

\lambda_{\min}\big(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ii,t}^{UU}\big)\geq\alpha q^{-1}\lambda_{\min}\big\{\bm{V}_{t}(s)^{\intercal}\bm{V}_{t}(s)\big\},\qquad\lambda_{\max}\big(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ii,t}^{UU}\big)\leq\beta q^{-1}\lambda_{\max}\big\{\bm{V}_{t}(s)^{\intercal}\bm{V}_{t}(s)\big\},

and similarly

\lambda_{\min}\big(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell\ell,t}^{VV}\big)\geq\alpha n^{-1}\lambda_{\min}\big\{\bm{U}_{t}(s)^{\intercal}\bm{U}_{t}(s)\big\},\qquad\lambda_{\max}\big(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell\ell,t}^{VV}\big)\leq\beta n^{-1}\lambda_{\max}\big\{\bm{U}_{t}(s)^{\intercal}\bm{U}_{t}(s)\big\}.

Next, because
q^{-1/2}\|\bm{V}^{*}\|\leq\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
,
\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}
by the induction hypothesis, and
\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}\leq c_{0}\alpha\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}/\kappa}
by the scaling condition
one has

\displaystyle q^{-1}\big\|\bm{V}_{t}(s)^{\intercal}\bm{V}_{t}(s)-(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{V}^{*}\big\|

\displaystyle\leq 2q^{-1/2}\|\bm{V}^{*}\|\,q^{-1/2}\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+q^{-1}\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}\alpha\sigma_{\min}.

Similarly, one can obtain
n^{-1}\big\|\bm{U}_{t}(s)^{\intercal}\bm{U}_{t}(s)-(\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\big\|\leq Cc_{0}\alpha\sigma_{\min}
.
Recall that we have assumed
q^{-1}\lambda_{\min}\big((\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{V}^{*}\big)=n^{-1}\lambda_{\min}\big((\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\big)=\sigma_{\min}
. Then, after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary, we obtain

\lambda_{\min}\big(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ii,t}^{UU}\big)\wedge\lambda_{\min}\big(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell\ell,t}^{VV}\big)\geq\frac{7}{8}\alpha\sigma_{\min}.

Similarly, with
q^{-1}\|\bm{V}_{t}(s)\|^{2}\leq q^{-1}\|\bm{V}^{*}\|^{2}+q^{-1}\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 1.01\kappa\sigma_{\min}
,
and the same bound holds for
n^{-1}\|\bm{U}_{t}(s)\|^{2}
. Again, following Step 2 in the proof of Theorem
1
, one can obtain

\lambda_{\max}\big(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ii,t}^{UU}\big)\vee\lambda_{\max}\big(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell\ell,t}^{VV}\big)\leq 1.01(\beta+\alpha)\kappa\sigma_{\min},

after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary.
Since
\eta\leq\{10(\alpha+\beta)\kappa\sigma_{\min}\}^{-1}
, all eigenvalues of
\bm{I}_{r}-\eta\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ii,t}^{UU}
and
\bm{I}_{r}-\eta\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell\ell,t}^{VV}
lie in
[0,1)
, and therefore, we arrive at

\delta_{1,t}\leq\Big(1-\frac{7}{8}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Big)\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}.

(D.11)

For
\delta_{2,t}
, we again argue row by row. We first bound
\delta_{2,t}^{U}
.
Fix
i\in[n]
and
\bm{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
with
\|\bm{h}\|=1
, and define

\bm{L}_{i}:=\bm{e}_{i}^{(n)}\bm{h}^{\intercal},\qquad(\bm{L}^{(i)})_{j,\cdot}:=\begin{cases}(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{j,\cdot},&j\neq i,\\
{\bm{0}},&j=i,\end{cases}\qquad\bm{R}:=\bm{E}_{V}^{t}.

By the definitions of
\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ij,t}^{UU}
and
\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{i\ell,t}^{UV}
, we note that

\displaystyle\,\bm{h}^{\intercal}\Big\{\sum_{j\neq i}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ij,t}^{UU}(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{j,\cdot}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{i\ell,t}^{UV}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{\ell,\cdot}\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{q}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\bm{X}}^{2}\mathcal{L}\big(\bm{U}_{t}(s)\bm{V}_{t}(s)^{\intercal}\big)\Big[\mathcal{P}_{(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s))}({\bm{0}},\bm{L}_{i}),\,\mathcal{P}_{(\bm{U}_{t}(s),\bm{V}_{t}(s))}(\bm{R},\bm{L}^{(i)})\Big]\,ds.

Applying Assumption
B.7
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\bm{h}^{\intercal}\Big\{\sum_{j\neq i}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ij,t}^{UU}(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{j,\cdot}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{i\ell,t}^{UV}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{\ell,\cdot}\Big\}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\beta}{q}\int_{0}^{1}\|\bm{V}_{t}(s)\bm{h}\|\,\|(\bm{U}_{t}(s))_{i,\cdot}\bm{R}^{\intercal}\|\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\beta}{q}\int_{0}^{1}\|\bm{V}_{t}(s)\|\,\|\bm{h}\|\,\|(\bm{U}_{t}(s))_{i,\cdot}\|\,\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\beta}{q}\,\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\|\bm{V}_{t}(s)\|\,\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\|(\bm{U}_{t}(s))_{i,\cdot}\|\,\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

By the induction hypothesis and the definition of the interpolation path, one can get

\|\bm{U}_{t}(s)\|_{2\to\infty}\vee\|\bm{V}_{t}(s)\|_{2\to\infty}\leq(1+\epsilon/2)\omega_{*}\leq 2\omega_{*}.

In addition, by Step 1, we already know
n^{-1/2}\|\bm{U}_{t}(s)\|\vee q^{-1/2}\|\bm{V}_{t}(s)\|\leq C\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
. This further yields

\displaystyle\Big|\bm{h}^{\intercal}\Big\{\sum_{j\neq i}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ij,t}^{UU}(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{j,\cdot}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{i\ell,t}^{UV}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{\ell,\cdot}\Big\}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\beta\,\frac{\sqrt{q}\,\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}}{q}\,\omega_{*}\,\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=C\beta\omega_{*}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\frac{\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sqrt{q}}.

Taking the supremum over all
\bm{h}
with
\|\bm{h}\|=1
yields

\Big\|\sum_{j\neq i}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ij,t}^{UU}(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{j,\cdot}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{i\ell,t}^{UV}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{\ell,\cdot}\Big\|\leq C\beta\omega_{*}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\frac{\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sqrt{q}}.

Since
q^{-1/2}\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
by definition, we conclude that

\delta_{2,t}^{U}\leq C\eta\beta\omega_{*}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

Similarly, one can obtain
\delta_{2,t}^{V}\leq C\eta\beta\omega_{*}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
. Combining the bounds for
\delta_{2,t}^{U}
and
\delta_{2,t}^{V}
, we conclude that

\delta_{2,t}=\delta_{2,t}^{U}\vee\delta_{2,t}^{V}\leq C\eta\beta\omega_{*}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

(D.12)

For
\delta_{3,t}
, similar to bounding
\delta_{3,t}
in the proof of Theorem
1
in Section
C.2
, apply Assumption
B.8
to get

\displaystyle\delta_{3,t}^{U}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{q}\max_{i\in[n]}\Big\|\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\bar{G}_{i\ell,t}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{\ell,\cdot}\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\eta q^{-1/2}\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}\|_{2\to\infty}\,q^{-1/2}\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\eta L_{\infty}q^{-1/2}\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|\bm{U}_{t}(s)\bm{V}_{t}(s)^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

Now that

\bm{U}_{t}(s)\bm{V}_{t}(s)^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}=s\,\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})^{\intercal}+s\,\bm{E}_{U}^{t}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}+s^{2}\,\bm{E}_{U}^{t}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})^{\intercal}.

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}_{t}(s)\bm{V}_{t}(s)^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{U}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|\bm{V}^{*}\|\|\bm{E}_{U}^{t}\|_{2,\infty}+\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{E}_{U}^{t}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{q}\,\omega_{*}\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+(\|\bm{V}^{*}\|+\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}})\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{q}\,\omega_{*}\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+C\sqrt{q\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty},

uniformly over
s\in[0,1]
, where we used

\|\bm{E}_{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{q}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq c_{0}\tfrac{\alpha(\alpha+\kappa)}{\beta^{2}\kappa^{2}}\sqrt{q\sigma_{\min}}\leq c_{0}\sqrt{q\sigma_{\min}/\kappa}
for sufficiently small
c_{0}
. Hence, we arrive at

\displaystyle\delta_{3,t}^{U}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\Big(\omega_{*}\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}+C\eta\omega_{*}\alpha\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\kappa}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}},

where the last step uses
\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}\leq c_{0}\alpha\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}/\kappa}
obtained by the induction hypothesis and the scaling condition. By symmetry, the same bound holds for
\delta_{3,t}^{V}
. Therefore, we have

\delta_{3,t}\leq\frac{1}{16}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}+C\eta\omega_{*}\alpha\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\kappa}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

(D.13)

where the last step uses
\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}
from the induction hypothesis together with the scaling condition
\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}\leq c_{0}\alpha\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}/\kappa}
.
Finally, we bound
\delta_{4,t}
.
Since
\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
and
\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
, the same row-wise expansion as above gives

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{q}\|\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\max_{i\in[n]}\|\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ii,t}^{UU}(\bm{E}_{U}^{t})_{i,\cdot}\|+\delta_{2,t}^{U}+\delta_{3,t}^{U},

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{n}\|\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell\ell,t}^{VV}(\bm{E}_{V}^{t})_{\ell,\cdot}\|+\delta_{2,t}^{V}+\delta_{3,t}^{V}.

Using the upper bounds on the diagonal Hessian blocks derived above, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{q}\|\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\|_{2\to\infty}\!\vee\frac{\eta}{n}\|\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\|_{2\to\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\,1.01\eta(\alpha+\beta)\kappa\sigma_{\min}\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\,+\delta_{2,t}+\delta_{3,t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,2.02\eta\beta\kappa\sigma_{\min}\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}+\delta_{2,t}+\delta_{3,t}.

Recall that from Step 1, we have defined
\vartheta_{t}:=\|\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\vee\|(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\|
.
Therefore, by (
D.6
), we know

\vartheta_{t}\leq\frac{\alpha}{10\beta\kappa}.

Consequently,
\delta_{4,t}
can be controlled by

\displaystyle\delta_{4,t}

\displaystyle\leq\vartheta_{t}\Big(\frac{\eta}{q}\|\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\|_{2\to\infty}\;\vee\;\frac{\eta}{n}\|\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\|_{2\to\infty}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}+\vartheta_{t}\delta_{2,t}+\vartheta_{t}\delta_{3,t}.

Next, by the estimate for
\delta_{2,t}
obtained above, we know

\vartheta_{t}\delta_{2,t}\leq C\eta\alpha\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\kappa}}\,\omega_{*}\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}},

and a similar bound holds for
\vartheta_{t}\delta_{3,t}
, while its
\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}
-part is absorbed into the first term. Hence, we arrive at

\delta_{4,t}\leq\frac{1}{4}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}+C\eta\alpha\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\kappa}}\,\omega_{*}\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

(D.14)

Combining (
D.10
), (
D.11
), (
D.12
),
(
D.13
), and (
D.14
), and using
\beta\geq\alpha
and
\kappa\geq 1
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\,\Big(1-\frac{9}{16}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Big)\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\,+C\eta\beta\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\omega_{*}\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

By Step 1 and the induction hypotheses
\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho^{t}\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}
and
\|(\bm{E}_{U}^{t},\bm{E}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}\omega_{*}
, we further have

\displaystyle\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\Big(1-\frac{9}{16}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Big)\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}\omega_{*}+C\eta\beta\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\omega_{*}\,\rho^{t}\phi_{nq}\tau_{*},

where we used
\tau_{*}^{2}\leq r\kappa\sigma_{\min}
.
By the scaling condition that
\tfrac{\beta}{\alpha}\kappa^{3/2}\sqrt{r}\,{\phi_{nq}}/{\psi_{nq}}\leq c_{0}
, after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary, there is
C\beta\kappa\sqrt{r}\,\phi_{nq}\leq\alpha\psi_{nq}/(16)
.
Consequently, with
\rho=1-\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}/4
,

\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\Big(1-\frac{1}{2}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Big)\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}\omega_{*}=(2\rho-1)\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}\omega_{*}.

(D.15)

Step 3: existence of
\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*}
and alignment of
(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1})
with
(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1})
.

Now we prove the induction hypotheses
(1)
and
(4)
for step
t+1
. Then we transfer the one-step bounds for
(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1})
to iterate
(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1})
, which further yields the induction hypotheses
(2)
and
(3)
. For each
t
, set

\delta_{t+1}:=\max\Big\{n^{-1/2}\|\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}},\,q^{-1/2}\|\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\Big\}.

By (
D.7
), we know
\delta_{t+1}\leq\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}\tau_{*}
. Further with
\sigma_{r}(n^{-1/2}\bm{U}^{*})=\sigma_{r}(q^{-1/2}\bm{V}^{*})=\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}
,
\tau_{*}\leq\sqrt{r\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
, and
the scaling condition
\phi_{nq}\leq c_{0}\frac{\alpha(\alpha+\kappa)}{\beta^{2}\kappa^{2}}\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}
, it holds that, after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary,

\delta_{t+1}\leq\frac{1}{80}\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}=\frac{1}{80}\Big(\sigma_{r}(n^{-1/2}\bm{U}^{*})\wedge\sigma_{r}(q^{-1/2}\bm{V}^{*})\Big).

Moreover, the induction hypothesis
(4)
gives
\|\bm{G}_{t}^{*}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\leq\iota_{0}\frac{\alpha}{\beta\kappa}<1/6
, after shrinking
\iota_{0}
if necessary, so all singular values of
\bm{G}_{t}^{*}
lie in
[2/3,3/2]
.
Therefore Lemma
11
, applied to the pair
(\bm{U}^{t+1},\bm{V}^{t+1})
with
\bm{P}=\bm{G}_{t}^{*}
, yields that the optimal alignment matrix
\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*}
exists. In addition,

\displaystyle\|\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*}-\bm{G}_{t}^{*}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5\delta_{t+1}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}

(D.16)

\displaystyle\leq 5\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}\frac{\tau_{*}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}\sqrt{r\kappa},

which can be sufficiently small due to the scaling condition.
This proves
(1)
at time
t+1
.

To verify
(4)
, we telescope the increments. Since the same argument gives
(
D.16
) at every previous iteration, we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{G}_{0}^{*}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{0}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|

(D.17)

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=0}^{t}\Big(\|\bm{G}_{s+1}^{*}-\bm{G}_{s}^{*}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{s+1}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-(\bm{G}_{s}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}\|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\phi_{nq}\frac{\tau_{*}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}\sum_{s=0}^{t+1}\rho^{s}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\phi_{nq}\sqrt{r\kappa}}{1-\rho}.

Hence, to guarantee

\|\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\leq\iota_{0}\frac{\alpha}{\beta\kappa},

it suffices to show that

\phi_{nq}\leq c_{0}(1-\rho)\frac{\alpha}{\beta\kappa\sqrt{r\kappa}}=c_{0}\,\eta\sigma_{\min}\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta\kappa\sqrt{r\kappa}},

(D.18)

for
c_{0}>0
sufficiently small. One can check that this holds under the scaling condition,
\eta=\{10(\alpha+\beta)\kappa\sigma_{\min}\}^{-1}
, and
\beta\geq\alpha
. This proves
(4)
at time
t+1
.

Now we show induction hypotheses
(2)
and
(3)
. First,
(2)
is easy to show. Note that
\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*}
minimizes the weighted Frobenius distance, which gives
\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
.
Combining this with (
D.7
) gives

\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}\tau_{*},

which proves
(2)
at time
t+1
.

It remains to transfer the
2\to\infty
bound. Define

\bm{Q}_{t+1}:=(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{-1}\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*}.

Then
\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1}=\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}\bm{Q}_{t+1}
and
\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1}=\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}\bm{Q}_{t+1}^{-{\intercal}}
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\,+\Big(\|\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}\|_{2\to\infty}\vee\|\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}\|_{2\to\infty}\Big)\big(\|\bm{Q}_{t+1}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\vee\|\bm{Q}_{t+1}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\big).

By (
D.15
), we have
\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\rho^{t+1}\psi_{nq}\omega_{*}
, and thus

\|\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}\|_{2\to\infty}\vee\|\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\omega_{*}+\rho^{t+1}\psi_{nq}\omega_{*}\leq(1+c_{0})\omega_{*}\leq 2\omega_{*},

after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary.
Also, since all singular values of
\bm{G}_{t}^{*}
lie in
[2/3,3/2]
, we have
\|\bm{G}_{t}^{*}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{-1}\|\leq 3/2
. Hence

\displaystyle\|\bm{Q}_{t+1}-\bm{I}_{r}\|

\displaystyle=\|(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{-1}(\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*}-\bm{G}_{t}^{*})\|\leq\|(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{-1}\|\,\|\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*}-\bm{G}_{t}^{*}\|,

\displaystyle\|\bm{Q}_{t+1}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{I}_{r}\|

\displaystyle=\|((\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{-{\intercal}})\,(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{\intercal}\|\leq\|\bm{G}_{t}^{*}\|\,\|(\bm{G}_{t+1}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}-(\bm{G}_{t}^{*})^{-{\intercal}}\|.

Combining these bounds with (
D.16
), we get

\|\bm{Q}_{t+1}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\vee\|\bm{Q}_{t+1}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\leq C\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}\frac{\tau_{*}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}\leq C\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}\sqrt{r\kappa}.

It then follows from (
D.15
) that

\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}\leq(2\rho-1)\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}\omega_{*}+C\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}\sqrt{r\kappa}\,\omega_{*}.

With the scaling condition that

\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\kappa^{3/2}\sqrt{r}\,\frac{\phi_{nq}}{\psi_{nq}}\leq c_{0}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sqrt{r\kappa}\leq\kappa^{3/2}\sqrt{r},

after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary, we conclude that
C\phi_{nq}\sqrt{r\kappa}\leq\psi_{nq}
. This finally leads to

\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\rho^{t+1}\psi_{nq}\omega_{*}.

This proves
(3)
at time
t+1
, and closes the induction.

D.3
Proof of Theorem
4

We prove in this subsection the stronger contraction statement under the localization
\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
. Accordingly, throughout the proof we invoke Assumptions
3
,
4
,
B.7
, and
B.8
with
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
, which has been defined as

\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)=\Big\{(\bm{U},\bm{V}):\frac{dist_{2}\{(\bm{U},\bm{V}),(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\}}{\tau_{*}}\leq\epsilon,\quad\frac{dist_{\infty}\{(\bm{U},\bm{V}),(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\}}{\omega_{*}}\leq\epsilon\Big\}.

The
\ell_{2}
-only part of Theorem
2
, where only Assumptions
3
–
4
are imposed with
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
, is proved later in Section
D.4
by the same argument after removing the row-wise estimates.

Throughout the proof, we use the population first-order condition
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})=\mathbb{E}\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
. We write
\Delta_{2}
and
\Delta_{\infty}
for
\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)
and
\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)
in (
21
) for brevity. We work throughout on the event in (
21
), which has probability at least
1-\delta
.

Similar to the proof of Theorem
4
, we introduce a local empirical optimizer
that will serve as the statistical contraction target. Let
(\widehat{\bm{U}},\widehat{\bm{V}})\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})
and
\widehat{\bm{G}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\bm{G}\in GL(r)}\|(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{G}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{G}}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
. In what follows, we show that, with probability at least
1-\delta
,
(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{G}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{G}}^{-{\intercal}})=(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
is unique and satisfies

{dist_{2}\{(\widehat{\bm{U}},\widehat{\bm{V}}),(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\}}\leq C\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\tau_{*}\,\text{;}\,{dist_{\infty}\{(\widehat{\bm{U}},\widehat{\bm{V}}),(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\}}\leq C\frac{\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}{\omega_{*}},

(D.19)

for some universal constant
C
. Moreover, it satisfy the first-order condition
\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{V}}^{\intercal})={\bm{0}}
and
\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{V}}^{\intercal})={\bm{0}}
, and thus serves as the contraction target of (
2
).

Fix the radius
\epsilon
, we choose some
\varepsilon
such that

\frac{1}{c_{0}}\Big(\sqrt{r\kappa}\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\vee\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\Big)\leq\varepsilon\leq c_{0}\frac{\epsilon}{\zeta_{r}},

(D.20)

where
c_{0}>0
is a sufficiently small universal constant. Moreover, after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary, we record the consequences

\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\leq c_{0}\frac{\alpha}{\sqrt{\kappa r}}\varepsilon,\qquad\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\leq c_{0}\big(\frac{\alpha}{\kappa\sqrt{r}}\wedge\varepsilon\big),\qquad\varepsilon\leq c_{0}\big(\frac{\epsilon}{\zeta_{r}}\wedge\frac{\alpha}{\sqrt{r}\,\kappa}\big).

(D.21)

We next introduce a smaller compact neighborhood
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)
for the same
\epsilon
and
\varepsilon
to construct a stationary point by minimizing the gradient norm on a compact set, and a slightly larger neighborhood
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
on which the local curvature and Lipschitz bounds will be invoked. In particular, define

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)=\Big\{(\bm{U},\bm{V}):\frac{\|(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}}{\tau_{*}}\leq\varepsilon,\quad\frac{\|(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}}{\omega_{*}}\leq\epsilon\Big\}.

(D.22)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)=\Big\{(\bm{U},\bm{V}):\frac{\|(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}}{\tau_{*}}\leq\epsilon,\quad\frac{\|(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}}{\omega_{*}}\leq\epsilon\Big\},

(D.23)

Since
\varepsilon\leq\epsilon
, we have
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)\subseteq\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
. In particular,
(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)
.
Similar to the proof of Theorem
3
, define

(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)}\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big\|_{2,\infty}^{2}.

Such a minimizer exists because
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)
is compact and
(\bm{U},\bm{V})\mapsto\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\|_{2,\infty}^{2}
is continuous. Let

\bm{E}_{U}:=\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\qquad\bm{E}_{V}:=\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*},\qquad\bm{E}:=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{E}_{U}\\
\bm{E}_{V}\end{pmatrix}.

Our proof proceeds as follows.

(1)

constructs an interior stationary point
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
of
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}
in
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\varepsilon,\epsilon}
and establish the first-order optimality condition;

(2)

identifies this point with the aligned constrained optimizer
(\widehat{\bm{U}},\widehat{\bm{V}})
and sharpens the statistical rates to prove (
D.19
)

(3)

re-centers the recursion at
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
and proves the contraction bounds for the gradient iterates.

Step 1: construct an interior stationary point of
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}
.

Since
(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)
,
\nabla p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
, and
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
minimizes
\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\cdot)\|_{2,\infty}
over
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\big\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\big\|_{2,\infty}

(D.24)

\displaystyle=\max\Big\{q^{-1}\|\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty},\ n^{-1}\|\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\max\Big\{q^{-1}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty},\ n^{-1}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq C\Delta_{\infty}\max\Big\{\|\bm{V}^{*}\|/\sqrt{q},\ \|\bm{U}^{*}\|/\sqrt{n}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq C\Delta_{\infty}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}},

where we used
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
and the bound in (
21b
).
Consequently,

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\Big(q^{-1}\|\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+n^{-1}\|\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}

(D.25)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2nq}\,\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\big\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\Delta_{\infty}\sqrt{nq\kappa\sigma_{\min}}.

For
s\in[0,1]
, define the interpolation segment

(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))=(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})+s(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V}).

Since
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)\subseteq\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
, the whole segment lies in
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
. Applying the integral mean value theorem to
\mathrm{vec}\{\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\cdot)^{\intercal}\}
along this segment yields

\displaystyle\underbrace{\bm{S}_{z}\bar{\mathcal{H}}\bm{S}_{z}\,\mathrm{vec}\big((\bm{S}_{Z}^{-1}\bm{E})^{\intercal}\big)}_{\Gamma_{1}}+\underbrace{\bm{S}_{z}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\bm{S}_{z}\,\mathrm{vec}\big((\bm{S}_{Z}^{-1}\bm{E})^{\intercal}\big)}_{\Gamma_{2}}+\underbrace{\bm{S}_{z}\bar{\mathcal{G}}\bm{S}_{z}\,\mathrm{vec}\big((\bm{S}_{Z}^{-1}\bm{E})^{\intercal}\big)}_{\Gamma_{3}}

(D.26)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathrm{vec}\Big(\bm{S}_{Z}\{\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})-\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\}^{\intercal}\Big)}_{\Gamma_{4}},

where

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{H}}:=

\displaystyle\,\int_{0}^{1}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))-\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\Big\}\,ds,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}:=

\displaystyle\,\int_{0}^{1}\Big\{\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))-\mathbb{E}\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\Big\}\,ds,\qquad\bar{\mathcal{G}}:=\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\,ds.

Here
\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
is the block operator defined in Lemma
8
.
We next bound
\Gamma_{1}
–
\Gamma_{4}
.

For
\Gamma_{1}
, Lemma
8
yields

\lambda_{\min}(\bm{S}_{z}\bar{\mathcal{H}}\bm{S}_{z})\geq\frac{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}{2}.

(D.27)

For
\Gamma_{2}
, similar to the calculation of
\gamma_{2,t}
as in the deterministic contraction proof (Theorem
2
), apply the bound in (
21a
) to obtain

\displaystyle\|\Gamma_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{s\in[0,1]}\big\|\bm{S}_{z}\{\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))-\mathbb{E}\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\}\bm{S}_{z}\big\|\cdot\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{-1}\bm{E}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(D.28)

\displaystyle\leq C\Delta_{2}\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{-1}\bm{E}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=C\Delta_{2}\sqrt{nq}\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

Here, the last step follows from
\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{-1}\bm{E}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\sqrt{nq}\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
.

For
\Gamma_{3}
, use
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
and Assumption
4
with
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
in place of
\mathcal{G}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
to obtain

\displaystyle\|\Gamma_{3}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{nq}}\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\|\,\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{-1}\bm{E}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(D.29)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{2}}{\sqrt{nq}}\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|\bm{U}(s)\bm{V}(s)^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\,\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{-1}\bm{E}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}nq}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Here, we telescope

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}(s)\bm{V}(s)^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\|\bm{U}^{*}\|\,\|\bm{E}_{V}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|\bm{V}^{*}\|\,\|\bm{E}_{U}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|\bm{E}_{U}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\,\|\bm{E}_{V}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\sqrt{nq\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}},

where the last step uses
\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\varepsilon\tau_{*}\leq c_{0}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
as
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)
.

For
\Gamma_{4}
, note first that
\nabla p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
, so

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\Big(q^{-1}\|\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+n^{-1}\|\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}

(D.30)

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{G}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\|\Big(q^{-1}\|\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+n^{-1}\|\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{nq}\,\Delta_{2}\,\tau_{*}.

Combining this estimate with (
D.25
), we arrive at

\|\Gamma_{4}\|\leq\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\Delta_{\infty}\sqrt{nq\kappa\sigma_{\min}}+C\sqrt{nq}\,\Delta_{2}\,\tau_{*}.

(D.31)

Combining (
D.27
)–(
D.31
) and using
\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{-1}\bm{E}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\sqrt{nq}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq

\displaystyle C\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+C\frac{\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(D.32)

\displaystyle+C\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}+C\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\tau_{*}.

Since
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)
, we have
\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\varepsilon\tau_{*}
. Together with
\tau_{*}\leq\sqrt{r\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
and (
D.21
), this implies

\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\leq c_{0},\qquad\frac{\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\varepsilon\frac{\sqrt{r}\,\kappa}{\alpha}\leq c_{0}.

Hence, after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary, the first two terms on the right-hand side of (
D.32
) can be absorbed into the left-hand side, and we obtain

\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq C\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}+C\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\tau_{*}.

(D.33)

Note that
\tau_{*}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sigma_{j}(\bm{X}^{*})/\sqrt{nq}\geq(r\vee\kappa)\sigma_{\min}
, so
\tau_{*}\geq\sqrt{(r\vee\kappa)\sigma_{\min}}
. Combining this lower bound with (
D.20
) and the theorem scaling condition
\zeta_{r}\Delta_{\infty}/(\alpha\sigma_{\min})\leq c_{0}\epsilon
shows that the right-hand side of (
D.33
) is at most
(\varepsilon/2)\tau_{*}
after shrinking
c_{0}
. That is,

\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon\tau_{*}.

(D.34)

We next derive a crude weighted
2\to\infty
bound. For
i,j\in[n]
and
\ell,k\in[q]
, define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{ij}^{\mathcal{L},UU}

\displaystyle:=q^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\bm{u}_{i}\bm{u}_{j}}^{2}\mathcal{L}\big(\bm{U}(s)\bm{V}(s)^{\intercal}\big)\,ds,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{i\ell}^{\mathcal{L},UV}

\displaystyle:=q^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{u}_{i}\bm{v}_{\ell}}^{2}\mathcal{L}\big(\bm{U}(s)\bm{V}(s)^{\intercal}\big)-G_{i\ell}\big(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s)\big)\bm{I}_{r}\Big\}\,ds,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{\ell i}^{\mathcal{L},VU}

\displaystyle:=n^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{v}_{\ell}\bm{u}_{i}}^{2}\mathcal{L}\big(\bm{U}(s)\bm{V}(s)^{\intercal}\big)-G_{i\ell}\big(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s)\big)\bm{I}_{r}\Big\}\,ds,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{\ell k}^{\mathcal{L},VV}

\displaystyle:=n^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\bm{v}_{\ell}\bm{v}_{k}}^{2}\mathcal{L}\big(\bm{U}(s)\bm{V}(s)^{\intercal}\big)\,ds,

and

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{i\ell}:=\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{G}_{i\ell}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\,ds,\qquad\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{i\ell}:=\int_{0}^{1}\bar{G}_{i\ell}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\,ds.

Then the weighted row-wise mean-value expansion gives, for each
i\in[n]
,

\Delta_{1,i}^{(U)}+\Delta_{2,i}^{(U)}+\Delta_{3,i}^{(U)}+\Delta_{4,i}^{(U)}+\Delta_{5,i}^{(U)}=\Delta_{6,i}^{(U)},

(D.35)

where, by letting
\bm{E}_{U,i}=(\bm{E}_{U})_{i,}
and
\bm{E}_{V,\ell}=(\bm{E}_{V})_{\ell,}
for
i\in[n]
and
\ell\in[q]
, we define

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,i}^{(U)}

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{ii}^{\mathcal{L},UU}\bm{E}_{U,i}^{\intercal},\qquad\qquad\;\Delta_{2,i}^{(U)}:=\sum_{j\neq i}\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{ij}^{\mathcal{L},UU}\bm{E}_{U,j}^{\intercal}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{i\ell}^{\mathcal{L},UV}\bm{E}_{V,\ell}^{\intercal},

\displaystyle\Delta_{3,i}^{(U)}

\displaystyle:=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{i\ell}\bm{E}_{V,\ell}^{\intercal},\qquad\quad\Delta_{4,i}^{(U)}:=q^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{i\ell}\bm{E}_{V,\ell}^{\intercal},

\displaystyle\Delta_{5,i}^{(U)}

\displaystyle:=q^{-1}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{u}_{i}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})-\nabla_{\bm{u}_{i}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\Big\},

\displaystyle\Delta_{6,i}^{(U)}

\displaystyle:=q^{-1}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{u}_{i}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})-\nabla_{\bm{u}_{i}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\Big\}.

Likewise, for each
\ell\in[q]
,

\Delta_{1,\ell}^{(V)}+\Delta_{2,\ell}^{(V)}+\Delta_{3,\ell}^{(V)}+\Delta_{4,\ell}^{(V)}+\Delta_{5,\ell}^{(V)}=\Delta_{6,\ell}^{(V)},

(D.36)

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,\ell}^{(V)}

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{\ell\ell}^{\mathcal{L},VV}\bm{E}_{V,\ell}^{\intercal},\qquad\qquad\;\Delta_{2,\ell}^{(V)}:=\sum_{k\neq\ell}\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{\ell k}^{\mathcal{L},VV}\bm{E}_{V,k}^{\intercal}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{\ell i}^{\mathcal{L},VU}\bm{E}_{U,i}^{\intercal},

\displaystyle\Delta_{3,\ell}^{(V)}

\displaystyle:=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{i\ell}\bm{E}_{U,i}^{\intercal},\qquad\quad\Delta_{4,\ell}^{(V)}:=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{i\ell}\bm{E}_{U,i}^{\intercal},

\displaystyle\Delta_{5,\ell}^{(V)}
