\displaystyle:=n^{-1}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{v}_{\ell}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})-\nabla_{\bm{v}_{\ell}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\Big\},

\displaystyle\Delta_{6,\ell}^{(V)}

\displaystyle:=n^{-1}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{v}_{\ell}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})-\nabla_{\bm{v}_{\ell}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\Big\}.

Let

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{LD}:=\mathrm{diag}\Big(\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{11}^{\mathcal{L},UU},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{nn}^{\mathcal{L},UU},\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{11}^{\mathcal{L},VV},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{qq}^{\mathcal{L},VV}\Big).

By Lemma
9
and the fact that
(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
for all
s\in[0,1]
, we have

\min\Big\{\min_{i\in[n]}\lambda_{\min}\big(\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{ii}^{\mathcal{L},UU}\big),\ \min_{\ell\in[q]}\lambda_{\min}\big(\widetilde{\bm{H}}_{\ell\ell}^{\mathcal{L},VV}\big)\Big\}\geq(1-c_{0})\alpha\sigma_{\min}.

(D.37)

Similar to the treatment of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{LD}
in the proof of Theorem
3
, by the block diagonal structure of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{LD}
, one has

\displaystyle\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle=\|\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{LD}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{LD}\,\mathrm{vec}(\bm{E}^{\intercal})\|_{\infty,r}

(D.38)

\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{LD}^{-1}\|_{\infty,r}\sum_{p=2}^{6}\Big(\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{p,i}^{(U)}\|\vee\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\Delta_{p,\ell}^{(V)}\|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-c_{0})\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\sum_{p=2}^{6}\Big(\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{p,i}^{(U)}\|\vee\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\Delta_{p,\ell}^{(V)}\|\Big).

Here, we abuse the notation to denote

\|\bm{A}\|_{\infty,r}:=\max_{i\in[n+q]}\sum_{j=1}^{n+q}\|\bm{A}_{\mathcal{R}_{i},\mathcal{R}_{j}}\|,\text{ for }\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{(n+q)r\times(n+q)r}

We now bound the five terms on the right-hand side.

Bound for
\Delta_{2,i}^{(U)}
and
\Delta_{2,\ell}^{(V)}
.
Using Assumption
B.7
exactly as in the Step 2 in the proof of Theorem
2
, we obtain

\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{2,i}^{(U)}\|\vee\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\Delta_{2,\ell}^{(V)}\|\leq C\beta\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\omega_{*}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

(D.39)

Bound for
\Delta_{3,i}^{(U)}
and
\Delta_{3,\ell}^{(V)}
.

For the
U
-part, by Cauchy–Schwarz,

\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{3,i}^{(U)}\|\leq q^{-1}\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{ave}}\|_{2\to\infty}\|\bm{E}_{V}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq q^{-1}\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{ave}}\|_{2\to\infty}\|\bm{E}_{V}\|_{\mathrm{F}},

where
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{ave}}:=\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\,ds
.
Moreover, by Assumption
B.8
, we have
\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{ave}}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq L_{\infty}

\times\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|\bm{U}(s)\bm{V}(s)^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{2\to\infty}
.
For each
s\in[0,1]
,

\|\bm{U}(s)\bm{V}(s)^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|\bm{U}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\|\bm{E}_{V}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|\bm{E}_{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\|\bm{V}^{*}\|+\|\bm{E}_{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\|\bm{E}_{V}\|_{\mathrm{F}},

and therefore

\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{ave}}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq C\sqrt{q}\,\omega_{*}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+C\sqrt{q\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|\bm{E}\|_{2,\infty}.

Using
\|\bm{E}_{V}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{q}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
, we obtain

\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{3,i}^{(U)}\|\leq C\omega_{*}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}+C\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{E}\|_{2,\infty}.

The same argument for the
V
-part gives

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{3,i}^{(U)}\|\vee\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\Delta_{3,\ell}^{(V)}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\omega_{*}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}+C\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{E}\|_{2,\infty}

(D.40)

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\omega_{*}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+c_{0}\alpha\sigma_{\min}\,\|\bm{E}\|_{2,\infty},

where the second line uses (
D.21
) and
\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\varepsilon\tau_{*}\leq c_{0}\alpha(\sigma_{\min}/\kappa)^{1/2}
.

Bound for
\Delta_{4,i}^{(U)}
and
\Delta_{4,\ell}^{(V)}
.

By Assumption
21
,

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{4,i}^{(U)}\|

\displaystyle\leq q^{-1}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{ave}}\|_{\infty\to 1}\|\bm{E}_{V}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\,\|\bm{E}_{V}\|_{2\to\infty},

\displaystyle\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\Delta_{4,\ell}^{(V)}\|

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{ave}}^{\intercal}\|_{\infty\to 1}\|\bm{E}_{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\,\|\bm{E}_{U}\|_{2\to\infty},

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{ave}}:=\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\,ds
.
Hence, by the theorem assumption
\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\leq\alpha\sigma_{\min}/4
,

\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{4,i}^{(U)}\|\vee\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\Delta_{4,\ell}^{(V)}\|\leq\frac{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}{4}\,\|\bm{E}\|_{2,\infty}.

(D.41)

Bound for
\Delta_{5,i}^{(U)}
and
\Delta_{5,\ell}^{(V)}
.

Recall that
M(\bm{U},\bm{V})
has been defined as
n^{-1}(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}-q^{-1}\bm{V}^{\intercal}(\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})
and thus we write
p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\frac{\alpha nq}{4}\|M(\bm{U},\bm{V})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
. For a perturbation
(\bm{H}_{U},\bm{H}_{V})\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}\times\mathbb{R}^{q\times r}
,

DM(\bm{U},\bm{V})[\bm{H}_{U},\bm{H}_{V}]=n^{-1}\Big\{\bm{H}_{U}^{\intercal}\bm{U}+(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{H}_{U}\Big\}-q^{-1}\Big\{\bm{H}_{V}^{\intercal}(\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})+\bm{V}^{\intercal}\bm{H}_{V}\Big\}.

(D.42)

Hence the gradients are

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{U}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha q}{2}\Big\{\bm{U}M(\bm{U},\bm{V})^{\intercal}+(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*})M(\bm{U},\bm{V})\Big\},

(D.43)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{V}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha n}{2}\Big\{(\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})M(\bm{U},\bm{V})^{\intercal}+\bm{V}M(\bm{U},\bm{V})\Big\},

and the directional Hessian action is

\displaystyle D\!\left(\nabla_{\bm{U}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}\right)(\bm{U},\bm{V})[\bm{H}_{U},\bm{H}_{V}]=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha q}{2}\Big\{\bm{H}_{U}\big(M^{\intercal}+M\big)+\bm{U}\big(DM[\bm{H}_{U},\bm{H}_{V}]\big)^{\intercal}

(D.44)

\displaystyle+(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*})DM[\bm{H}_{U},\bm{H}_{V}]\Big\},

\displaystyle D\!\left(\nabla_{\bm{V}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}\right)(\bm{U},\bm{V})[\bm{H}_{U},\bm{H}_{V}]=

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha n}{2}\Big\{\bm{H}_{V}\big(M^{\intercal}+M\big)+(\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})\big(DM[\bm{H}_{U},\bm{H}_{V}]\big)^{\intercal}

\displaystyle+(\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})\,DM[\bm{H}_{U},\bm{H}_{V}]\Big\},

where
M=M(\bm{U},\bm{V})
and
DM[\bm{H}_{U},\bm{H}_{V}]=DM(\bm{U},\bm{V})[\bm{H}_{U},\bm{H}_{V}]
.
Since
\nabla p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
, we may use the simpler identity
\Delta_{5,i}^{(U)}=q^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{u}_{i}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
and
\Delta_{5,\ell}^{(V)}=n^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{v}_{\ell}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
. The decomposition
M(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})=n^{-1}\bm{E}_{U}^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}-q^{-1}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{E}_{V}+n^{-1}\bm{E}_{U}^{\intercal}\bm{E}_{U}-q^{-1}\bm{E}_{V}^{\intercal}\bm{E}_{V}
implies that

\|M(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|\leq C\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq C\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}},

where the last step again uses
\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\varepsilon\tau_{*}\leq c_{0}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
.
Applying (
D.43
), we find

q^{-1}\|\nabla_{\bm{u}_{i}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}\Big(\|\bm{u}_{i}^{*}\|+2\|\bm{E}_{U,i}\|\Big)\|M(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|,

and similarly

n^{-1}\|\nabla_{\bm{v}_{\ell}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}\Big(\|\bm{v}_{\ell}^{*}\|+2\|\bm{E}_{V,\ell}\|\Big)\|M(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{5,i}^{(U)}\|\vee\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\Delta_{5,\ell}^{(V)}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha\|M(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|\big(\omega_{*}+\|\bm{E}\|_{2,\infty}\big)

(D.45)

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\omega_{*}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+C\alpha\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{E}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\omega_{*}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+c_{0}\alpha\sigma_{\min}\,\|\bm{E}\|_{2,\infty}.

Bound for
\Delta_{6,i}^{(U)}
and
\Delta_{6,\ell}^{(V)}
.

Using the minimizing property of
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
together with (
D.24
), we have

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{6,i}^{(U)}\|\vee\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\Delta_{6,\ell}^{(V)}\|

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\big\|_{2,\infty}+\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\big\|_{2,\infty}

(D.46)

\displaystyle\leq C\Delta_{\infty}\max\Big\{\|\bm{V}^{*}\|/\sqrt{q},\ \|\bm{U}^{*}\|/\sqrt{n}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq C\Delta_{\infty}\omega_{*},

where the last step uses
\|\bm{V}^{*}\|/\sqrt{q}\leq\|\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\omega_{*}
and
\|\bm{U}^{*}\|/\sqrt{n}\leq\|\bm{U}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\omega_{*}
.

Substituting (
D.39
), (
D.40
), (
D.41
), (
D.45
), and (
D.46
) into (
D.38
), we obtain

\|\bm{E}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\frac{1}{3}\|\bm{E}\|_{2,\infty}+C\sqrt{\frac{\kappa}{\sigma_{\min}}}\Big(\frac{\beta}{\alpha}+1\Big)\omega_{*}\,\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+C\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\omega_{*}.

Using (
D.34
) and
\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon\tau_{*}\leq C\varepsilon\sqrt{r\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
, we conclude that

\|\bm{E}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\frac{1}{3}\|\bm{E}\|_{2,\infty}+C\Big(\frac{\beta}{\alpha}+1\Big)\sqrt{r}\,\kappa\,\varepsilon\,\omega_{*}+C\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\omega_{*}.

By (
D.20
) and
\varepsilon\leq\epsilon
, the last two terms on the right-hand side are bounded by
C\zeta_{r}\varepsilon\,\omega_{*}+C\Delta_{\infty}\omega_{*}/(\alpha\sigma_{\min})\leq Cc\epsilon\,\omega_{*}+Cc_{0}\varepsilon\,\omega_{*}\leq c_{0}\epsilon\omega_{*},
. After shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary, this yields

\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}=\|\bm{E}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}\epsilon\omega_{*}.

(D.47)

Therefore
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
lies in the interior of
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)
.

It remains to show that
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
is stationary for
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}
, and that
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}
is uniformly strongly convex on
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
. For any
(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
, Lemma
8
gives
\lambda_{\min}\!\big(\bm{S}_{z}\big\{\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})-\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big\}\bm{S}_{z}\big)\geq{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}/{2}
.
Moreover, using the population first-order condition
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
, Equation
21a
, and Assumption
B.8
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)}\big\|\bm{S}_{z}\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\bm{S}_{z}\big\|

\displaystyle=\sup_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)}\frac{\|\mathcal{G}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\|}{\sqrt{nq}}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{2}+\sup_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)}\frac{\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})-\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\|}{\sqrt{nq}}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{2}+\frac{L_{2}}{\sqrt{nq}}\sup_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)}\|\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

For
(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
, we note that we can bound

\displaystyle\frac{\|\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sqrt{nq}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\|\bm{V}^{*}\|}{\sqrt{q}}+\frac{\|\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sqrt{q}}\frac{\|\bm{U}^{*}\|}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\|\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sqrt{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\tau_{*}\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}+\epsilon^{2}\tau_{*}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\sqrt{r}\,\kappa\,\sigma_{\min},

where we used
\|(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\epsilon\tau_{*}
and
\tau_{*}\leq\sqrt{r\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
.
Hence we know

\sup_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)}\big\|\bm{S}_{z}\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\bm{S}_{z}\big\|\leq\Delta_{2}+CL_{2}\epsilon\sqrt{r}\,\kappa\,\sigma_{\min}\leq c_{0}\alpha\sigma_{\min},

after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary, where we used (
D.21
). It therefore follows that

\inf_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)}\lambda_{\min}\!\Big(\bm{S}_{z}\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\bm{S}_{z}\Big)\geq\frac{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}{4}.

(D.48)

Since
\bm{S}_{z}
is invertible,
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}
is strongly convex on
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
; in particular, it has a unique minimizer there.
Because
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
is an interior minimizer of
(\bm{U},\bm{V})\mapsto\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\|_{2,\infty}^{2}
over
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)
, and (
D.48
) implies that
\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
is positive definite, we must have

\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})={\bm{0}}.

(D.49)

Indeed, if
\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\neq{\bm{0}}
, then with the following direction

\bm{d}:=-\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\Big\}^{-1}\mathrm{vec}\!\left(\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})^{\intercal}\right),

and
(\bm{D}_{U},\bm{D}_{V})
denoting the matrix version of
\bm{d}
, we would have

\displaystyle\mathrm{vec}\!\left(\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}+t\bm{D}_{U},\widetilde{\bm{V}}+t\bm{D}_{V})^{\intercal}\right)=

\displaystyle\,\mathrm{vec}\!\left(\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})^{\intercal}\right)

\displaystyle\,-t\,\mathrm{vec}\!\left(\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})^{\intercal}\right)+o(t)

as
t\to 0
. Therefore, it holds that
\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}+t\bm{D}_{U},\widetilde{\bm{V}}+t\bm{D}_{V})\|_{2,\infty}<\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\infty}
for all sufficiently small
t>0
, contradicting the interior optimality of
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
.
Hence, we establish that
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
is stationary for
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}
. Furthermore, since
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\cdot)
is strongly convex on
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
, (
D.49
) further implies that
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
is the unique minimizer of
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\cdot)
on
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
.
This completes Step 1.

Step 2: sharpen the statistical rates.

We now sharpen the weighted Frobenius and weighted
\ell_{\infty}
bounds for
(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})=(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})
. We first sharpen the weighted Frobenius error.
Returning to (
D.26
), Step 1 gives (
D.49
), so
\Gamma_{4,i}
on the right-hand side becomes

\Gamma_{4}=-\mathrm{vec}\Big(\bm{S}_{Z}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\Big).

Since (
D.30
) gives that
\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\sqrt{nq}\,\Delta_{2}\,\tau_{*}
, together with the bounds on
\Gamma_{2}
and
\Gamma_{3}
from Step 1, we follow the derivation of (
D.32
) to obtain

\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq C\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+C\frac{\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}+C\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\tau_{*}.

(D.50)

Using (
D.34
) and
\tau_{*}\leq\sqrt{r\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
, we further have

\frac{\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq C\frac{\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\varepsilon\tau_{*}\leq C\varepsilon\frac{\sqrt{r}\,\kappa}{\alpha}\leq Cc_{0}.

Together with
\Delta_{2}/(\alpha\sigma_{\min})\leq c_{0}
, after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary, the first two terms on the right-hand side of (
D.50
) can be absorbed into the left-hand side. Therefore, we arrive at

\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq C\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\tau_{*}.

(D.51)

We next sharpen the weighted
\ell_{\infty}
bound.
Returning to the last pre-absorption inequality in Step 1, one has

\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\frac{1}{3}\|(\bm{E}_{U},\bm{E}_{V})\|_{2,\infty}+C\Big(\frac{\beta}{\alpha}+1\Big)\sqrt{r}\,\kappa\,\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\omega_{*}+C\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\omega_{*},

(D.52)

where the second term on the right side follows from (
D.51
). Therefore, we have from the scaling condition (
D.21
) that
(\alpha^{-1}\beta+1)\sqrt{r}\,\kappa\leq(\alpha^{-1}\beta+\sqrt{r})\sqrt{r}\kappa=\zeta_{r}\leq c_{0}\Delta_{\infty}/\Delta_{2}
. We therefore obtain

\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}\leq C\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\omega_{*}.

(D.53)

Next, we show that
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})=(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{Q}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{-{\intercal}})
. We first claim

p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})=0.

(D.54)

Suppose not.
Apply Lemma
11
with
\bm{P}=\bm{I}_{r}
to
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
. By (
D.51
), (
D.21
), and

\tau_{*}\leq\sqrt{r\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
,

\max\Big\{n^{-1/2}\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}},\ q^{-1/2}\|\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\Big\}\leq C\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\tau_{*}\leq Cc_{0}\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}<\frac{1}{80}\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}

for sufficiently small
c_{0}
.
Hence the optimal alignment matrix
\bm{Q}_{e}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\bm{G}\in GL(r)}\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\bm{G}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}\bm{G}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
exists. Moreover, Lemma
11
implies

\|\bm{Q}_{e}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\vee\|\bm{Q}_{e}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\leq C\frac{\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}\leq C\sqrt{r\kappa}\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\leq Cc_{0}\varepsilon,

where the last step uses the lower bound in (
D.20
).

Together with the strict interior bounds from Step 1, one has

\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon\tau_{*},\qquad\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}\epsilon\omega_{*},

which subsequently gives

\displaystyle\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\bm{Q}_{e}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}\bm{Q}_{e}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}(\bm{Q}_{e}-\bm{I}_{r}),\widetilde{\bm{V}}(\bm{Q}_{e}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{I}_{r}))\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon\tau_{*}+C\tau_{*}\big(\|\bm{Q}_{e}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\vee\|\bm{Q}_{e}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon\tau_{*}+Cc_{0}\varepsilon\tau_{*}<\varepsilon\tau_{*}.

Similarly, one can get

\displaystyle\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\bm{Q}_{e}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}\bm{Q}_{e}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}+\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}(\bm{Q}_{e}-\bm{I}_{r}),\widetilde{\bm{V}}(\bm{Q}_{e}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{I}_{r}))\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\epsilon\omega_{*}+C\omega_{*}\big(\|\bm{Q}_{e}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\vee\|\bm{Q}_{e}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\epsilon\omega_{*}+Cc_{0}\varepsilon\omega_{*}<\epsilon\omega_{*},

for sufficiently small
c_{0}
.
Therefore, we arrive at
(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\bm{Q}_{e},\widetilde{\bm{V}}\bm{Q}_{e}^{-{\intercal}})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)\subseteq\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
. Applying Lemma
10
to the aligned pair

(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\bm{Q}_{e},\widetilde{\bm{V}}\bm{Q}_{e}^{-{\intercal}})
, we obtain
p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\bm{Q}_{e},\widetilde{\bm{V}}\bm{Q}_{e}^{-{\intercal}})=0
.
The rotational invariance
(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\bm{Q}_{e})(\widetilde{\bm{V}}\bm{Q}_{e}^{-{\intercal}})^{\intercal}=\widetilde{\bm{U}}\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{\intercal}
therefore yields

h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\bm{Q}_{e},\widetilde{\bm{V}}\bm{Q}_{e}^{-{\intercal}})=\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{\intercal})<\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{\intercal})+p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})=h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}}),

contradicting the fact that
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
is the unique minimizer of
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\cdot)
on
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
. This proves (
D.54
).

Since (
D.49
) and (
D.54
) hold, and the gradient of
p_{\alpha}^{\natural}
vanishes whenever
p_{\alpha}^{\natural}=0
, we conclude that

\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{\intercal})={\bm{0}},\qquad\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{\intercal})={\bm{0}}.

(D.55)

Equivalently, there is

\mathcal{G}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\widetilde{\bm{V}}={\bm{0}},\qquad\mathcal{G}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})^{\intercal}\widetilde{\bm{U}}={\bm{0}}.

(D.56)

We finally identify
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
with the aligned constrained optimizer
(\widehat{\bm{U}},\widehat{\bm{V}})
.
By definition of
\widehat{\bm{Q}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\bm{G}\in GL(r)}=\|(\widehat{\bm{U}}\bm{G}-\bm{U}^{*},\widehat{\bm{V}}\bm{G}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
, it holds that
(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{Q}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{-{\intercal}})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
, and therefore Lemma
10
yields
p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{Q}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{-{\intercal}})=0
.
Since
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
and
(\widehat{\bm{U}},\widehat{\bm{V}})
minimizes
\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})
over
\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
, we have

h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{Q}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{-{\intercal}})=\mathcal{L}(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{V}}^{\intercal})\leq\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{\intercal})=h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}}),

where the last equality uses (
D.54
).
On the other hand,
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
is the unique minimizer of
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\cdot)
on
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
, and consequently,
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\leq h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{Q}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{-{\intercal}})
.
Hence, equality holds, and uniqueness implies

(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})=(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{Q}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{-{\intercal}}).

(D.57)

Consequently, with the rates for
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
established above and the equivalence (
D.57
), we conclude that the optimizer bounds in (
D.19
) hold, and that the aligned solution
(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{Q}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{-{\intercal}})
is unique and satisfies the first-order condition.
Step 2 is completed.

Step 3:
\ell_{2}
- and
\ell_{\infty}
error contraction for
(\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t})
.

By Step 2, we have
(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{Q}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{-{\intercal}})=(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
.
It suffices to control the gradient iterates relative to the re-centered pair

(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
.
Specifically, we define the re-centered balancing penalty

p_{\alpha,\dagger}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V}):=\frac{\alpha nq}{4}\Big\|n^{-1}(\bm{U}-\widetilde{\bm{U}})^{\intercal}\bm{U}-q^{-1}\bm{V}^{\intercal}(\bm{V}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

and the associated objective
h_{\alpha,\dagger}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V}):=\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})+p_{\alpha,\dagger}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
.
By (
D.55
), there is

\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha,\dagger}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})={\bm{0}},\qquad\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha,\dagger}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})={\bm{0}}.

(D.58)

For later use, similar to the purpose of defining
\phi^{\dagger}
and
\psi_{n}^{\dagger}
, we let

\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}:=\phi_{nq}+C\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}},\qquad\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}:=\psi_{nq}+C\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}},

(D.59)

where
C>0
is a sufficiently large universal constant. By the initialization assumptions in Theorem
2
, the noise assumptions in Theorem
4
, and the estimator bounds proved in Steps 2,
after enlarging
C
and shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary, we have

\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\leq\frac{2}{3}\epsilon\wedge\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{2}\kappa^{2}\sqrt{r\kappa}},\qquad\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\leq\frac{2}{3}\epsilon,\qquad\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\kappa\sqrt{\kappa r}\frac{\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}}{\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}}\leq c_{0}.

(D.60)

Here, the bound for the ratio
\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}/\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}
can be verified via
\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}/\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\leq\phi_{nq}/\psi_{nq}+\Delta_{2}/\Delta_{\infty}
together with the initialization requirement (
13
) and (
15
) and the theorem’s scaling condition (
D.21
).

Moreover, (
D.51
), (
D.53
), and Weyl’s inequality give

\sigma_{r}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}/\sqrt{n})\wedge\sigma_{r}(\widetilde{\bm{V}}/\sqrt{q})\geq(1-c_{0})\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}},\qquad\frac{\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\vee\frac{\|\widetilde{\bm{V}}\|}{\sqrt{q}}\leq C\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}},

(D.61)

\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}\|_{2\to\infty}\vee\|\widetilde{\bm{V}}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq 2\omega_{*},\qquad\widetilde{\tau}_{*}^{2}:=\frac{n^{-1}\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+q^{-1}\|\widetilde{\bm{V}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{2}\leq 2\tau_{*}^{2}.

(D.62)

The explicit Hessian formulas for
p_{\alpha}^{\natural}
established in Step 1 are reference invariant. Therefore the proof of Theorem
2
applies verbatim after replacing
(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})
by
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
. In particular, the same lower and upper bounds for the re-centered Hessian blocks hold along any segment contained in
\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
, up to changing absolute constants.

For each
t\geq 0
, let
\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\bm{G}\in GL(r)}\|(\bm{U}^{t}\bm{G}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\,\bm{V}^{t}\bm{G}^{-{\intercal}}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
,
and define the aligned iterates and errors

\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}:=\bm{U}^{t}\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger},\qquad\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}:=\bm{V}^{t}(\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}},\qquad\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t}:=\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\qquad\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t}:=\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\widetilde{\bm{V}}.

(D.63)

Since
\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}
is the optimal alignment of
(\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t})
to
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
, the proof of Lemma
10
, with
(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})
replaced by
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
, leads to
n^{-1}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}-\widetilde{\bm{U}})^{\intercal}\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}=q^{-1}\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t{\intercal}}(\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})
. This identity is the re-centered analogue of the balancing relation used in the deterministic proof, and it again forces the penalty gradient to vanish at the aligned iterate. Subsequently, we know that

\nabla_{\bm{U}}p_{\alpha,\dagger}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})={\bm{0}},\qquad\nabla_{\bm{V}}p_{\alpha,\dagger}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})={\bm{0}}.

Consequently, writing
\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger}:=(\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger})^{\intercal}\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}
, the gradient update
can be rewritten as the re-centered version

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle:=\bm{U}^{t+1}\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}=\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}-\frac{\eta}{q}\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha,\dagger}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})-\frac{\eta}{q}\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha,\dagger}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger}-\bm{I}_{r}),

(D.64)

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle:=\bm{V}^{t+1}(\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}=\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\frac{\eta}{n}\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha,\dagger}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})-\frac{\eta}{n}\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha,\dagger}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\big\{(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big\}.

(D.65)

In what follows, we follow the proof of Theorem
2
to prove by induction on
t
that, for all
0\leq s\leq t
,

(1)

the optimal alignment matrix
\bm{G}_{s}^{\dagger}
exists;

(2)

\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{s},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{s})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho^{s}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\tau_{*}
;

(3)

\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{s},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{s})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\rho^{s}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\omega_{*}
;

(4)

\|\bm{G}_{s}^{\dagger}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{s}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\leq\iota_{0}\alpha/(\beta\kappa)
.

for some sufficiently small
\iota_{0}\in(0,1/30)
.

Initialization.

By the theorem’s condition on the initialization
(\bm{U}^{0},\bm{V}^{0})
, we know that

\max\Big\{n^{-1/2}\|\bm{U}^{0}\bm{R}^{0}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}\|_{\mathrm{F}},q^{-1/2}\|\bm{V}^{0}(\bm{R}^{0})^{-{\intercal}}-\widetilde{\bm{V}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\Big\}\leq\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\tau_{*}.

With the scaling condition
\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\tau_{*}\leq c_{0}{\alpha}/(\beta^{2}\kappa^{2}\sqrt{r\kappa})\times\sqrt{r\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\leq c_{0}{\alpha}(\beta\kappa)^{-1}\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}
by (
D.60
) and
\tau_{*}\leq\sqrt{r\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
, Lemma
11
, applied with
\bm{P}=\bm{R}^{0}
and reference
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
, yields that
\bm{G}_{0}^{\dagger}
exists and that

\|\bm{G}_{0}^{\dagger}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{0}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\leq\frac{5\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\tau_{*}}{\sigma_{r}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}/\sqrt{n})\wedge\sigma_{r}(\widetilde{\bm{V}}/\sqrt{q})}\leq C\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\frac{\tau_{*}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}\leq\iota_{0}\frac{\alpha}{\beta\kappa},

(D.66)

after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary.
This proves induction hypotheses
(1)
and
(4)
at
t=0
.

The theorem assumptions together with (
D.51
) and (
D.53
) imply

\|(\bm{U}^{0}\bm{R}^{0}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bm{V}^{0}(\bm{R}^{0})^{-{\intercal}}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\tau_{*},\qquad\|(\bm{U}^{0}\bm{R}^{0}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bm{V}^{0}(\bm{R}^{0})^{-{\intercal}}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\omega_{*}.

Hence with (
D.66
), one can verify that the induction hypotheses
(2)
and
(3)
hold at
t=0
.
Now assume
(1)
–
(4)
hold for all
0\leq s\leq t
. We verify them at time
t+1
.

Weighted
\ell_{2}
contraction for iterate
(\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t})
.

For
s\in[0,1]
and
t\geq 0
, define

\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s):=\widetilde{\bm{U}}+s\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\qquad\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s):=\widetilde{\bm{V}}+s\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t}.

By (
D.51
), (
D.53
), and the induction hypotheses, we have

\displaystyle dist_{2}\{(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)),(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+s\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(C\frac{\Delta_{2}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}+\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\Big)\tau_{*}\leq\epsilon\tau_{*},

\displaystyle dist_{\infty}\{(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)),(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\infty}+s\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(C\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}+\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\Big)\omega_{*}\leq\epsilon\omega_{*},

where the last two inequalities follow from (
D.21
) and
(
D.60
). Hence, we know that for all
s\in[0,1]
,

(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s))\in\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon).

(D.67)

Therefore, all assumptions stated with
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
are available along this interpolation path. Further let

\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{t}:=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{vec}\big((\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t})^{\intercal}\big)\\
\mathrm{vec}\big((\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})^{\intercal}\big)\end{pmatrix},\;\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}:=\bm{S}_{z}^{-1}\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{t},\;\bar{\bm{e}}_{t+1}:=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{vec}\big((\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}})^{\intercal}\big)\\
\mathrm{vec}\big((\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})^{\intercal}\big)\end{pmatrix},\;\bar{\bm{w}}_{t+1}:=\bm{S}_{z}^{-1}\bar{\bm{e}}_{t+1},

and

\vartheta_{t}^{\dagger}:=\|\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\vee\|(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\|,\qquad\mathcal{R}_{t}^{\dagger}:=\begin{pmatrix}(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger}-\bm{I}_{r})\otimes\bm{I}_{n}&{\bm{0}}\\
{\bm{0}}&\big((\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big)\otimes\bm{I}_{q}\end{pmatrix}.

Using (
D.64
)–(
D.65
),
(
D.58
), and the fundamental theorem of calculus (Theorem 4.2 in
28
, Chapter XIII) along the segment

\{(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)):0\leq s\leq 1\}
, we know

\bar{\bm{w}}_{t+1}=(\bm{I}_{nr+qr}-\eta\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}^{\dagger})\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}-\eta\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{t}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}-\eta\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}-\eta\mathcal{R}_{t}^{\dagger}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}^{\dagger}+\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{t}^{\dagger}+\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}^{\dagger})\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t},

where we define

\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}^{\dagger}:=\bm{S}_{z}\int_{0}^{1}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha,\dagger}^{\natural}(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s))-\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s))\Big\}ds\,\bm{S}_{z},

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{t}^{\dagger}:=\bm{S}_{z}\int_{0}^{1}\Big\{\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s))-\mathbb{E}\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s))\Big\}ds\,\bm{S}_{z},

\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}^{\dagger}:=\bm{S}_{z}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s))ds\,\bm{S}_{z}.

Therefore, we bound
\|\bar{\bm{\omega}}_{t+1}\|
by

\|\bar{\bm{w}}_{t+1}\|\leq\gamma_{1,t}^{\dagger}+\gamma_{2,t}^{\dagger}+\gamma_{3,t}^{\dagger}+\gamma_{4,t}^{\dagger},

with

\gamma_{1,t}^{\dagger}:=\|(\bm{I}_{nr+qr}-\eta\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}^{\dagger})\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|,\qquad\gamma_{2,t}^{\dagger}:=\eta\|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{t}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|,\qquad\gamma_{3,t}^{\dagger}:=\eta\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|,

\gamma_{4,t}^{\dagger}:=\eta\|\mathcal{R}_{t}^{\dagger}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}^{\dagger}+\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{t}^{\dagger}+\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}^{\dagger})\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|.

The same proof as in Step 1 of Theorem
2
, using the reference pair
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
and (
D.67
), gives

\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}^{\dagger})\geq\frac{7}{8}\alpha\sigma_{\min},\qquad\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}^{\dagger})\leq 2(\alpha+\beta)\kappa\sigma_{\min}.

(D.68)

It follows that

\gamma_{1,t}^{\dagger}\leq\Big(1-\frac{7}{8}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Big)\|\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|.

(D.69)

Next, similar to bounding
\gamma_{2,t}
in the proof of Theorem
2
, Equation
21a
and the theorem’s scaling condition on
\Delta_{2}
yield

\gamma_{2,t}^{\dagger}\leq C\eta\Delta_{2}\|\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|\leq\frac{1}{16}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|,

(D.70)

after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary.

For
\gamma_{3,t}^{\dagger}
, use
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
,
Assumption
B.8
with
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
in place of
\mathcal{G}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
, and (
D.67
) to get

\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}^{\dagger}\|\leq\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\frac{\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s))\|}{\sqrt{nq}}\leq\frac{L_{2}}{\sqrt{nq}}\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\|\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\epsilon\sqrt{r}\,\kappa\sigma_{\min}.

The theorem scaling condition implies that
\sqrt{r}\,\kappa\epsilon/\alpha\leq c_{0}
, and therefore, we have

\gamma_{3,t}^{\dagger}\leq C\eta\epsilon\sqrt{r}\,\kappa\sigma_{\min}\|\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|\leq\frac{1}{16}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|.

(D.71)

Finally, exactly as in the proof of Theorem
2
, the induction hypothesis
(4)
implies

\vartheta_{t}^{\dagger}\leq\frac{\alpha}{10\beta\kappa},

(D.72)

after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary. Combining this with
(
D.68
)–(
D.71
), we obtain

\gamma_{4,t}^{\dagger}\leq\eta\vartheta_{t}^{\dagger}\Big(\lambda_{\max}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{t}^{\dagger})\|\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|+\|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{t}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|+\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{t}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|\Big)\leq\frac{5}{8}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|.

(D.73)

Combining (
D.69
), (
D.70
),
(
D.71
), and (
D.73
), we conclude that
\|\bar{\bm{w}}_{t+1}\|\leq(1-\frac{1}{4}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min})\|\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}\|
.
With the induction hypothesis, we know

\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\tau_{*}.

(D.74)

Weighted
\ell_{\infty}
contraction for iterate
(\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t})
.

Write
\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{ii,t}^{UU,\dagger}
,
\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{i\ell,t}^{UV,\dagger}
, and
\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell\ell,t}^{VV,\dagger}
for the averaged Hessian blocks defined exactly as in Step 2 of the proof of Theorem
2
, but now for the re-centered objective
h_{\alpha,\dagger}^{\natural}
along the segment
\{(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)):0\leq s\leq 1\}
.
The same proof as in Theorem
2
, using
(
D.67
), (
D.61
), and (
D.62
), yields

\delta_{1,t}^{\dagger}\leq\Big(1-\frac{7}{8}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Big)\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty},\qquad\delta_{2,t}^{\dagger}\leq C\eta\beta\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\omega_{*}\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}},

(D.75)

where
\delta_{1,t}^{\dagger}
and
\delta_{2,t}^{\dagger}
denote the diagonal and off-diagonal Hessian contributions similar to
\delta_{1,t}
and
\delta_{2,t}
in Section
D.2
, respectively.

The new terms come from the stochastic and deterministic parts of
\mathcal{G}
.
Define

\displaystyle\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger,U}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\eta}{q}\max_{i\in[n]}\Big\|\Big[\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s))ds\;\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t}\Big]_{i,\cdot}\Big\|,

\displaystyle\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger,V}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\eta}{n}\max_{\ell\in[q]}\Big\|\Big[\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s))^{\intercal}ds\;\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t}\Big]_{\ell,\cdot}\Big\|,

and let
\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger}:=\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger,U}\vee\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger,V}
.
By (
21c
) and the theorem’s scaling condition for
\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)
, one has

\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger}\leq\eta\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\frac{1}{4}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}.

(D.76)

Next, we further define

\displaystyle\delta_{4,t}^{\dagger,U}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\eta}{q}\max_{i\in[n]}\Big\|\Big[\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s))ds\;\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t}\Big]_{i,\cdot}\Big\|,

\displaystyle\delta_{4,t}^{\dagger,V}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\eta}{n}\max_{\ell\in[q]}\Big\|\Big[\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s),\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s))^{\intercal}ds\;\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t}\Big]_{\ell,\cdot}\Big\|,

and let
\delta_{4,t}^{\dagger}:=\delta_{4,t}^{\dagger,U}\vee\delta_{4,t}^{\dagger,V}
.
By Assumption
B.8
with
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
in place of
\mathcal{G}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
,
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
, and
(
D.67
), we have for every
s\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle q^{-1/2}\|\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\,\|\bm{U}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\frac{\|\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sqrt{q}}

\displaystyle\,+\Big(\frac{\|\bm{V}^{*}\|}{\sqrt{q}}+\frac{\|\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sqrt{q}}\Big)\|\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\omega_{*}\,\epsilon\tau_{*}+C\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\epsilon\omega_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\alpha\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\kappa}}\,\omega_{*},

where the last step uses
\tau_{*}\leq\sqrt{r\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
and the theorem scaling condition
\sqrt{r}\,\kappa\epsilon/\alpha\leq c_{0}
.
Therefore, one has

\displaystyle\delta_{4,t}^{\dagger}\leq

\displaystyle C\eta L_{\infty}\Big(q^{-1/2}\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\|\bm{U}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)\bm{V}_{t}^{\dagger}(s)^{\intercal}-\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{2\to\infty}\Big)\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}

(D.77)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\eta\alpha\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\kappa}}\,\omega_{*}\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

Finally, let
\delta_{5,t}^{\dagger}
denote the balancing term coming from
\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger}-\bm{I}_{r}
and
(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}
.
Exactly as in the proof of Theorem
2
, (
D.68
), (
D.75
), (
D.76
), (
D.77
), and (
D.72
) imply

\delta_{5,t}^{\dagger}\leq\frac{1}{4}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}+C\eta\alpha\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\kappa}}\,\omega_{*}\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

(D.78)

Combining (
D.75
)–(
D.78
), and using
\beta\geq\alpha
and
\kappa\geq 1
, we obtain

\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\Big(1-\frac{9}{16}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Big)\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}+C\eta\beta\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\omega_{*}\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

Since
\tfrac{\beta}{\alpha}\kappa^{3/2}\sqrt{r}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}/\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\leq c_{0}
by (
D.60
), we know
C\beta\kappa\sqrt{r}\,\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\leq\tfrac{1}{16}\alpha\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}
after shrinking
c_{0}
. Therefore with induction hypotheses
(2)
–
(3)
, we arrive at

\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\infty}\leq(2\rho-1)\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\omega_{*}.

(D.79)

Update the alignment and transfer the bounds.

Set

\delta_{t+1}^{\dagger}:=\max\Big\{n^{-1/2}\|\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}\|_{\mathrm{F}},q^{-1/2}\|\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\Big\}.

Equation (
D.74
) already shows that
\delta_{t+1}^{\dagger}\leq\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\tau_{*}
.
Then using (
D.60
), (
D.61
), and
\tau_{*}\leq\sqrt{r\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
, we obtain
\delta_{t+1}^{\dagger}\leq Cc_{0}\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}<\big\{\sigma_{r}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}/\sqrt{n})\wedge\sigma_{r}(\widetilde{\bm{V}}/\sqrt{q})\big\}/80
, after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary.
Moreover, the induction hypothesis
(4)
implies

\|\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\leq\iota_{0}\frac{\alpha}{\beta\kappa}<\frac{1}{6},

so all singular values of
\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}
lie in
[2/3,3/2]
.
Applying Lemma
11
to the pair
(\bm{U}^{t+1},\bm{V}^{t+1})
with
\bm{P}=\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}
and reference pair
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
, we conclude that
\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger}
exists and

\displaystyle\|\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger}-\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}-(\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}\|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{5\delta_{t+1}^{\dagger}}{\sigma_{r}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}/\sqrt{n})\wedge\sigma_{r}(\widetilde{\bm{V}}/\sqrt{q})}

(D.80)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\frac{\tau_{*}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\sqrt{r\kappa}.

This proves induction hypothesis
(1)
at time
t+1
.
Similar to (
D.17
), telescoping the above increments yields

\|\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\leq C\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\frac{\sqrt{r\kappa}}{1-\rho}.

By the first scaling condition in (
D.60
), the right-hand side is at most
\iota_{0}\alpha/(\beta\kappa)
after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary. Hence induction hypothesis
(4)
also holds at time
t+1
.

Since
\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger}
minimizes the weighted Frobenius distance to
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
, we have

\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

Combining this with (
D.74
) proves the induction hypothesis
(2)
at time
t+1
.

To transfer the weighted
\ell_{\infty}
bound, define
\bm{Q}_{t+1}^{\dagger}:=(\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-1}\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger}
. Then we know
\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1}=\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}\bm{Q}_{t+1}^{\dagger}
and
\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1}=\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}(\bm{Q}_{t+1}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}
.
Therefore, one has

\displaystyle\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\|\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}\|_{2\to\infty}\vee\|\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}\|_{2\to\infty}\Big)\Big(\|\bm{Q}_{t+1}^{\dagger}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\vee\|(\bm{Q}_{t+1}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\Big).

Further with (
D.79
) and (
D.62
), we have

\|\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}\|_{2\to\infty}\vee\|\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}\|_{2\to\infty}\vee\|\widetilde{\bm{V}}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\infty}\leq 3\omega_{*},

after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary.
Also, all singular values of
\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}
lie in
[2/3,3/2]
, which yields with Lemma
11
that

\|\bm{Q}_{t+1}^{\dagger}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\vee\|(\bm{Q}_{t+1}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\leq C\Big(\|\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger}-\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}-(\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}\|\Big)\leq C\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\sqrt{r\kappa},

where the last step uses (
D.80
).
Substituting the above bound and (
D.79
) yields

\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\infty}\leq(2\rho-1)\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\omega_{*}+C\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\sqrt{r\kappa}\,\omega_{*}.

Since
\tfrac{\beta}{\alpha}\kappa^{3/2}\sqrt{r}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}/\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\leq c_{0}
and
\eta=\{(10(\alpha+\beta)\kappa\sigma_{\min}\}^{-1}
, we know

\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\sqrt{r\kappa}\leq\frac{1}{4}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}=(1-\rho)\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger},

after shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary. This eventually leads to

\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\rho^{t+1}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\omega_{*},

which proves the induction hypothesis
(3)
at time
t+1
and closes the induction.

To conclude, we have shown that, for all
t\geq 0
,

dist_{2}\{(\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t}),(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\}\leq\rho^{t}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\tau_{*},\qquad dist_{\infty}\{(\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t}),(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\}\leq\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\omega_{*}.

(D.81)

Hence, combining (
D.81
) with the optimizer bounds (
D.51
), (
D.53
), and the definition (
D.59
), we prove the iterate bounds in Theorem
4
.

D.4
Proof sketch for the
\ell_{2}
part of Theorems
2
and
4

We outline the common
\ell_{2}
argument under the larger local region
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
. Here, similar to the proof of Theorem
4
, we take
\epsilon=c_{0}\alpha/(\kappa\sqrt{r})
without loss of generality.
In the noisy case, we work on the event where (
21a
) holds with
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
, and assume Assumptions
3
and
4
hold with
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
and
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
in place of
\mathcal{G}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
. The noiseless case is recovered by setting
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\equiv 0
. Set

\varepsilon:=M\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}},\qquad\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{(2)}_{uv}(\varepsilon):=\Big\{(\bm{U},\bm{V}):\|(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\varepsilon\tau_{*}\Big\},

where
M>0
is a sufficiently large universal constant. After shrinking
c_{0}
if necessary, the smallness assumption on
\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)/(\alpha\sigma_{\min})
ensures
\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{(2)}_{uv}(\varepsilon)\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
.

As in Step 1 of the proof of Theorem
4
, define

(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{(2)}_{uv}(\varepsilon)}\Big\{q^{-1}\|\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+n^{-1}\|\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big\},

where
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V}):=\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})+p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
. Since
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
and
\nabla p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})={\bm{0}}
, we know by definition that

\displaystyle\,\Big\{q^{-1}\|\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+n^{-1}\|\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\Big\{q^{-1}\|\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+n^{-1}\|\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big\}^{1/2}\leq C\sqrt{nq}\,\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)\tau_{*}.

Repeating the weighted Frobenius-norm mean-value expansion from the optimizer analysis of Theorem
4
, but dropping all row-wise terms, yields

\displaystyle\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq

\displaystyle C\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\tau_{*}+C\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle+C\frac{\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

The theorem scaling allows us absorb the last two terms into the left-hand side, so

\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq C\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\tau_{*}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\tau_{*}.

The same argument from Lemma
8
then shows that
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}
is strongly convex on the aligned
\epsilon
-neighborhood, so
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
is an interior stationary point and hence the unique minimizer of
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}
on that set. The same identification argument as in step 2 in the proof of Theorem
4
in Section
D.3
, when restricted to the weighted Frobenius neighborhood, gives

p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})=0,\qquad\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{\intercal})={\bm{0}},\qquad\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{\intercal})={\bm{0}},

and
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})=(\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{G}},\widehat{\bm{V}}\widehat{\bm{G}}^{-{\intercal}})
. This proves the bound for the estimator.

Now we prove the result for the iterates. Let

\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}:=\phi_{nq}+C\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}},

and fix a sufficiently small constant
\iota_{0}\in(0,1/30)
. Similar to the weighted
\ell_{2}
contraction argument in Step 3 of the proof of Theorem
4
, one can prove by induction on
t
that, for all
0\leq s\leq t
,

(i)

the optimal alignment matrix
\bm{G}_{s}^{\dagger}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\bm{G}\in GL(r)}\|(\bm{U}^{s}\bm{G}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bm{V}^{s}\bm{G}^{-{\intercal}}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
exists;

(ii)

for
\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{s}:=(\bm{U}^{s}\bm{G}_{s}^{\dagger}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\,\bm{V}^{s}(\bm{G}_{s}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})
, then
\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{s}\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho^{s}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\tau_{*}
;

(iii)

\|\bm{G}_{s}^{\dagger}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{s}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\leq\iota_{0}\alpha/(\beta\kappa)
.

The base case follows from the initialization bound together with the estimate for
\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
, exactly as in Step 3 of Theorem
4
. Now assume

(
i
)

–

(

⁢
i
i
i

)

hold up to time
t
, and define the iterate
\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}:=\bm{U}^{t+1}\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}
,
\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}:=\bm{V}^{t+1}(\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}
.
The induction hypotheses imply that the line segment between
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
and
(\bm{U}^{t}\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger},\bm{V}^{t}(\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}})
stays inside
\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
. Therefore, the same one-step decomposition as in Step 3 gives

\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\gamma_{1,t}^{\dagger}+\gamma_{2,t}^{\dagger}+\gamma_{3,t}^{\dagger}+\gamma_{4,t}^{\dagger},

with

\gamma_{1,t}^{\dagger}\leq\Bigl(1-\frac{7}{8}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Bigr)\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2,\mathrm{F}},\qquad\gamma_{2,t}^{\dagger}+\gamma_{3,t}^{\dagger}+\gamma_{4,t}^{\dagger}\leq\frac{5}{8}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}.

Hence

\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\tau_{*}.

Since the right-hand side is again of order
o(\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}})
under the same scaling assumptions, the same alignment perturbation lemma as in Step 3 shows that
\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger}
exists and the same strategy of telescoping the increment yields

\|\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\leq\iota_{0}\frac{\alpha}{\beta\kappa}.

This proves

(
i
)

and

(

⁢
i
i
i

)

. Finally, by the optimality of
\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger}
,

\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t+1}\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\|(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq\rho^{t+1}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\tau_{*}.

This closes the induction. Combining the bound on
\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{t}\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
with the estimate for
\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
yields the stated contraction toward
(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})
.

Appendix E
Proof of Examples

Throughout this section, we use the notation defined in Section
D.1
. We present a proof of (
E.1
) in Section
E.1
. The proof of Theorems
A.5
and
A.6
are then provided in Sections
E.2
and
E.3
, respectively.

E.1
Proof of Bounds for Linear Model in (
18
)

As a warm-up, we verify (
18
). For the quadratic loss, we have
\nabla_{\bm{X}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{X})=n^{-1}(\bm{X}-\bm{X}^{*}+\bm{E})
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{Z})=\nabla_{\bm{X}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal})-\mathbb{E}\nabla_{\bm{X}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal})=n^{-1}\bm{E}
.
Note that
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{Z})
is independent of
\bm{Z}
, so all suprema over
\bm{Z}\in\mathcal{D}
are automatic.
For operator norm of
\bm{E}
, by the standard spectral norm bound for an
n\times n
matrix with independent mean-zero sub-Gaussian entries
[
2
]
, there is

\|\bm{E}\|\lesssim\sigma\{\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}\}

with probability at least
1-\delta
. Therefore, for (
17a
) to hold, we let

\Delta_{2}(n,\delta)\asymp\sigma\tfrac{\sqrt{n+\log(1/\delta)}}{n}.

Next, we control
\Delta_{\infty}(n,\delta)
. For each row
i\in[n]
, let
\bm{e}_{i}^{\intercal}
denote the
i
th row of
\bm{E}
. Then
\|\bm{E}\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\bm{e}_{i}^{\intercal}\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{2}
. For a fixed
i
, any unit vector
\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{r-1}
, we know
\bm{e}_{i}^{\intercal}\bm{Z}^{*}\bm{u}
is a mean-zero sub-Gaussian random variable with sub-Gaussian norm bounded by
\|\bm{e}_{i}^{\intercal}\bm{Z}^{*}\bm{u}\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\sigma\|\bm{Z}^{*}\bm{u}\|_{2}\leq\sigma\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|
. A standard
\varepsilon
-net argument on
\mathbb{S}^{r-1}
then gives, for every
u>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\|\bm{e}_{i}^{\intercal}\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{2}\geq C\sigma\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|\sqrt{r+u}\right)\leq e^{-u}.

Taking
t=\log(n/\delta)
and applying a union bound over
i=1,\ldots,n
, we obtain

\|\bm{E}\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\sigma\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|\sqrt{r+\log(n/\delta)},

with probability at least
1-\delta
. Hence, for (
17b
) to hold, we let

\Delta_{\infty}(n,\delta)\asymp\sigma\sqrt{\frac{r+\log(n/\delta)}{n}}.

Finally, we bound the maximal row-wise
\ell_{1}
norm. By definition,
\|\bm{E}\|_{\infty\to 1}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|E_{ij}|
.
For each
i,j\in[n]
, since
E_{ij}
is sub-Gaussian with
\|E_{ij}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\sigma
, the folded variable
|E_{ij}|-\mathbb{E}|E_{ij}|
is sub-exponential with sub-exponential norm bounded by
C\sigma
, and satisfied
\mathbb{E}|E_{ij}|\leq C\sigma
.
Therefore, Bernstein’s inequality gives, for each fixed row
i
and every
t>0
, we know
\sum_{j=1}^{n}|E_{ij}|\leq C\sigma n+C\sigma\{\sqrt{nt}+t\}

with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
. Taking again
t=\log(n/\delta)
and applying a union bound over
i=1,\dots,n
, we get

\|\bm{E}\|_{\infty\to 1}\lesssim\sigma n+\sigma\sqrt{n\log(n/\delta)}+\sigma\log(n/\delta)

with probability at least
1-\delta
. Finally, for (
17b
) to hold, we let

\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,\delta)\asymp\sigma+\sigma\sqrt{\frac{\log(n/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(n/\delta)}{n}.

When
\log(n/\delta)\lesssim n
, the final term is dominated by the square-root term, and the right-hand side is of order
\sigma
. This proves the claimed scale of
\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,\delta)
.

E.2
Proof of Theorem
A.5

For the sensing model
y_{i}=\langle\bm{A}_{i},\bm{X}^{*}\rangle+\xi_{i}
, we treat the measurement matrix
\bm{A}_{i}
as fixed. When they are random in nature, our analysis can be viewed as conditioning on them. Then for the empirical loss
\mathcal{L}(\bm{M})=\frac{1}{2m}\|\bm{y}-\mathcal{A}(\bm{X})\|^{2}
, we define the population version as
\bar{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{X})=\frac{1}{2m}\|\mathcal{A}(\bm{X})-\mathcal{A}(\bm{X}^{*})\|^{2}
where
\bm{X}^{*}=\bm{U}^{*}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}
. Therefore, for any
(\bm{U},\bm{V})
,
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal}-\bm{X}^{*})/m
where
\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A}(\bm{H})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\langle\mathcal{A}_{i},\bm{H}\rangle\bm{A}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times q}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=-m^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\xi_{i}\bm{A}_{i}
with
\mathcal{A}^{*}(\cdot)
being the self-adjoint operator of
\mathcal{A}(\cdot)
.

Verification of Assumption
3
.
Because
\nabla_{\bm{X}}^{2}\mathcal{L}(\bm{X})[\bm{H}_{1},\bm{H}_{2}]=m^{-1}\langle\mathcal{A}(\bm{H}_{1}),\mathcal{A}(\bm{H}_{2})\rangle
, Assumption
3
is readily verified by (
A.1
) and
\|\bm{H}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1
, with
\alpha=1-\delta_{0}

\beta=1+\delta_{0}
, and
\epsilon=\infty
(
\mathcal{D}=\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}\times\mathbb{R}^{q\times r}
).

Verification of Assumption
4
.
For any
\bm{H}:=\bm{U}_{1}\bm{V}_{1}^{\intercal}-\bm{U}_{2}\bm{V}_{2}^{\intercal}
with
\bm{U}_{1},\bm{U}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
and
\bm{V}_{1},\bm{V}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times r}
, because
\mathrm{rank}(\bm{H})\leq 2r
, we know

\displaystyle\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})-\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\|\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A}(\bm{H})\|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|\bm{a}\|_{2}=\|\bm{b}\|_{2}=1}\frac{1}{m}\big|\bm{a}^{\intercal}\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A}(\bm{H})\bm{b}\big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|\bm{a}\|_{2}=\|\bm{b}\|_{2}=1}\frac{1}{m}\big|\langle\bm{a}\bm{b}^{\intercal},\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A}(\bm{H})\rangle\big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|\bm{a}\|_{2}=\|\bm{b}\|_{2}=1}\frac{1}{m}\big|\langle\mathcal{A}(\bm{a}\bm{b}^{\intercal}),\mathcal{A}(\bm{H})\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\|\bm{a}\|_{2}=\|\bm{b}\|_{2}=1}\frac{1}{m}\|\mathcal{A}(\bm{a}\bm{b}^{\intercal})\|_{2}\,\|\mathcal{A}(\bm{H})\|_{2}.

Here, the first equality follows from that
\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A}(\cdot)
is linear operator. Now
\mathrm{rank}(\bm{a}\bm{b}^{\intercal})=1\leq 2r
and
\|\bm{a}\bm{b}^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1
, so (
A.1
) yields
\|\mathcal{A}(\bm{a}\bm{b}^{\intercal})\|\leq\sqrt{1+\delta_{0}}
. Similarly, because
\mathrm{rank}(\bm{H})\leq 2r
,
\|\mathcal{A}(\bm{H})\|\leq\sqrt{1+\delta_{0}}\,\|\bm{H}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
. Combining these, we conclude that
Recalling the definition of
\bm{H}
, we obtain

\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})-\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})\|\leq(1+\delta_{0})\|\bm{U}_{1}\bm{V}_{1}^{\intercal}-\bm{U}_{2}\bm{V}_{2}^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 2\|\bm{U}_{1}\bm{V}_{1}^{\intercal}-\bm{U}_{2}\bm{V}_{2}^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

That is, Assumption
4
holds for
L_{2}=2
and
\epsilon=\infty
(
\mathcal{D}=\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}\times\mathbb{R}^{q\times r}
.

Verification of noise condition for
\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)
in (
21a
).
Matrix Bernstein inequality (Theorem 4.1.1 in
44
) for sums of independent mean-zero sub-exponential rectangular matrices gives an event
\mathcal{E}
with
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-\delta
such that on
\mathcal{E}
,

(nq)^{-1/2}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\|=\frac{1}{m}(nq)^{-1/2}\|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\xi_{i}\bm{A}_{i}\|\leq C\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{A}\log((n+q)/\delta)}{m^{2}nq}}

where
\nu_{A}:=\max\big\{\|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bm{A}_{i}\bm{A}_{i}^{\intercal}\|,\|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bm{A}_{i}^{\intercal}\bm{A}_{i}\|\big\}
. With (
A.1
), one can check that
\nu_{A}\leq m(1+\delta_{0})(n\vee q)
. Consequently, for (
21a
), one may take

\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)\asymp\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{(1+\delta_{0})\log((n+q)/\delta)}{m(n\wedge q)}}.

The theorem’s scaling condition implies that
\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)\leq c_{0}(1-\delta_{0})^{2}\sigma_{\min}/(\kappa\sqrt{r})
as required by Theorem
4
.
Thus, we have verified every condition required for the
\ell_{2}
error contraction part of Theorem
4
. This proves Theorem
A.5
.

E.3

Proof of Theorem
A.6

In this proof, we first verify the conditions required by Theorem
4
except that on
\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)
. Next, we show how to establish the result in Theorem
4
under the Bernoulli low-rank model with a leave-one-out argument that remedies that
\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)
cannot be properly bounded. Define the scaled logistic loss
\ell_{\alpha_{0}}(x;y):=\nu_{\star}\{\log(1+\exp(\alpha_{0}+x))-y(\alpha_{0}+x)\}
. Recall
\sigma(x)=\exp(\alpha_{0}+x)/\{\exp(\alpha_{0}+x)+1\}
and then we note that the derivatives of
\ell_{\alpha_{0}}
can be written as

s(x)=\partial_{x}\ell_{\alpha_{0}}(x;y)=\nu_{\star}\{\sigma(x)-y\},\qquad w(x)=\partial_{x}^{2}\ell_{\alpha_{0}}(x;y)=\nu_{\star}\sigma(x)\{1-\sigma(x)\}

Recall that
\nu_{\star}=e^{-(\alpha_{0}+M_{2})}
. Obviously,
P_{ij}^{*}\leq e^{\alpha_{0}+M_{2}}\leq\nu_{\star}^{-1}
uniformly across
i\in[n]
and
j\in[q]
.

Verifying of conditions of Theorem
4
.

We first prove the following assertions.

(a)

Recall that
\alpha=\frac{1}{4}e^{-M_{\star}}
,
\beta=1
. For any
(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
and any
\bm{H}
of the form
\mathcal{P}_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})}(\bm{R},\bm{L})=\bm{U}\bm{R}^{\intercal}+\bm{L}\bm{V}^{\intercal}
, we have

\alpha\|\bm{H}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\nabla_{\bm{X}}^{2}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})[\bm{H},\bm{H}]\leq\beta\|\bm{H}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

which verifies Assumption
3
under the Bernoulli model.

(b)

Since the Hessian is diagonal entrywise, Assumption
B.7
holds with the same
\beta
.

(c)

We have
\mathcal{G}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\nabla_{\bm{X}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})=\nu_{\star}\{\sigma\{(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})_{ij}\}-Y_{ij}\}_{n\times q}
For
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\mathbb{E}\mathcal{G}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\nu_{\star}\{\sigma(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})-\sigma(\bm{U}^{*}\bm{V}^{*}{}^{\intercal})\}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})-\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})\|\leq\|\bm{U}_{1}\bm{V}_{1}^{\intercal}-\bm{U}_{2}\bm{V}_{2}^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}},

\displaystyle\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})-\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|\bm{U}_{1}\bm{V}_{1}^{\intercal}-\bm{U}_{2}\bm{V}_{2}^{\intercal}\|_{2\to\infty},

\displaystyle\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})^{\intercal}-\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})^{\intercal}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|\bm{V}_{1}\bm{U}_{1}^{\intercal}-\bm{V}_{2}\bm{U}_{2}^{\intercal}\|_{2\to\infty},

which verifies Assumption
B.8
with
\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
under the place of
\mathcal{G}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
under the Bernoulli model.

(d)

Writing
\bm{P}^{*}=\sigma(\bm{X}^{*})
, we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\mathcal{G}(\bm{U},\bm{V})-\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\nu_{\star}(\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y}),

which is independent of
(\bm{U},\bm{V})
. Therefore,
\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)
,
\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)
, and
\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)
in (
21
) does not depend on the region
\mathcal{D}
. For (
21a
), we can take

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)\asymp\nu_{\star}\sqrt{\frac{e^{\alpha_{0}+M_{2}}}{n\wedge q}+\frac{\log((n+q)/\delta)}{nq}}.

(e)

With
R_{\star}>1
, for (
21b
), we can take
\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\asymp\sqrt{\nu_{\star}L_{\star}R_{\star}/(n\wedge q)}
.

(f)

Let
\bar{v}_{\infty}=\max\left\{\max_{i\in[n]}q^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{q}2P_{ij}^{*}(1-P_{ij}^{*}),\max_{j\in[q]}n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2P_{ij}^{*}(1-P_{ij}^{*})\right\}\leq\frac{1}{2}\wedge 2e^{M_{2}}
. For (
21c
), we can only take

\displaystyle\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\asymp\nu_{\star}\bar{v}_{\infty}+C\nu_{\star}\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\log(n/\delta)}{q}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\log(q/\delta)}{n}}\right\}.

Subsequently, we have

\frac{\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\leq\frac{4e^{M_{1}+M_{2}+\alpha_{0}}}{\sigma_{\min}}

For
(a)
, we note that for any
(\bm{R},\bm{L})\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times r}\times\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
, we have

\nabla_{\bm{X}}^{2}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})[\bm{H},\bm{H}]=e^{-\alpha_{0}-M_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sigma\big((\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})_{ij}\big)\big\{1-\sigma\big((\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})_{ij}\big)\big\}\,H_{ij}^{2}.

Note that
\inf_{x\in[-M_{1},M_{2}]}\sigma(x)\{1-\sigma(x)\}=\frac{e^{\alpha_{0}-M_{1}}}{(1+e^{\alpha_{0}-M_{1}})^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{2}e^{\alpha_{0}-M_{1}}
and
\sup_{x\in[-M_{1},M_{2}]}\sigma(x)\{1-\sigma(x)\}\leq\nu_{\star}^{-1}
, which proves
(a)
.

For
(b)
, fix
(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
,
i\in[n]
,
\bm{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
, and let
\bm{L}_{i}=\bm{e}_{i}\bm{h}^{\intercal}
. Then
\mathcal{P}_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})}({\bm{0}},\bm{L}_{i})=\bm{L}_{i}\bm{V}^{\intercal}=\bm{e}_{i}(\bm{V}\bm{h})^{\intercal}
, which is supported only on row
i
.
Now let
(\bm{L},\bm{R})\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}\times\mathbb{R}^{q\times r}
with
\bm{L}_{-i}
having zero
i
th row, and define
\bm{H}_{1}:=\mathcal{P}_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})}({\bm{0}},\bm{L}_{i})=\bm{e}_{i}(\bm{V}\bm{h})^{\intercal}
,
\bm{H}_{2}:=\mathcal{P}_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})}(\bm{R},\bm{L}_{-i})=\bm{U}\bm{R}^{\intercal}+\bm{L}_{-i}\bm{V}^{\intercal}
.
Since
\bm{L}_{-i}
has zero
i
th row, the
i
th row of
\bm{H}_{2}
is
(\bm{H}_{2})_{i,\cdot}=\bm{U}_{i,\cdot}\bm{R}^{\intercal}
. One can then verify

\nabla_{\bm{X}}^{2}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})[\bm{H}_{1},\bm{H}_{2}]=\sum_{j=1}^{q}w\big((\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})_{ij}\big)\,(\bm{V}\bm{h})_{j}\,(\bm{U}_{i,\cdot}\bm{R}^{\intercal})_{j}.

By
(a)
, we know
w(\cdot)\leq 1
. Therefore, by Cauchy–Schwarz,
\big|\nabla_{\bm{X}}^{2}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})[\bm{H}_{1},\bm{H}_{2}]\big|\leq\|\bm{V}\bm{h}\|\,\|\bm{U}_{i,\cdot}\bm{R}^{\intercal}\|
.
This proves the first inequality in Assumption
B.7
.
The second part is identical by symmetry.

For
(c)
, let
\bm{X}_{1}:=\bm{U}_{1}\bm{V}_{1}^{\intercal}
and
\bm{X}_{2}:=\bm{U}_{2}\bm{V}_{2}^{\intercal}
.
Since
w(x)\leq 1
in the considered local region, the mean value theorem gives

\sigma\big((\bm{X}_{1})_{ij}\big)-\sigma\big((\bm{X}_{2})_{ij}\big)=\sigma^{\prime}(\xi_{ij})\big\{(\bm{X}_{1})_{ij}-(\bm{X}_{2})_{ij}\big\}\leq e^{\alpha_{0}+M_{2}}\big\{(\bm{X}_{1})_{ij}-(\bm{X}_{2})_{ij}\big\}

Then

\|\sigma(\bm{X}_{1})-\sigma(\bm{X}_{2})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq e^{2\alpha_{0}+2M_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\big\{(\bm{X}_{1}-\bm{X}_{2})_{ij}\big\}^{2}=e^{2\alpha_{0}+2M_{2}}\|\bm{X}_{1}-\bm{X}_{2}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Therefore,
\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})-\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})\|\leq\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})-\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\bm{X}_{1}-\bm{X}_{2}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
. Similarly, one can verify the row-wise Lipschitz conditions.

For
(d)
, recall that
\bm{P}^{*}=\sigma(\bm{X}^{*})
. Then
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\mathcal{G}(\bm{U},\bm{V})-\bar{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=e^{-\alpha_{0}-M_{2}}(\bm{P}-\bm{Y})
, which is independent of
(\bm{U},\bm{V})
. To bound
\bm{P}-\bm{Y}
, define the symmetric dilation

\mathcal{A}=\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{0}}&\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*}\\
(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})^{\intercal}&{\bm{0}}\end{pmatrix}.

We introduce the following lemma

Lemma 12

(Theorem 5.2 in
29
)
.

Let
\bm{Y}=(Y_{ij})\in\{0,1\}^{n\times q}
have independent entries with
Y_{ij}\sim\mathrm{Bernoulli}(P_{ij}^{*}),
for
i\in[n]
and
j\in[q]
. Assume that
(n+q)\max_{i\in[n],\,j\in[q]}P_{ij}^{*}\leq d
. Then for any
C_{0}>0
, there exists a constant
C=C(C_{0})>0
such that

\|\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*}\|\leq C\sqrt{d+\log(n+q)}

with probability at least
1-(n+q)^{-C_{0}}
.

Applying the lemma with
\|\mathcal{A}\|=\|\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*}\|
and
\max\Big\{\max_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{q}P_{ij}^{*},\ \max_{j}\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{ij}^{*}\Big\}\leq(n\vee q)e^{M_{2}+\alpha_{0}}
, we obtain
(d)
.

For
(e)
, fix
i\in[n]
and
\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{r-1}
. Write
a_{j}=\langle\bm{V}^{*}_{j,\cdot},\bm{u}\rangle
and
S_{i,\bm{u}}:=\sum_{j=1}^{q}(Y_{ij}-P_{ij}^{*})a_{j}
. The variables
(Y_{ij}-P_{ij}^{*})a_{j}
are independent and centered. Moreover,

\sum_{j=1}^{q}{\rm Var}\{(Y_{ij}-P_{ij}^{*})a_{j}\}=\sum_{j=1}^{q}P_{ij}^{*}(1-P_{ij}^{*})a_{j}^{2}\leq(\nu_{\star})^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{q}a_{j}^{2}\leq\|\bm{V}^{*}\|^{2}/\nu_{\star}.

Also, we know
\max_{j\in[q]}|a_{j}|\leq\|\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}
. Therefore, Bernstein’s inequality gives, for any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(|S_{i,\bm{u}}|\geq C\{\|\bm{V}^{*}\|\sqrt{t/\nu_{\star}}+\|\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}t\}\right)\leq 2e^{-t}.

Let
\mathcal{N}
be a
1/2
-net of
\mathbb{S}^{r-1}
with
|\mathcal{N}|\leq 5^{r}
.
Taking
t=C\{r+\log(n/\delta)\}
and applying a union bound over
\bm{u}\in\mathcal{N}
and
i\in[n]
, together with the standard net inequality,
yields

\|(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq C\left\{\|\bm{V}^{*}\|\sqrt{\nu_{\star}^{-1}\{r+\log(n/\delta)\}}+\|\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\{r+\log(n/\delta)\}\right\}.

Multiplying by the loss scaling
\nu_{\star}
and dividing by

\sqrt{q}\,\|\bm{V}^{*}\|
gives

\frac{\nu_{\star}\|(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}}{\sqrt{q}\,\|\bm{V}^{*}\|}\leq C\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}\{r+\log(n/\delta)\}}{q}}+\nu_{\star}\frac{\|\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}}{\|\bm{V}^{*}\|}\frac{r+\log(n/\delta)}{\sqrt{q}}\right\}.

The bound for
(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}
follows in the same way. With
R_{\star}>1
, we finish the proof.

For
(f)
, because
Z_{ij}:=|Y_{ij}-P_{ij}^{*}|
are independent and
\mathbb{E}Z_{ij}=2P_{ij}^{*}(1-P_{ij}^{*})
, we know by Hoeffding’ inequality that

\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1}{q}\sum_{j=1}^{q}Z_{ij}\geq\frac{1}{q}\sum_{j=1}^{q}2P_{ij}^{*}(1-P_{ij}^{*})+t\Big)\leq e^{-2qt^{2}}.

Union bound over
i\in[n]
yields that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

q^{-1}\|\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*}\|_{\infty\to 1}\leq\max_{i\in[n]}\frac{1}{q}\sum_{j=1}^{q}2P_{ij}^{*}(1-P_{ij}^{*})+C\sqrt{\frac{\log(n/\delta)}{q}}.

For
n^{-1}\|(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\infty\to 1}
, we can obtain a similar result. Combine these two, and we prove
(f)
.

Leave-one-out analysis.

The above analysis shows that
\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)
is comparably large in the considered setup. In this part, we show how the leave-one-out analysis can circumvent this issue. In the proof of Theorem
4
, the upper bound for
\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)
is used in Step 1 to bound
\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{4,i}^{(U)}\|\vee\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\Delta_{4,\ell}^{(V)}\|
in (
D.41
), and in Step 3 to bound
\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger}
in (
D.76
). In what follows, we show that both steps can instead be established without invoking
\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)
.

For
\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{4,i}^{(U)}\|\vee\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\Delta_{4,\ell}^{(V)}\|
, note that for the Bernoulli response model,
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\nu_{\star}(\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y})
is independent of
(\bm{U},\bm{V})
, and thus
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{i\ell}=\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{G}_{i\ell}ds=\nu_{\star}(P^{*}_{i\ell}-Y_{i\ell})
. Hence
\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{4,i}^{(U)}\|=\max_{i\in[n]}\big\|\nu_{\star}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}(P^{*}_{i\ell}-Y_{i\ell})\bm{E}_{V,\ell}^{\intercal}\big\|=\nu_{\star}\big\|(\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y})\bm{E}_{V}\big\|_{2\to\infty}
and
\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\Delta_{4,\ell}^{(V)}\|=\big\|\nu_{\star}(\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y})^{\intercal}\bm{E}_{U}\big\|_{2\to\infty}
. To control these quantities, we introduce an auxiliary point
(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})
, which plays the role of a leave-one-out proxy. As shown in the next lemma,
(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})
is very close to
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
in weighted Frobenius norm, while each row
\bar{\bm{U}}_{i,}
depends only weakly on
\{Y_{i\ell}\}_{\ell\in[q]}
, and each row
\bar{\bm{V}}_{j,}
depends only weakly on
\{Y_{ij}\}_{i\in[n]}
, yielding a sharp control on
q^{-1}\|(\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y})(\bar{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2\to\infty}\vee n^{-1}\|(\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y})^{\intercal}(\bar{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*})\|_{2\to\infty}
. Write
\ell_{\alpha_{0}}^{\prime}(x)=\partial_{x}\ell_{\alpha_{0}}(x;Y_{ij})
and
\ell_{\alpha_{0}}^{\prime\prime}(x)=\partial_{x}^{2}\ell_{\alpha_{0}}(x;Y_{ij})
for short when
Y_{ij}
is clear from the context.

Lemma 13
.

Assume the conditions of the Bernoulli response model in Example 2, and suppose the event in
Theorem
4
holds. Construct
(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})
as follows:

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{U}}_{i,}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}-\Big\{\sum_{j\in[q]}\ell_{\alpha_{0}}^{\prime\prime}(X_{ij}^{*})(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{V}_{j,}^{*}\Big\}^{-1}\sum_{j\in[q]}\ell_{\alpha_{0}}^{\prime}(X_{ij}^{*})(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{\intercal},\qquad i\in[n],

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{V}}_{j,}

\displaystyle=\bm{V}_{j,}^{*}-\Big\{\sum_{i\in[n]}\ell_{\alpha_{0}}^{\prime\prime}(X_{ij}^{*})(\bm{U}_{i,}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\Big\}^{-1}\sum_{i\in[n]}\ell_{\alpha_{0}}^{\prime}(X_{ij}^{*})(\bm{U}_{i,}^{*})^{\intercal},\qquad j\in[q].

Let
R_{\bar{Z}}:=\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})\big\|_{2,\infty}
.
Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the following hold:

1.

The score at
(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})
is bounded by

R_{\bar{Z}}\leq C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\omega_{*}.

(E.1)

2.

The auxiliary point is close to
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
in weighted Frobenius norm:

\|(\bar{\bm{U}}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq C\,\frac{R_{\bar{Z}}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}.

(E.2)

3.

The quadratic noise terms satisfy

\displaystyle\nu_{\star}\Big(\frac{\|(\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y})(\bar{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2\to\infty}}{q}\vee\frac{\|(\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y})^{\intercal}(\bar{\bm{U}}-\bm{U}^{*})\|_{2\to\infty}}{n}\Big)

(E.3)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big\{\nu_{\star}L_{\star}\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{\alpha nq\sigma_{\min}}}+\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta\,\omega_{*}}{\alpha(n\wedge q)\sigma_{\min}}+\frac{\nu_{\star}^{2}L_{\star}\,\omega_{*}}{\alpha nq\sigma_{\min}}\Big\}\leq C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\omega_{*}.

Proof.

See Section
F.6
.
∎

With this lemma, we write
\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{4,i}^{(U)}\|
as

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\|\Delta_{4,i}^{(U)}\|=

\displaystyle\,\nu_{\star}\big\|(\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y})\bm{E}_{V}\big\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\nu_{\star}\big\|(\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y})(\bar{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*})\big\|_{2\to\infty}+\nu_{\star}\big\|\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y}\big\|\big\|\bar{\bm{V}}-\widetilde{\bm{V}}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\omega_{*},

where the second inequality follows from parts 2 and 3 of
Lemma
13
, and the last inequality follows from the scaling conditions.
By symmetry, the same bound holds for
\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\Delta_{4,\ell}^{(V)}\|
.
Therefore, (
D.41
) can be similarly established.

Consequently, the minimizer
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
lies in the interior of
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)
