. The remainder of Step 1, namely the proof that
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
is an interior stationary point of
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\cdot)
, now proceeds exactly as in the proof of Theorem
4
, since it only uses (
D.34
), (
D.47
), and the already verified curvature and Lipschitz conditions.
Likewise, Step 2 can be repeated, because once the conclusions of Step 1 are available, the subsequent argument only invokes the same local curvature, Lipschitz, and noise bounds, all of which have already been verified for Example 2 above.

It remains to sharpen the row-wise analysis in Step 3 by deriving a bound for
\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger}
as in (
D.76
). Our idea is to invoke the LOO initialization and construct a LOO sequence along with
(\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t})
such that the sequence is close to
(\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t})
and has tractable dependence with respect to the noise matrix
\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*}
. We summarize the result in Lemma
14
.

Lemma 14
.

Under the setting of Theorem
4
, let
\widetilde{\bm{E}}^{t}_{V}
and
\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t}
be defined as in (
D.63
). On an event with probability at least
1-\delta
, uniformly for all integers
0\leq t\leq T_{\star}
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger,U}\vee\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger,V}=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta\nu_{\star}}{q}\|(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t}\|_{2\to\infty}\vee\frac{\eta\nu_{\star}}{n}\|(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})^{\intercal}\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t}\|_{2\to\infty}

(E.4)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta\big\{\frac{1}{8}\alpha\sigma_{\min}\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}+C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\big\}\omega_{*}.

Consequently, the induction hypotheses in Step 3 of the proof of
Theorem
4
is verified at time
t+1
.

Proof.

See Section
F.7
.
∎

The bound (
E.4
) replaces the bound for
\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)
in (
D.76
). The contraction part is absorbed exactly as in Step 3, while the non-contracting part contributes the final statistical error
C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\omega_{*}/(\alpha\sigma_{\min})
. Hence, the result of Theorem
4
applies to the Bernoulli low-rank model without assuming
\bar{\Delta}_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\leq\alpha\sigma_{\min}/4
.

Appendix F

Proof of Technical Lemmas

F.1

Proof of Lemma
1
and Lemma
2

Throughout the proof, we write the objective as
h_{\alpha}^{*}(\cdot)
. In what follows, we prove Lemma
2
only, and Lemma
1
can be immediately obtained with
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{z}^{(2)}(\epsilon)
, Weyl’s inequality, and

\|\mathcal{G}(\bm{Z})\|\leq L_{2}\|\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal}-\bm{Z}^{*}(\bm{Z}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq L_{2}(2\epsilon+\epsilon^{2})\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 4L_{2}\epsilon\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

by Assumption
2
.
For any
\bm{Z}\in\mathcal{D}
and any direction
\bm{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
, the Hessian quadratic form for
h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z})
is:

\nabla^{2}_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z})[\bm{W},\bm{W}]=\nabla^{2}_{\bm{Z}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal})[\bm{W},\bm{W}]+\nabla^{2}_{\bm{Z}}p^{*}_{\alpha}(\bm{Z})[\bm{W},\bm{W}].

For
\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}\mathcal{L}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal})
, one can check
\big\{\mathrm{vec}(\bm{W}^{\intercal})\big\}^{\intercal}\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}\mathcal{L}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal})\mathrm{vec}(\bm{W}^{\intercal})=\nabla^{2}_{\bm{Z}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal})[\bm{W},\bm{W}]
. Using the matrix chain rule, there is

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\bm{Z}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal})[\bm{W},\bm{W}]=\nabla^{2}_{\bm{X}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal})[\mathcal{P}_{\bm{Z}}(\bm{W}),\mathcal{P}_{\bm{Z}}(\bm{W})]+2\langle\nabla_{\bm{X}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal}),\bm{W}\bm{W}^{\intercal}\rangle,

where
\mathcal{P}_{\bm{Z}}(\bm{W})=\bm{Z}\bm{W}^{\intercal}+\bm{W}\bm{Z}^{\intercal}
.
With
\mathcal{G}(\bm{Z})=\nabla_{\bm{X}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal})
, Assumption
1
then yields:

\nabla^{2}_{\bm{Z}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal})[\bm{W},\bm{W}]-2\langle\mathcal{G}(\bm{Z}),\bm{W}\bm{W}^{\intercal}\rangle\geq\alpha\|\mathcal{P}_{\bm{Z}}(\bm{W})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

With the decomposition
\mathcal{P}_{\bm{Z}}(\bm{W})=\mathcal{P}_{\bm{Z}^{*}}(\bm{W})+\mathcal{P}_{\bm{Z}-\bm{Z}^{*}}(\bm{W})
and the inequality
\|\bm{A}+\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
, we arrive at

\|\mathcal{P}_{\bm{Z}}(\bm{W})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\|\mathcal{P}_{\bm{Z}^{*}}(\bm{W})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\|\mathcal{P}_{\bm{Z}-\bm{Z}^{*}}(\bm{W})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

By noting that
\|\mathcal{P}_{\bm{Z}^{*}}(\bm{W})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=2|\bm{W}\bm{Z}^{*{\intercal}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big\{(\bm{W}^{\intercal}\bm{Z}^{*})^{2}
and
\|\mathcal{P}_{\bm{Z}-\bm{Z}^{*}}(\bm{W})\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\|(\bm{Z}-\bm{Z}^{*})\bm{W}^{\intercal}+\bm{W}(\bm{Z}-\bm{Z}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 2\|\bm{Z}-\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{W}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\bm{Z}}\mathcal{L}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\intercal})[\bm{W},\bm{W}]\geq\alpha\left[\|\bm{Z}^{*}\bm{W}^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\{(\bm{W}^{\intercal}\bm{Z}^{*})^{2}\}\right]-4\alpha\|\bm{W}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\|\bm{Z}-\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(F.1)

Next, for
p_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z})
, one can compute that the second-order derivative of the regularization term
p^{*}_{\alpha}(\bm{Z})
in direction
\bm{W}
is:

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\bm{Z}}p^{*}(\bm{Z})[\bm{W},\bm{W}]=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\alpha}{2}\|(\bm{Z}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{W}-\bm{W}^{\intercal}\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\alpha\left\{\|\bm{W}^{\intercal}\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\bm{W}^{\intercal}\bm{Z}^{*})^{2})\right\}.

Adding this to (
F.1
), the trace terms cancel, and we get

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\bm{Z}}h^{*}_{\alpha}(\bm{Z})[\bm{W},\bm{W}]-2\langle\mathcal{G}(\bm{Z}),\bm{W}\bm{W}^{\intercal}\rangle\geq

\displaystyle\,\alpha\|\bm{W}(\bm{Z}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\alpha\|\bm{W}^{\intercal}\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\,-4\|\bm{W}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\|\bm{Z}-\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\,\left\{\alpha\sigma_{r}(\bm{Z}^{*})^{2}-4\|\bm{Z}-\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right\}\|\bm{W}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

The last inequality follows from
\|\bm{W}(\bm{Z}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\{\bm{W}^{\intercal}\bm{W}\bm{Z}^{*}(\bm{Z}^{*})^{\intercal}\}\geq\sigma_{r}(\bm{Z}^{*})^{2}\|\bm{W}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
.

Now let
\bm{z}=\mathrm{vec}(\bm{Z}^{\intercal})
. Using
\langle\mathcal{G}(\bm{Z}),\bm{W}\bm{W}^{\intercal}\rangle=\big\{\mathrm{vec}(\bm{W}^{\intercal})\big\}^{\intercal}\big(\mathcal{G}(\bm{Z})\otimes\bm{I}_{r}\big)\mathrm{vec}(\bm{W}^{\intercal})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\big\{n^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z})\!-\!2n^{-1}\mathcal{G}(\bm{Z})\otimes\bm{I}_{r}\big\}\!=\!\min_{\|\bm{W}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=1}\big(\nabla_{\bm{Z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z})[\bm{W},\bm{W}]\!-\!2\langle\mathcal{G}(\bm{Z}),\bm{W}\bm{W}^{\intercal}\rangle\big)/n.

Taking the minimum over
\bm{Z}\in\mathcal{D}
completes the proof.

F.2

Proof of Lemma
7

First, we note that with Lemma
2
and
\|\widetilde{\bm{Z}}-\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\epsilon\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|
,

\lambda_{\min}\Big\{n^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z})-2n^{-1}\mathcal{G}(\bm{Z})\otimes\bm{I}_{r}\Big\}\geq\alpha\sigma_{\min}-4n^{-1}\epsilon^{2}\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Then with
\|\bm{Z}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq nr\kappa\sigma_{\min}
,
\epsilon^{2}r\kappa\leq c_{0}\alpha
for sufficiently small
c_{0}
, we know

\lambda_{\min}\Big\{n^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z})-2n^{-1}\mathcal{G}(\bm{Z})\otimes\bm{I}_{r}\Big\}\geq 3\alpha\sigma_{\min}/4.

In bounding
\gamma_{2}
in the proof of Theorem
3
, we know

\displaystyle n^{-1}\|\mathcal{G}(\bm{Z})\|\leq\Delta_{2}(n,\delta)+(2+\epsilon)\epsilon C\sqrt{r}\kappa\sigma_{\min}.

With
\epsilon\sqrt{r}\kappa\leq c_{0}\alpha
and
\Delta_{2}(n,\delta)/(\alpha\sigma_{\min})\leq c_{0}
for sufficiently small, we concluded that, for any
\bm{Z}\in\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon}
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\bigl(n^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z})\bigr)\geq

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{\min}\Big\{n^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z})-2n^{-1}\mathcal{G}(\bm{Z})\otimes\bm{I}_{r}\Big\}-2n^{-1}\|\mathcal{G}(\bm{Z})\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\alpha\sigma_{\min}>0.

Since
\widetilde{\bm{Z}}
is a strict interior point of
\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon}
, it is a local minimizer to
\|\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z})\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}
.
Suppose, for contradiction, that
\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}})\neq{\bm{0}}
. Let

\widetilde{\bm{z}}:=\mathrm{vec}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}}^{\intercal}),\qquad\bm{v}:=\mathrm{vec}\bigl(\{\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}})\}^{\intercal}\bigr),\qquad\bm{H}:=\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}}).

Then
\bm{v}\neq{\bm{0}}
and
\bm{H}\succ 0
. Define
\bm{d}:=-\bm{H}^{-1}\bm{v}
and let
\bm{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
be the matrix satisfying
\mathrm{vec}(\bm{D}^{\intercal})=\bm{d}
.
By differentiability of
\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\cdot)
,

\mathrm{vec}\Bigl(\bigl\{\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}}+s\bm{D})\bigr\}^{\intercal}\Bigr)=\bm{v}+s\bm{H}\bm{d}+o(s)=(1-s)\bm{v}+o(s),\text{ as }s\to 0.

Equivalently,

\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}}+t\bm{D})=(1-t)\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}})+\bm{R}_{t},\qquad\|\bm{R}_{t}\|_{2\to\infty}=o(t).

Consequently, for sufficiently small
t>0
, there is

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}}+t\bm{D})\|_{2\to\infty}<

\displaystyle\,(1-t)\|\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}})\|_{2\to\infty}+\|\bm{R}_{t}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,(1-t)\|\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}})\|_{2\to\infty}+o(t)<\|\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\widetilde{\bm{Z}})\|_{2\to\infty},

This contradicts the fact that
\widetilde{\bm{Z}}
is a local minimizer of
\|\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{Z})\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}
. This completes the proof.

F.3

Proof of Lemma
8

Fix any
(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}
, and let
(\bm{L},\bm{R})\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}\times\mathbb{R}^{q\times r}
be arbitrary. Let
\bm{w}:=\mathrm{vec}\big((\bm{L}^{\intercal},\bm{R}^{\intercal})\big)
, and define

\bar{\bm{L}}:=q^{-1/2}\bm{L},\qquad\bar{\bm{R}}:=n^{-1/2}\bm{R}.

Then, by construction,
\bm{S}_{Z}\bm{w}=\mathrm{vec}\big((\bar{\bm{L}}^{\intercal},\bar{\bm{R}}^{\intercal})\big)
, where by abuse of notation

\bm{S}_{Z}
stands for
\bm{S}_{Z}\otimes\bm{I}_{r}
when acting on
\bm{z}
.

By the chain rule,

\displaystyle\bm{w}^{\intercal}\bm{S}_{z}\big\{\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})-\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big\}\bm{S}_{z}\bm{w}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\bm{X}}^{2}\mathcal{L}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})\big[\bm{U}\bar{\bm{R}}^{\intercal}+\bar{\bm{L}}\bm{V}^{\intercal},\,\bm{U}\bar{\bm{R}}^{\intercal}+\bar{\bm{L}}\bm{V}^{\intercal}\big]

(F.2)

\displaystyle+\nabla_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})}^{2}p_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big[(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}}),(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}})\big].

Here
\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
is the block matrix collecting the first-order terms produced by the nonlinear map
(\bm{U},\bm{V})\mapsto\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal}
.

Since the map inside
p_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
is linear in
(\bm{U},\bm{V})
, one can check

\displaystyle\nabla_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})}^{2}p_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big[(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}}),(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}})\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha nq}{2}\left\|n^{-1}(\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bar{\bm{L}}-q^{-1}\bar{\bm{R}}^{\intercal}\bm{V}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(F.3)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{2}\left\|n^{-1/2}\bm{L}^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}-q^{-1/2}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Hence, plugging (
F.3
) into (
F.2
), and with Assumption
3
, we have

\displaystyle\bm{w}^{\intercal}\bm{S}_{z}\big\{\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})-\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big\}\bm{S}_{z}\bm{w}\geq

\displaystyle\alpha\|\bm{U}\bar{\bm{R}}^{\intercal}+\bar{\bm{L}}\bm{V}^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(F.4)

\displaystyle+\frac{\alpha}{2}\left\|n^{-1/2}\bm{L}^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}-q^{-1/2}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Now decompose

\bm{U}\bar{\bm{R}}^{\intercal}+\bar{\bm{L}}\bm{V}^{\intercal}=\underbrace{n^{-1/2}\bm{U}^{*}\bm{R}^{\intercal}+q^{-1/2}\bm{L}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}}_{=:\bm{A}}+\underbrace{n^{-1/2}(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*})\bm{R}^{\intercal}+q^{-1/2}\bm{L}(\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}}_{=:\bm{B}}.

Using
\|\bm{A}+\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
, we obtain from (
F.4
) that

\displaystyle\bm{w}^{\intercal}\bm{S}_{z}\big\{\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})-\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big\}\bm{S}_{z}\bm{w}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\alpha\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(F.5)

\displaystyle+\frac{\alpha}{2}\left\|n^{-1/2}\bm{L}^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}-q^{-1/2}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

We now combine the first and third terms. By direct expansion, one can obtain

\displaystyle\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\left\|n^{-1/2}\bm{U}^{*}\bm{R}^{\intercal}+q^{-1/2}\bm{L}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,n^{-1}\|\bm{U}^{*}\bm{R}^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+q^{-1}\|\bm{L}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{nq}}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\!\big(\bm{L}^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\bm{R}^{\intercal}\bm{V}^{*}\big),

and

\displaystyle\big\|n^{-1/2}\bm{L}^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}-q^{-1/2}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{R}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=n^{-1}\|\bm{L}^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+q^{-1}\|(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\!-\!\frac{2}{\sqrt{nq}}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\!\big(\bm{L}^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\bm{R}^{\intercal}\bm{V}^{*}\big).

The cross terms cancel, so

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|n^{-1/2}\bm{L}^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}-q^{-1/2}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}n^{-1}\|\bm{U}^{*}\bm{R}^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}q^{-1}\|\bm{L}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(F.6)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}n^{-1}\|\bm{L}^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}q^{-1}\|(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Using the balancing condition
n^{-1}(\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}=q^{-1}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{V}^{*}=\bm{\Sigma}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle n^{-1}\|\bm{U}^{*}\bm{R}^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}^{*}\bm{R}^{\intercal})\quad\text{ and }\quad q^{-1}\|\bm{L}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\bm{L}^{\intercal}\bm{L}\bm{\Sigma}^{*}).

Therefore (
F.6
) further simplifies to

\eqref{eq_rect_identity1}=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\bm{L}^{\intercal}\bm{L}\bm{\Sigma}^{*})+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}^{*}\bm{R}^{\intercal})\geq\sigma_{\min}\big(\|\bm{L}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big).

(F.7)

It remains to bound
\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
. By Cauchy Schwarz,

\displaystyle\|\bm{B}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+q^{-1/2}\|\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{L}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(F.8)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{n}+\frac{\|\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{q}\Big)^{1/2}\big(\|\bm{L}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big)^{1/2},

Substituting (
F.7
) and (
F.8
) into (
F.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle\bm{w}^{\intercal}\bm{S}_{z}\big\{\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})-\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big\}\bm{S}_{z}\bm{w}\geq

\displaystyle\,\alpha\sigma_{\min}\big(\|\bm{L}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\,-\Big(\frac{\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{n}+\frac{\|\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{q}\Big)\big(\|\bm{L}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big).

Since
\|\bm{w}\|^{2}=\|\bm{L}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
, taking the infimum over all
\|\bm{w}\|=1
and then
the minimum over
(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}
yields the results.

Now we study
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\cdot,\cdot)
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V}):=n^{-1}(\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}-q^{-1}\bm{V}^{\intercal}\bm{V}^{*}
, and
\mathcal{A}_{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V}):=n^{-1}(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}-q^{-1}\bm{V}^{\intercal}(\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})
.
Then one has

p_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\frac{\alpha nq}{4}\|\mathcal{A}_{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},\qquad p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\frac{\alpha nq}{4}\|\mathcal{A}_{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Since
\mathcal{A}_{*}
is linear,
D\mathcal{A}_{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})[(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}})]=n^{-1}(\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bar{\bm{L}}-q^{-1}\bar{\bm{R}}^{\intercal}\bm{V}^{*}
and
D^{2}\mathcal{A}_{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})={\bm{0}}
. For
\mathcal{A}_{\natural}
, we note that

\displaystyle D\mathcal{A}_{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})[(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}})]=

\displaystyle\,n^{-1}(\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bar{\bm{L}}-q^{-1}\bar{\bm{R}}^{\intercal}\bm{V}^{*}\quad(:=\bm{\Delta}_{1})

\displaystyle\,+n^{-1}\bar{\bm{L}}^{\intercal}\bm{E}_{U}+n^{-1}\bm{E}_{U}^{\intercal}\bar{\bm{L}}-q^{-1}\bar{\bm{R}}^{\intercal}\bm{E}_{V}-q^{-1}\bm{E}_{V}^{\intercal}\bar{\bm{R}}\quad(:=\bm{\Delta}_{2}),

where
\bm{E}_{U}=\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*}
and
\bm{E}_{V}=\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*}
, and

D^{2}\mathcal{A}_{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})[(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}}),(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}})]=2n^{-1}\bar{\bm{L}}^{\intercal}\bar{\bm{L}}-2q^{-1}\bar{\bm{R}}^{\intercal}\bar{\bm{R}}.

Therefore with identity
D^{2}\big(\|\mathcal{A}(\bm{\theta})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big)[\bm{H},\bm{H}]=2\|D\mathcal{A}(\bm{\theta})[\bm{H}]\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\langle\mathcal{A}(\bm{\theta}),D^{2}\mathcal{A}(\bm{\theta})[\bm{H},\bm{H}]\rangle
for any mapping
\mathcal{A}(\bm{\theta})
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})}^{2}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big[(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}}),(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}})\big]-\nabla_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})}^{2}p_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big[(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}}),(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}})\big]

(F.9)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha nq}{2}\Big(\|D\mathcal{A}_{\natural}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\|D\mathcal{A}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big)+\frac{\alpha nq}{2}\Big\langle\mathcal{A}_{\natural},\,D^{2}\mathcal{A}_{\natural}[(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}}),(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}})]\Big\rangle.

We now bound the right hand side of (
F.9
). First, as
\|\bm{U}^{*}\|^{2}/n\leq\kappa\sigma_{\min}
and
\|\bm{V}^{*}\|^{2}/q\leq\kappa\sigma_{\min}
, one has

\sqrt{nq}\,\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\sqrt{nq}\,\|D\mathcal{A}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\big(\|\bm{L}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big)^{1/2}.

Similarly, with

d:=\frac{\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{n}+\frac{\|\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{q}\leq c_{0}\sigma_{\min}/\kappa

one can verify with Cauchy inequality that

\sqrt{nq}\,\|\bm{\Delta}_{2}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\sqrt{d}\,\big(\|\bm{L}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big)^{1/2}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{c_{0}\sigma_{\min}}{\kappa}}\big(\|\bm{L}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big)^{1/2}.

Hence with Cauchy inequality,

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha nq}{2}\Big|\|D\mathcal{A}_{\natural}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\|D\mathcal{A}_{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big|=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha nq}{2}\Big|\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}+\bm{\Delta}_{2}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big|

(F.10)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha nq}{2}\Big|2\mathrm{Tr}\big(\bm{\Delta}_{1}\bm{\Delta}_{2}^{\intercal}\big)+\|\bm{\Delta}_{2}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sqrt{c_{0}}\alpha\sigma_{\min}\big(\|\bm{L}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big)

Moreover, with
\mathcal{A}_{\natural}=n^{-1}\bm{E}_{U}^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}-q^{-1}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{E}_{V}+n^{-1}\bm{E}_{U}^{\intercal}\bm{E}_{U}-q^{-1}\bm{E}_{V}^{\intercal}\bm{E}_{V}
, we have

\|\mathcal{A}_{\natural}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\big(\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}d}+d\big)\leq C\sqrt{c_{0}}\sigma_{\min}.

Together with

nq\left\|D^{2}\mathcal{A}_{\natural}[(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}}),(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}})]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\big(\|\bm{L}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big),

and Cauchy inequality,
we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha nq}{2}\left|\Big\langle\mathcal{A}_{\natural},\,D^{2}\mathcal{A}_{\natural}[(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}}),(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}})]\Big\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{c_{0}}\alpha\sigma_{\min}\big(\|\bm{L}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big).

(F.11)

Combining (
F.9
), (
F.10
), and
(
F.11
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\nabla_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})}^{2}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big[(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}}),(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}})\big]-

\displaystyle\,\nabla_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})}^{2}p_{\alpha}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big[(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}}),(\bar{\bm{L}},\bar{\bm{R}})\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\sqrt{c_{0}}\alpha\sigma_{\min}\big(\|\bm{L}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big).

With (
D.1
) and Weyl’s inequality, we arrive at

\displaystyle\min_{(\bm{U},\bm{V})\in\mathcal{D}}\lambda_{\min}\!\Big[\bm{S}_{z}\big\{\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})-\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\big\}\bm{S}_{z}\Big]\geq

\displaystyle\,{\alpha}\sigma_{\min}-\alpha d-C\sqrt{c_{0}}\alpha\sigma_{\min}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\,{\alpha}\sigma_{\min}-\alpha\frac{c_{0}\sigma_{\min}}{\kappa}-C\sqrt{c_{0}}\alpha\sigma_{\min},

The proof is then finished after shrinking
c_{0}
sufficiently small.

F.4

Proof of Lemma
10

For any
\bm{H}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
, let
\bm{G}(s)=\bm{I}_{r}+s\bm{H}
. Since
\bm{G}(s)^{-{\intercal}}=\bm{I}_{r}-s\bm{H}^{\intercal}+o(s)
,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}\Phi(\bm{G}(s))\Big|_{s=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\langle\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*},\,\bm{U}\bm{H}\rangle-\frac{2}{q}\langle\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*},\,\bm{V}\bm{H}^{\intercal}\rangle

\displaystyle=2\Big\langle n^{-1}(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}-q^{-1}\bm{V}^{\intercal}(\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*}),\,\bm{H}\Big\rangle.

If
\bm{I}_{r}
is a local minimizer, the derivative above vanishes for every
\bm{H}
, which proves that
\bm{M}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})={\bm{0}}
. The equivalence with (
D.2
) is immediate from the
definition of
p_{\alpha}^{\natural}
.

F.5

Proof of Lemma
11

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma 1 in
[
33
]
. For completeness, we state it here. Define

f_{alg}(\bm{Q})=n^{-1}\big\|\bm{U}\bm{Q}-\bm{U}^{*}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+q^{-1}\big\|\bm{V}\bm{Q}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}^{*}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

First the first part, consider the minimizer of
f_{alg}
inside a small region around
\bm{P}

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}\text{ is invertible}}

\displaystyle\,f_{alg}(\bm{Q})

(F.12)

\displaystyle\,\|\bm{Q}-\bm{P}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 5\delta\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{r}\left(\bm{U}^{*}\right)}.

By Weyl’s inequality, we obtain that for any feasible
\bm{Q}
,
\sigma_{\min}(\bm{Q})\geq\sigma_{\min}(\bm{P})-5\delta\sqrt{n}/\sigma_{r}(\bm{U}^{*})

\geq 1/2
given that
\delta\leq\sigma_{r}(\bm{U}^{*})/(80\sqrt{n})
. Therefore
f_{alg}(\bm{Q})
is continuous over the feasible region (
F.12
). Notably this region is compact inside the manifold of invertible matrices, which implies the existence of a solution to (
F.12
). Then it suffices to show that this solution corresponds to the optimal alignment
\bm{Q}^{*}
. Note that by definition

n^{-1}\big\|\bm{U}\bm{Q}^{*}-\bm{U}^{*}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq f_{alg}(\bm{Q}^{*})\leq f_{alg}(\bm{P})\leq 2\delta^{2}.

With
n^{-1}\|\bm{U}\bm{Q}^{*}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq n^{-1}\|\bm{U}\bm{Q}^{*}-\bm{U}\bm{P}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-n^{-1}\|\bm{U}\bm{P}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
and
n^{-1/2}\|\bm{U}\bm{P}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta
, there is

\displaystyle\|\bm{Q}^{*}-\bm{P}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq

\displaystyle\,\sigma_{r}(\bm{U})^{-1}\big\|\bm{U}\bm{Q}^{*}-\bm{U}\bm{P}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{n}\sigma_{r}(\bm{U})^{-1}\sqrt{n^{-1}\|\bm{U}\bm{Q}^{*}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+n^{-1}\|\bm{U}\bm{P}-\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{3n}\sigma_{r}(\bm{U})^{-1}\delta.

Then it suffices to show that
\sigma_{r}(\bm{U}^{*})^{-1}\geq\sqrt{3}\sigma_{r}(\bm{U})^{-1}/5
. Again, by Weyl’s inequality,

\big|\sigma_{\min}(\bm{U}\bm{P})-\sigma_{\min}(\bm{U})\big|\leq\big\|\bm{U}\bm{P}-\bm{U}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta\leq\frac{1}{80}\sigma_{r}(\bm{U}^{*}).

Therefore, we know
\tfrac{79}{80}\sigma_{r}(\bm{U})\leq\sigma_{\min}(\bm{U}\bm{P})\leq\sigma_{r}(\bm{U})\sigma_{\max}(\bm{P})\leq\tfrac{3}{2}\sigma_{r}(\bm{U})
,
which gives
\sigma_{r}(\bm{U})\geq\tfrac{79}{120}\sigma_{r}(\bm{U})\geq 0.66\sigma_{r}(\bm{U})\geq\sqrt{3}\sigma_{r}(\bm{U})/5
.
We then conclude that

\|\bm{Q}^{*}-\bm{P}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{3}\sigma_{r}(\bm{U})^{-1}\delta\leq 5\delta\{\sigma_{r}(\bm{U}^{*}/\sqrt{n})\}^{-1},

which proves the first part. The second part follows similarly by noting that
2/3\leq\sigma_{r}(\bm{P}^{-{\intercal}})\leq\sigma_{1}(\bm{P}^{-{\intercal}})\leq 3/2
.
∎

F.6

Proof of Lemma
13

Throughout this proof, all concentration bounds are stated on finite events whose failure probabilities are summed by a union bound. Namely, one may replace
\delta
by a sufficiently small constant multiple of
\delta
in each Bernstein bound below.
For each
i\in[n]
and
j\in[q]
, write
\xi_{ij}:=\ell_{\alpha_{0}}^{\prime}(X_{ij}^{*})=\nu_{\star}\{\sigma(X_{ij}^{*})-Y_{ij}\}
and
\omega_{ij}:=\ell_{\alpha_{0}}^{\prime\prime}(X_{ij}^{*})=\nu_{\star}\sigma(X_{ij}^{*})\{1-\sigma(X_{ij}^{*})\}
. We then have
\mathbb{E}\xi_{ij}=0
,
\mathbb{E}\xi_{ij}^{2}=\nu_{\star}\omega_{ij}\leq\nu_{\star}\beta
, and
|\xi_{ij}|\leq\nu_{\star}
.
By construction,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}\bm{V}_{j,}^{*}+\sum_{j=1}^{q}\omega_{ij}(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U,i}\bm{V}_{j,}^{*\,{\intercal}})\bm{V}_{j,}^{*}={\bm{0}},\qquad i\in[n],

(F.13)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{ij}\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{ij}(\bar{\bm{E}}_{V,j}\bm{U}_{i,}^{*\,{\intercal}})\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}={\bm{0}},\qquad j\in[q].

(F.14)

We first bound
R_{\bar{Z}}=\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})\big\|_{2,\infty}
. For the Bernoulli model, for each
(i,j)
,
\bar{X}_{ij}-X_{ij}^{*}=\bar{\bm{E}}_{U,i}\bm{V}_{j,}^{*\,{\intercal}}+\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\bar{\bm{E}}_{V,j}^{{\intercal}}+\bar{\bm{E}}_{U,i}\bar{\bm{E}}_{V,j}^{{\intercal}}
,
and a second-order Taylor expansion gives

\ell^{\prime}_{\alpha_{0}}(\bar{X}_{ij})=\ell^{\prime}_{\alpha_{0}}(X_{ij}^{*})+\ell^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha_{0}}(X_{ij}^{*})(\bar{X}_{ij}-X_{ij}^{*})+\bar{R}_{ij}\;\text{, where}\;|\bar{R}_{ij}|\leq C|\bar{X}_{ij}-X_{ij}^{*}|^{2}.

Substituting this into the row gradients and using
(
F.13
)–(
F.14
), we obtain

\displaystyle q^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{U}_{i,}}\mathcal{L}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})={}

\displaystyle\,q^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}\bar{\bm{E}}_{V,j}+q^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\omega_{ij}(\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\bar{\bm{E}}_{V,j}^{{\intercal}})\bm{V}_{j,}^{*}+\bar{\bm{R}}_{U,i},

(F.15)

\displaystyle n^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{V}_{j,}}\mathcal{L}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})={}

\displaystyle\,n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{ij}\bar{\bm{E}}_{U,i}+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{ij}(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*}\bar{\bm{E}}_{U,i}^{{\intercal}})\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}+\bar{\bm{R}}_{V,j},

(F.16)

where, by telescoping, the remainders satisfy

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\|\bar{\bm{R}}_{U,i}\|\vee\max_{j\in[q]}\|\bar{\bm{R}}_{V,j}\|\leq C\beta\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\,\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\infty}+C\beta\omega_{*}\,\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(F.17)

We next invoke the explicit form of
(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})
to control (
F.15
) and (
F.16
). Let
\bm{H}_{V,j}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{ij}(\bm{U}_{i,}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}
and
\bm{H}_{U,i}:=\sum_{j=1}^{q}\omega_{ij}(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{V}_{j,}^{*}
for
j\in[q]
and
i\in[n]
.
Then the error can be written as
\bar{\bm{E}}_{V,j}=-\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{ij}(\bm{U}_{i,}^{*})^{\intercal}
and
\bar{\bm{E}}_{U,i}=-\bm{H}_{U,i}^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{\intercal}
.
By Lemma
9
, we know that, uniformly over
i\in[n]
and
j\in[q]
, it holds that

\|\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}\|\leq\frac{1}{\alpha n\sigma_{\min}},\quad\text{ and }\quad\|\bm{H}_{U,i}^{-1}\|\leq\frac{1}{\alpha q\sigma_{\min}}.

Consider the first term on the right side of (
F.15
). For each
i\in[n]
, there is

\displaystyle q^{-1}\big\|(\xi\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})_{i,\cdot}\big\|=

\displaystyle q^{-1}\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\xi_{i^{\prime}j}(\bm{U}_{i^{\prime}}^{*})^{\intercal}\Big\|

(F.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}^{2}\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}(\bm{U}_{i}^{*})^{\intercal}\Big\|+\Big||\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}\xi_{i^{\prime}j}\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}(\bm{U}_{i^{\prime}}^{*})^{\intercal}\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\|\bm{U}_{i}^{*}\|}{\alpha n\sigma_{\min}}\cdot\frac{1}{q}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}^{2}+\frac{1}{q}\Big\|\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}\xi_{i^{\prime}j}\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}(\bm{U}_{i^{\prime}}^{*})^{\intercal}\Big\|.

Hence, by Bernstein’s inequality applied to the bounded variables

\xi_{ij}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\xi_{ij}^{2}
, uniformly over
i\in[n]
, the diagonal part in (
F.18
) is bounded by

\frac{\|\bm{U}_{i}^{*}\|}{\alpha n\sigma_{\min}}\cdot\frac{1}{q}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}^{2}\leq C\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta\,\omega_{*}}{\alpha n\sigma_{\min}}+C\frac{\nu_{\star}^{2}L_{\star}\,\omega_{*}}{\alpha nq\sigma_{\min}}.

(F.19)

Here, we use that, if
\bm{A}_{1},\ldots,\bm{A}_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
are fixed, or measurable with respect to a
\sigma
-field independent of
\{\xi_{ij}\}_{j=1}^{m}
, then with probability at least
1-Ce^{-cL}
,

\big\|\sum_{j=1}^{m}\xi_{ij}\bm{A}_{j}\big\|\leq C\Big\{\sqrt{\nu_{\star}\beta L}\,\big(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\|\bm{A}_{j}\|^{2}\big)^{1/2}+\nu_{\star}L\,\max_{j\in[m]}\|\bm{A}_{j}\|\Big\}.

(F.20)

For the off-diagonal part, let
T_{i,2}:=q^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}\bm{A}_{ij}^{(-i)}
for
\bm{A}_{ij}^{(-i)}:=\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}\xi_{i^{\prime}j}(\bm{U}_{i^{\prime}}^{*})^{\intercal}
.
Applying (
F.20
) conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{-i}
, and then applying Bernstein again to the
\mathcal{F}_{-i}
-measurable quantities
\{\bm{A}_{ij}^{(-i)}\}_{j=1}^{q}
, yields, uniformly over
i\in[n]
,

\|T_{i,2}\|\leq C\Big\{\nu_{\star}L_{\star}\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{\alpha nq\sigma_{\min}}}+\frac{\nu_{\star}^{2}L_{\star}\,\omega_{*}}{\alpha nq\sigma_{\min}}\Big\}.

Together with the bound for the residual, we conclude that

q^{-1}\|\xi\bar{\bm{E}}_{V}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq C\Big\{\nu_{\star}L_{\star}\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{\alpha nq\sigma_{\min}}}+\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta\,\omega_{*}}{\alpha n\sigma_{\min}}+\frac{\nu_{\star}^{2}L_{\star}\,\omega_{*}}{\alpha nq\sigma_{\min}}\Big\}.

(F.21)

By symmetry, one can obtain as well

n^{-1}\|\xi^{\intercal}\bar{\bm{E}}_{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq C\Big\{\nu_{\star}L_{\star}\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{\alpha nq\sigma_{\min}}}+\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta\,\omega_{*}}{\alpha q\sigma_{\min}}+\frac{\nu_{\star}^{2}L_{\star}\,\omega_{*}}{\alpha nq\sigma_{\min}}\Big\}.

(F.22)

Next, consider the second term on the right side of (
F.15
), which can be bounded following the same strategy. Specifically, let
\bm{Z}_{ijk}:=q^{-1}\omega_{ij}\xi_{kj}\{\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}(\bm{U}_{k,}^{*})^{{\intercal}}\}\bm{V}_{j,}^{*}
, which are independent and centered across
k,i\in[n]
and
j\in[q]
, as the only randomness is from
\xi_{kj}
. Then the second term of (
F.15
) can be written as
-\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\bm{Z}_{ijk}
. For any unit vector
\bm{a}\in\mathbb{S}^{r-1}
, with
\omega_{ij}\leq\beta
and
\|\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}\|\leq\{\alpha n\sigma_{\min}\}^{-1}
, we know

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[q],k\in[n]}\mathbb{E}(\bm{a}^{\intercal}\bm{Z}_{ijk})^{2}

\displaystyle=\nu_{\star}q^{-2}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\omega_{ij}^{2}(\bm{a}^{\intercal}\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{2}\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}\Big\{\sum_{k=1}^{n}\omega_{kj}(\bm{U}_{k,}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}_{k,}^{*}\Big\}\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}(\bm{U}_{i,}^{*})^{\intercal}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta^{2}\|\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\|^{2}}{\alpha nq^{2}\sigma_{\min}}\sum_{j=1}^{q}(\bm{a}^{\intercal}\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{2}\leq C\frac{\beta^{2}\omega_{*}^{2}\kappa}{\alpha nq}.

Moreover, by definition, one can check that
\max_{j\in[q],i,k\in[n]}\|\bm{Z}_{ijk}\|\leq C\nu_{\star}{\beta\omega_{*}^{3}}/{(\alpha nq\sigma_{\min})}
. Thus vector Bernstein, a
1/2
-net argument, and a union bound over
i\in[n]
give

q^{-1}\max_{i\in[n]}\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{q}\omega_{ij}(\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\bar{\bm{E}}_{V,j}^{{\intercal}})\bm{V}_{j,}^{*}\Big\|=\max_{i\in[n]}\big\|\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\bm{Z}_{ijk}\big\|\leq C\beta\omega_{*}\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}\kappa L_{\star}}{\alpha nq}}+C\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta\omega_{*}^{3}L_{\star}}{\alpha nq\sigma_{\min}}.

(F.23)

By symmetry,
n^{-1}\max_{j\in[q]}\big\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{ij}(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*}\bar{\bm{E}}_{U,i}^{{\intercal}})\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\big\|
can be bounded similarly.

For the penalty term in
R_{\bar{Z}}=\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})\big\|_{2,\infty}
, recall that
\bm{M}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=n^{-1}(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{U}-q^{-1}\bm{V}^{\intercal}(\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{*})
. Hence, at
(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})
, we telescope

\bm{M}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})=n^{-1}\bar{\bm{E}}_{U}^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}-q^{-1}(\bm{V}^{*})^{\intercal}\bar{\bm{E}}_{V}+n^{-1}\bar{\bm{E}}_{U}^{\intercal}\bar{\bm{E}}_{U}-q^{-1}\bar{\bm{E}}_{V}^{\intercal}\bar{\bm{E}}_{V}.

(F.24)

We now invoke the expansion of
(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})
. Let
\bm{M}_{U}=-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{H}_{U,i}^{-1}\big\{\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{\intercal}\big\}\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}
,
\bm{M}_{V}=-q^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{q}(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}\big\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{ij}(\bm{U}_{i,}^{*})^{\intercal}\big\}
, and
\bm{M}_{\rm quad}=n^{-1}\bar{\bm{E}}_{U}^{\intercal}\bar{\bm{E}}_{U}-q^{-1}\bar{\bm{E}}_{V}^{\intercal}\bar{\bm{E}}_{V}
. Then (
F.24
) becomes

\bm{M}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})=\bm{M}_{U}-\bm{M}_{V}+\bm{M}_{\rm quad},

(F.25)

Note that
\bm{M}_{U}
is a sum of independent mean zero random matrices over
i\in[n]
, while

\bm{M}_{V}
is a sum of independent mean-zero random matrices over
j\in[q]
.
For each
i
, letting
\bm{M}_{U,i}=-n^{-1}\bm{H}_{U,i}^{-1}\big\{\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{\intercal}\big\}\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}
, we have
\mathbb{E}(\bm{M}_{U,i})={\bm{0}}
and

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbb{E}(\bm{M}_{U,i}\bm{M}_{U,i}^{\intercal})\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\|^{2}}{n^{2}}\Big\|\bm{H}_{U,i}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\{\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{\intercal}\Big\}\times\Big\{\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}\bm{V}_{j,}^{*}\Big\}\Big]\bm{H}_{U,i}^{-1}\Big\|

\displaystyle=\frac{\nu_{\star}\|\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\|^{2}}{n^{2}}\big\|\bm{H}_{U,i}^{-1}\Big\{\sum_{j=1}^{q}\omega_{ij}(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{\intercal}\bm{V}_{j,}^{*}\Big\}\bm{H}_{U,i}^{-1}\big\|

\displaystyle=\frac{\nu_{\star}\|\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\|^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\bm{H}_{U,i}^{-1}\|\leq\frac{\nu_{\star}\|\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\|^{2}}{\alpha n^{2}q\sigma_{\min}}.

Here, the expectation is taken with respect to
\bm{Y}
, and note that
w_{ij}
is independent of
\bm{Y}
, and so is
\bm{H}_{U,i}
. Thus, we arrive at

\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}(\bm{M}_{U,i}\bm{M}_{U,i}^{\intercal})\Big\|\leq\frac{\nu_{\star}\|\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{\alpha n^{2}q\sigma_{\min}}\leq C\frac{\nu_{\star}\kappa r}{\alpha nq}.

Moreover, since
|\xi_{ij}|\leq 1
for all
(i,j)
, we know
\|\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{\intercal}\|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{q}\|\bm{V}_{j,}^{*}\|\leq q\|\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}
, which yields

\|\bm{M}_{U,i}\|\leq\frac{1}{n}\|\bm{H}_{U,i}^{-1}\|\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{\intercal}\Big\|\|\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\|\leq\frac{\nu_{\star}\|\bm{U}_{i,}^{*}\|\,\|\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}}{\alpha n\sigma_{\min}}.

Thus matrix Bernstein yields

\|\bm{M}_{U}\|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}\kappa L_{\star}}{\alpha nq}}+C\frac{\nu_{\star}\omega_{*}\|\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}L_{\star}}{\alpha n\sigma_{\min}},

(F.26)

with probability at least
1-\delta
. The second term is of lower order under the present
scaling assumptions. A completely symmetric argument gives the same bound for
\|\bm{M}_{V}\|
.

On the other hand, it is easy to see that
\|\bm{M}_{\rm quad}\|\leq\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}

Combining this with (
F.25
), and (
F.26
), we obtain

\|\bm{M}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})\|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta\kappa L_{\star}}{\alpha nq}}+C\frac{\nu_{\star}\omega_{*}^{2}L_{\star}}{\alpha m_{\star}\sigma_{\min}}+\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(F.27)

For
p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\alpha nq\|\bm{M}^{*}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}/4
, we know
q^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{U}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})=\alpha\{\bar{\bm{U}}\bm{M}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})^{\intercal}+\bar{\bm{E}}_{U}\bm{M}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})\}/2
and
n^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{V}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})=-\alpha\{\bar{\bm{E}}_{V}\bm{M}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})^{\intercal}+\bar{\bm{V}}\bm{M}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})\}/2
. We then conclude that

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})\big\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\Big(\omega_{*}+\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\infty}\Big)\|\bm{M}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})\|

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha\Big(\omega_{*}+\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\infty}\Big)\Bigg[\nu_{\star}\sqrt{\frac{\beta\kappa L_{\star}}{\alpha nq}}+\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Bigg].

(F.28)

Combining (
F.15
)–(
F.23
),
(
F.17
), and (
F.28
), we obtain

\displaystyle R_{\bar{Z}}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\nu_{\star}L_{\star}\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{\alpha nq\,\sigma_{\min}}}+C\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta\,\omega_{*}}{\alpha(n\wedge q)\sigma_{\min}}+C\frac{\nu_{\star}^{2}L_{\star}\omega_{*}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}{nq}}+C\beta\omega_{*}\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}\kappa L_{\star}}{\alpha nq}}+\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta\omega_{*}^{3}L_{\star}}{\alpha nq\sigma_{\min}}

(F.29)

\displaystyle+C\alpha\Big(\omega_{*}+\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\infty}\Big)\Bigg[\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}\kappa L_{\star}}{\alpha nq}}+\frac{\nu_{\star}\omega_{*}^{2}L_{\star}}{\alpha(n\wedge q)\sigma_{\min}}+\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+C(\alpha+\beta)\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\,\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\,\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle+C(\alpha+\beta)\omega_{*}\,\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

We next bound
(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})
. Recall that
\bar{\bm{E}}_{U,i}=-\bm{H}_{U,i}^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\xi_{ij}(\bm{V}_{j,}^{*})^{\intercal}
and
\bar{\bm{E}}_{V,j}=-\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{ij}(\bm{U}_{i,}^{*})^{\intercal}
. With
\|\bm{H}_{U,i}^{-1}\|\leq(\alpha q\sigma_{\min})^{-1}
and
\|\bm{H}_{V,j}^{-1}\|\leq(\alpha n\sigma_{\min})^{-1}
, we have
\|\bar{\bm{E}}_{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|\xi\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}/(\alpha q\sigma_{\min})
and
\|\bar{\bm{E}}_{V}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|\xi^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}/(\alpha n\sigma_{\min})
.
Consequently, one has

\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\left\{q^{-1}\|\xi\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\vee n^{-1}\|\xi^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\right\}.

Invoking item
(e)
, we get

\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\frac{C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\omega_{*}.

(F.30)

Similarly, by summing the row-wise bounds, there is

\displaystyle n^{-1}\|\bar{\bm{E}}_{U}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\bar{\bm{E}}_{U,i}\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}q^{2}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\,n^{-1}\|\xi\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

\displaystyle q^{-1}\|\bar{\bm{E}}_{V}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=q^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\|\bar{\bm{E}}_{V,j}\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}n^{2}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\,q^{-1}\|\xi^{\intercal}\bm{U}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Therefore, we arrive at

\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\leq C\frac{\tau_{*}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\left\{\frac{\nu_{\star}}{n\wedge q}+\frac{\nu_{\star}^{2}\log((n+q)/\delta)}{nq}\right\}^{1/2}\leq C\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\tau_{*},

(F.31)

which together with (
F.30
) gives

\frac{\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\infty}}{\omega_{*}}\leq\frac{\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}},\qquad\frac{\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}}{\tau_{*}}\leq\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}.

(F.32)

Next, substituting (
F.30
) and (
F.31
) into the last three lines of (
F.29
), and using
\|\bm{U}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\vee\|\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\omega_{*}
, one can check that with the scaling conditions (
A.2
), the right side of (
F.29
) can be further bounded by
C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\omega_{*}
, which therefore proves (
E.1
).

We now show the second part of Lemma
13
.
Define
\bm{\Delta}=(\bm{\Delta}_{U}^{\intercal},\bm{\Delta}_{V}^{\intercal})^{\intercal}
with
(\bm{\Delta}_{U},\bm{\Delta}_{V}):=(\widetilde{\bm{U}}-\bar{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bar{\bm{V}})
, and let
(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))=(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})+s(\bm{\Delta}_{U},\bm{\Delta}_{V})
for
s\in[0,1]
. Applying the integral mean value theorem to

\mathrm{vec}\{\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\cdot)^{\intercal}\}
along this segment yields

\bm{S}_{z}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}\bm{S}_{z}\,\mathrm{vec}\big((\bm{S}_{Z}^{-1}\bm{\Delta})^{\intercal}\big)=\mathrm{vec}\Big(\bm{S}_{Z}\{\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})-\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})\}^{\intercal}\Big),

(F.33)

where
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}:=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\,ds
and recall that we have defined

\bm{S}_{Z}=\begin{pmatrix}q^{-1/2}\bm{I}_{n}&{\bm{0}}\\
{\bm{0}}&n^{-1/2}\bm{I}_{q}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\bm{S}_{z}=\begin{pmatrix}q^{-1/2}\bm{I}_{nr}&{\bm{0}}\\
{\bm{0}}&n^{-1/2}\bm{I}_{qr}\end{pmatrix}.

Compared with a similar expansion (
D.26
), the terms corresponding to
\Gamma_{2}
and
\Gamma_{3}
are absorbed into
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
. In particular, as in Step 1 of the proof of Theorem
4
, Lemma
8
gives

\lambda_{\min}\!\left(\bm{S}_{z}\int_{0}^{1}\{\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))-\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\}\,ds\bm{S}_{z}\right)\geq\frac{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}{2}.

For the Bernoulli model,
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}(\bm{U},\bm{V})=\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y}
is constant, hence
\int_{0}^{1}\{\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))-\mathbb{E}\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\}\,ds

=\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\rm ave}
, with
\|\bm{S}_{z}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\rm ave}\bm{S}_{z}\|\leq C\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)
, while
\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{G}_{e}(\bm{U}(s),\bm{V}(s))\,ds=\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{\rm ave}
satisfies

\|\bm{S}_{z}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{\rm ave}\bm{S}_{z}\|\leq C\sqrt{\kappa\sigma_{\min}}\Big(\|(\bar{\bm{E}}_{U},\bar{\bm{E}}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}+\|(\bm{\Delta}_{U},\bm{\Delta}_{V})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}\Big).

Therefore, under the same small constant conditions as in
Theorem
4
, both terms are absorbed, and hence

\lambda_{\min}(\bm{S}_{z}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}\bm{S}_{z})\geq\frac{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}{4}.

(F.34)

Since
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
minimizes
(\bm{U},\bm{V})\mapsto\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})\|_{2,\infty}^{2}
over
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(\infty)}(\varepsilon,\epsilon)
, we have
\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}^{2}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\big\|_{2,\infty}

\leq R_{\bar{Z}}
. We therefore arrive at

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}\{\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})-\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})\}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\big\|\bm{S}_{Z}\nabla_{\bm{Z}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bar{\bm{U}},\bar{\bm{V}})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(F.35)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{2nq}\,R_{\bar{Z}}.

Combining (
E.1
) (
F.29
), (
F.33
), (
F.34
), and
(
F.35
), we obtain (
E.2
).

Finally, because
\xi=\nu_{\star}(\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y})
, the two inequalities (
F.21
) and (
F.22
), after replacing
\log(n/\delta)
and
\log(q/\delta)
by
\log((n+q)/\delta)
, using
(n\wedge q)^{-1}\geq n^{-1}\vee q^{-1}
and invoking
scaling condition (
A.2
), give
(
E.3
).

F.7

Proof of Lemma
14

Proof.

Write
L_{\star}=r+\log\{(n+q)T_{\star}/\delta\}
. The Bernstein inequalities below are applied with failure probability of order
\delta/T_{\star}
for each step
t\in\{1,\dots,T_{\star}\}
and then union bounded over rows, columns. Let
\mathcal{L}^{-i}(\bm{X})
and
\mathcal{L}^{-\ell}(\bm{X})
denote the same empirical loss as
\mathcal{L}(\bm{X})
, but constructed from
\bm{Y}^{-i}
and
\bm{Y}^{-\ell}
, respectively, where the leave-one-out data matrices are defined in Theorem
A.6
. Denote the corresponding gradient matrices by

\mathcal{G}^{-i}(\bm{U},\bm{V}):=\nabla_{\bm{X}}\mathcal{L}^{-i}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal}),\quad\mathcal{G}^{-\ell}(\bm{U},\bm{V}):=\nabla_{\bm{X}}\mathcal{L}^{-\ell}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal}).

(F.36)

Starting from
(\bm{U}^{0,-i},\bm{V}^{0,-i})
, define the row wise leave-one-out gradient iterates by

\bm{U}^{t+1,-i}=\bm{U}^{t,-i}-\frac{\eta}{q}\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}^{-i}(\bm{U}^{t,-i}\bm{V}^{t,-i{\intercal}}),\quad\bm{V}^{t+1,-i}=\bm{V}^{t,-i}-\frac{\eta}{n}\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}^{-i}(\bm{U}^{t,-i}\bm{V}^{t,-i{\intercal}}).

Starting from
(\bm{U}^{0,-\ell},\bm{V}^{0,-\ell})
, define the column wise leave-one-out gradient iterates similarly by

\bm{U}^{t+1,-\ell}=\bm{U}^{t,-\ell}-\frac{\eta}{q}\nabla_{\bm{U}}\mathcal{L}^{-\ell}(\bm{U}^{t,-\ell}\bm{V}^{t,-\ell{\intercal}}),\qquad\bm{V}^{t+1,-\ell}=\bm{V}^{t,-\ell}-\frac{\eta}{n}\nabla_{\bm{V}}\mathcal{L}^{-\ell}(\bm{U}^{t,-\ell}\bm{V}^{t,-\ell{\intercal}}).

By construction,
(\bm{U}^{0,-i},\bm{V}^{0,-i})
is independent of
\{\bm{Y}_{i\ell}\}_{\ell\in[q]}
, and the data matrix
\bm{Y}^{-i}
has deterministic
i
th row equal to
\bm{P}^{*}_{i,\cdot}
. Hence the whole trajectory
\{(\bm{U}^{t,-i},\bm{V}^{t,-i})\}_{t\geq 0}
is independent of
\{\bm{Y}_{i\ell}\}_{\ell\in[q]}
.
Likewise,
\{(\bm{U}^{t,-\ell},\bm{V}^{t,-\ell})\}_{t\geq 0}
is independent of
\{\bm{Y}_{k\ell}\}_{k\in[n]}
.

Next, recall that at Step 3 in the proof of Theorem
4
, we have defined
\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}
to be an optimal alignment matrix for the main iterate
(\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t})
relative to
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
with
\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}:=\bm{U}^{t}\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}
and
\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}:=\bm{V}^{t}(\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}
. Analogously, for each
i\in[n]
, let
\bm{G}_{t}^{-i}
be an optimal alignment matrix for
(\bm{U}^{t,-i},\bm{V}^{t,-i})
relative to
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
, i.e.,
\bm{G}_{t}^{-i}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\bm{G}\in GL(r)}\|(\bm{U}^{t,-i}\bm{G}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\bm{V}^{t,-i}\bm{G}^{-{\intercal}}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}}
, and define

\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i}:=\bm{U}^{t,-i}\bm{G}_{t}^{-i},\qquad\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}:=\bm{V}^{t,-i}(\bm{G}_{t}^{-i})^{-{\intercal}}.

For each
\ell\in[q]
, let
\bm{G}_{t}^{-\ell}
be an optimal alignment matrix for
(\bm{U}^{t,-\ell},\bm{V}^{t,-\ell})
relative to
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
, and define

\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-\ell}:=\bm{U}^{t,-\ell}\bm{G}_{t}^{-\ell},\qquad\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-\ell}:=\bm{V}^{t,-\ell}(\bm{G}_{t}^{-\ell})^{-{\intercal}}.

Write the error as

\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t,-i}:=\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i}-\bm{U}^{*},\;\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t,-i}:=\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}-\bm{V}^{*},\;\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t,-\ell}:=\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-\ell}-\bm{U}^{*},\;\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t,-\ell}:=\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-\ell}-\bm{V}^{*}.

Let
\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger}:=(\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger})^{\intercal}\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}
,
\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{-i}:=(\bm{G}_{t}^{-i})^{\intercal}\bm{G}_{t}^{-i}
, and
\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{-\ell}:=(\bm{G}_{t}^{-\ell})^{\intercal}\bm{G}_{t}^{-\ell}
.
By the same identity as in Step 3 of the proof of Theorem
2
, the balancing penalty has vanishing gradient at each aligned iterate. Therefore, the row-wise leave-one-out sequence admits

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1,-i}:=

\displaystyle\,\bm{U}^{t+1,-i}\bm{G}_{t}^{-i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i}-\frac{\eta}{q}\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})-\frac{\eta}{q}\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{-i}-\bm{I}_{r}),

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1,-i}:=

\displaystyle\,\bm{V}^{t+1,-i}(\bm{G}_{t}^{-i})^{-{\intercal}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}-\frac{\eta}{n}\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})-\frac{\eta}{n}\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})\big\{(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{-i})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big\},

where we write
h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V}):=\mathcal{L}^{-i}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})+p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
.
Similarly, for
h_{\alpha}^{-\ell,\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V}):=\mathcal{L}^{-\ell}(\bm{U}\bm{V}^{\intercal})+p_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U},\bm{V})
, the column-wise leave-one-out sequence admits

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1,-\ell}:=

\displaystyle\,\bm{U}^{t+1,-\ell}\bm{G}_{t}^{-\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-\ell}-\frac{\eta}{q}\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{-\ell,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-\ell},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-\ell})-\frac{\eta}{q}\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{-\ell,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-\ell},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-\ell})(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{-\ell}-\bm{I}_{r}),

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1,-\ell}:=

\displaystyle\,\bm{V}^{t+1,-\ell}(\bm{G}_{t}^{-\ell})^{-{\intercal}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-\ell}-\frac{\eta}{n}\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{-\ell,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-\ell},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-\ell})-\frac{\eta}{n}\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{-\ell,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-\ell},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-\ell})\big\{(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{-\ell})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big\}.

Recall that the full data gradient update can also be written as

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}=

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}-\frac{\eta}{q}\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})-\frac{\eta}{q}\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger}-\bm{I}_{r}),

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}=

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\frac{\eta}{n}\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})-\frac{\eta}{n}\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\big\{(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big\}.

We focus on establishing the error bounds for the row-wise leave-one-out sequence. The column-wise argument can be similarly obtained by replacing
(n,\bm{U})
with
(q,\bm{V})
. Let
\bm{\Delta}_{U}^{t,-i}:=\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}
and
\bm{\Delta}_{V}^{t,-i}:=\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}-\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}
. These quantities would be much smaller than the error between the iterates and LOO iterates to the true
(\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{*})
. Define

\displaystyle a_{t}^{\mathrm{row}}:=\max_{i\in[n]}\Big\{n^{-1/2}\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\vee q^{-1/2}\|\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}-\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\Big\},

\displaystyle a_{t}^{\mathrm{col}}:=\max_{\ell\in[q]}\Big\{n^{-1/2}\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-\ell}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\vee q^{-1/2}\|\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-\ell}-\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\Big\},

\displaystyle b_{t}:=\max_{i\in[n]}\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i}_{i,\cdot}-\bm{U}^{*}_{i,\cdot}\|\vee\max_{\ell\in[q]}\|\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-\ell}_{\ell,\cdot}-\bm{V}^{*}_{\ell,\cdot}\|,

\displaystyle c_{t}:=\max_{i\in[n]}\Big(\|\bm{G}_{t}^{-i}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{t}^{-i})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\Big)\vee\max_{\ell\in[q]}\Big(\|\bm{G}_{t}^{-\ell}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{t}^{-\ell})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\Big).

In addition, for the full data gradient descent sequence, we write

\displaystyle d_{t}:=\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}},\qquad r_{t}:=\|(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}-\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\|_{2,\infty},

\displaystyle\,\vartheta_{t}:=\|\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger}-\bm{I}_{r}\|\vee\|(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\|.

One can show by induction that, for all
0\leq s\leq t
,

\displaystyle a_{t}^{\rm row}

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{t}a_{0}^{\rm row}+\frac{C}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\left\{\tau_{*}\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta L_{\star}}{nq}}+\nu_{\star}\omega_{*}\frac{L_{\star}}{q\sqrt{n}}+\omega_{*}\sqrt{\nu_{\star}\frac{q\beta+\nu_{\star}L_{\star}}{n^{2}q}}\right\},

(F.37)

\displaystyle a_{t}^{\rm col}

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{t}a_{0}^{\rm col}+\frac{C}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\left\{\tau_{*}\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta L_{\star}}{nq}}+\nu_{\star}\omega_{*}\frac{L_{\star}}{n\sqrt{q}}+\omega_{*}\sqrt{\nu_{\star}\frac{n\beta+L_{\star}}{nq^{2}}}\right\},

(F.38)

\displaystyle b_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{t}b_{0}+C\frac{\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\omega_{*},

(F.39)

\displaystyle c_{t}

\displaystyle\leq 2\iota_{0}\frac{\alpha}{\beta\kappa},

(F.40)

\displaystyle\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger,U}\vee\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger,V}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\big\{\frac{1}{8}\alpha\sigma_{\min}\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}+C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\big\}\omega_{*}.

(F.41)

The quantities
d_{t}
,
r_{t}
and
\vartheta_{t}
satisfy the same local contraction arguments as in
(2)
–
(4)
from Step 3 of Section
D.3
, where
\iota_{0}
is the constant appearing in induction hypothesis
(4)
. In particular, once (
F.41
) is available, the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
4
yields the contractions for
d_{t+1}
,
r_{t+1}
, and
\vartheta_{t+1}
.

We therefore proceed as follows. First, assuming (
F.41
) holds for the previous step, we obtain the bounds for
d_{t+1}
,
r_{t+1}
, and
\vartheta_{t+1}
exactly as in the proof of Theorem
4
. Next, assuming that the local contraction bounds
(2)
–
(4)
hold, we verify (
F.37
)–(
F.40
). Finally, we prove (
F.41
). This closes the induction. At time
t=0
, the bounds (
F.37
)–(
F.40
) follow from the initialization assumptions in Theorem
A.6
.

For
a_{t}^{\mathrm{row}}
, we begin by bounding
\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1,-i}:=\bm{U}^{t+1,-i}\bm{G}_{t}^{-i}
and
\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1,-i}:=\bm{V}^{t+1,-i}(\bm{G}_{t}^{-i})^{-{\intercal}}
. Recall that
\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1}:=\bm{U}^{t+1}\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger}
and
\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}:=\bm{V}^{t+1}(\bm{G}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}
. Let

\bar{\bm{\Delta}}_{U}^{t+1,-i}:=\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1,-i}-\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1},\qquad\bar{\bm{\Delta}}_{V}^{t+1,-i}:=\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1,-i}-\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1}.

(F.42)

Using the aligned update identities for the main sequence and the leave-one-out sequence, we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\Delta}}_{U}^{t+1,-i}={}

\displaystyle\,\bm{\Delta}_{U}^{t,-i}-\frac{\eta}{q}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})-\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\Big\}

\displaystyle\,-\frac{\eta}{q}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{-i}-\bm{I}_{r})-\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger}-\bm{I}_{r})\Big\},

(F.43)

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\Delta}}_{V}^{t+1,-i}={}

\displaystyle\,\bm{\Delta}_{V}^{t,-i}-\frac{\eta}{n}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})-\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\Big\}

\displaystyle\,-\frac{\eta}{n}\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})\big((\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{-i})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big)-\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\big((\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{\dagger})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big)\Big\}.

(F.44)

We first analyze the principal gradient difference
\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})-\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})
. Note that it can be decomposed as

\displaystyle\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})\!-\!\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})\Big\}-\Big\{\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})\!-\!\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\Big\}.

(F.45)

The same decomposition holds for the
\bm{V}
gradient. We first bound the second term.
Since
\ell_{\alpha_{0}}^{\prime\prime}(x;y)=\nu_{\star}\sigma(x)\{1-\sigma(x)\}
does not depend on
y
, the Hessians of
h_{\alpha}^{\natural}
and
h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}
are identical. Hence, by the fundamental theorem of calculus (Theorem 4.2 in
28
, Chapter XIII) along the segment
\big(\bm{U}_{t,i}(s),\bm{V}_{t,i}(s)\big)=\big(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t}+s\bm{\Delta}_{U}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}+s\bm{\Delta}_{V}^{t,-i}\big)
for
s\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\,\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{vec}\Big(\big[\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})-\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\big]^{\intercal}\Big)\\
\mathrm{vec}\Big(\big[\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})-\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t})\big]^{\intercal}\Big)\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}_{t,i}(s),\bm{V}_{t,i}(s))\,ds\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{vec}\big((\bm{\Delta}_{U}^{t,-i})^{\intercal}\big)\\
\mathrm{vec}\big((\bm{\Delta}_{V}^{t,-i})^{\intercal}\big)\end{pmatrix}.

One can check that by the induction hypotheses and the bounds for the sequence
(\bm{U}^{t,-i},\bm{V}^{t,-i})
, the whole segment lies in the same local region
\mathcal{D}_{uv}^{(\infty)}(\epsilon)
. Therefore the same Hessian contraction argument as in Step 1 of the proof of Theorem
4
gives

\Big\|\bm{S}_{z}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{vec}\big((\bm{\Delta}_{U}^{t,-i})^{\intercal}\big)\\
\mathrm{vec}\big((\bm{\Delta}_{V}^{t,-i})^{\intercal}\big)\end{pmatrix}\!-\!\eta\bm{S}_{z}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\bm{z}}^{2}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\bm{U}_{t,i}(s),\bm{V}_{t,i}(s))\,ds\,\bm{S}_{z}\bm{w}_{t}^{-i}\Big\|\leq\Big(1-\frac{5}{8}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Big)\|\bm{w}_{t}^{-i}\|,

(F.46)

where we write
\bm{w}_{t}^{-i}=\bm{S}_{z}^{-1}\big(\mathrm{vec}\big\{(\bm{\Delta}_{U}^{t,-i})^{\intercal}\big\}^{\intercal},\mathrm{vec}\big\{(\bm{\Delta}_{V}^{t,-i})^{\intercal}\big\}^{\intercal}\big)^{\intercal}
.

We now bound the first term in (
F.45
). Note that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})-\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})=\nu_{\star}\bm{e}_{i}\Big\{\bm{e}_{i}^{\intercal}(\bm{P}^{*}-\bm{Y})\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\Big\},

(F.47)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})-\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})=\nu_{\star}(\bm{P}^{*}_{i,\cdot}-\bm{Y}_{i,\cdot})^{\intercal}\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i}_{i,\cdot}.

(F.48)

Now the perturbation is evaluated at
(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})
, which depends on the
i
th row of the data through
\bm{G}_{t}^{-i}
. To control this dependence, we let

\check{\bm{G}}_{t}^{-i}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\bm{G}\in GL(r)}\|(\bm{U}^{t,-i}\bm{G}-\bm{U}^{*},\bm{V}^{t,-i}\bm{G}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}^{*})\|_{2,\mathrm{F}},

and set
\check{\bm{U}}^{t,-i}:=\bm{U}^{t,-i}\check{\bm{G}}_{t}^{-i}
,
\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}:=\bm{V}^{t,-i}(\check{\bm{G}}_{t}^{-i})^{-{\intercal}}
. Then one can easily check with Lemma
11
that

\big\|\check{\bm{G}}_{t}^{-i}(\bm{G}_{t}^{-i})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big\|\vee\big\|\bm{G}_{t}^{-i}(\check{\bm{G}}_{t}^{-i})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big\|\leq C\big(\rho^{t}\phi_{nq}+\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\big)\frac{\tau_{*}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}.

(F.49)

Subsequently, let
\mathcal{F}_{-i}:=\sigma\!\big(\{Y_{k\ell}:k\neq i,\ \ell\in[q]\}\big)
. Then
(\check{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})
is
\mathcal{F}_{-i}
-measurable, while
\{Y_{ij}\}_{j=1}^{q}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{-i}
. By definition, we know
\nu_{\star}P_{ij}^{*}(1-P_{ij}^{*})\leq\beta
. Conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{-i}
, with probability at least
1-Ce^{-cL_{\star}}
,

\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{q}\nu_{\star}(Y_{ij}-P_{ij}^{*})(\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})_{j,}\Big\|\leq C\left\{\sqrt{\nu_{\star}\beta L_{\star}}\,\|\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\nu_{\star}L_{\star}\,\|\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\|_{2\to\infty}\right\}.

Furthermore, with (
F.49
), one can obtain that

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{e}_{i}^{\intercal}(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\big\|\leq

\displaystyle\,\big\|\bm{e}_{i}^{\intercal}(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\big\|\Big(1+\big\|\bm{G}_{t}^{-i}(\check{\bm{G}}_{t}^{-i})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big\|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\big\{\sqrt{\beta L_{\star}/\nu_{\star}}\,\|\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+L_{\star}\|\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\|_{2\to\infty}\big\}.

Next, because
\|(\bm{Y}_{i,\cdot}-\bm{P}^{*}_{i,\cdot})^{\intercal}\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i}_{i,\cdot}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\|\bm{Y}_{i,\cdot}-\bm{P}^{*}_{i,\cdot}\|_{2}\,\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i}_{i,\cdot}\|
, Bernstein inequality gives, with probability at least
1-\delta/n
,

\|\bm{Y}_{i,\cdot}-\bm{P}^{*}_{i,\cdot}\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{(q\beta+\nu_{\star}L_{\star})/{\nu_{\star}}}.

Therefore, using the induction hypotheses and the full sequence bounds, we get

\|\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|\bm{\Delta}_{V}^{t,-i}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\sqrt{q}\,\tau_{*},\qquad\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i}_{i,\cdot}\|\leq\|\bm{U}^{*}_{i,\cdot}\|+b_{t}\leq C\omega_{*}.

Substituting these estimates into (
F.47
) and (
F.48
), we obtain uniformly over
i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{q\sqrt{n}}\Big\|\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})-\nabla_{\bm{U}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\{\tau_{*}\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta L_{\star}}{nq}}+\nu_{\star}\omega_{*}\frac{L_{\star}}{q\sqrt{n}}\right\},

(F.50)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\sqrt{q}}\Big\|\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})-\nabla_{\bm{V}}h_{\alpha}^{-i,\natural}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C\omega_{*}\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}(q\beta+\nu_{\star}L_{\star})}{n^{2}q}}.

(F.51)

Now for the second term in (
F.43
) and (
F.44
) that controls the balancing along the LOO iterates, similar to handling
\gamma_{4,t}^{\dagger}
in the proof of Theorem
4
.
Using
\|\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{-i}-\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*}\|\vee\|(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{-i})^{-1}-(\bm{\Lambda}_{t}^{*})^{-1}\|\leq 6\iota_{0}\alpha/(\beta\kappa)
,
together with the bounds
(
F.50
) and (
F.51
), we know that the second term in (
F.43
) and (
F.44
) can be bounded similar to (
F.50
) and (
F.51
).
Thus, the error arising from balancing can be absorbed into (
F.50
) and (
F.51
).

Combining (
F.43
), (
F.44
),
(
F.46
), (
F.50
),
and (
F.51
), we know
\bar{a}^{\mathrm{row}}_{t}=\max_{i\in[n]}n^{-1/2}\big\|\bar{\bm{\Delta}}_{U}^{t+1,-i}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\vee\max_{i\in[n]}q^{-1/2}\big\|\bar{\bm{\Delta}}_{V}^{t+1,-i}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}
can be controlled by

\displaystyle\bar{a}^{\mathrm{row}}_{t}\leq\Big(1-\frac{5}{8}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Big)a_{t}^{\mathrm{row}}+C\eta\left\{\tau_{*}\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta L_{\star}}{nq}}+\nu_{\star}\omega_{*}\frac{L_{\star}}{q\sqrt{n}}+\omega_{*}\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}(q\beta+\nu_{\star}L_{\star})}{n^{2}q}}\right\}.

The column-wise version
a_{t}^{\rm col}
follows by swapping
n,\bm{U}
and
q,\bm{V}
.

We next bound
b_{t+1}
. The key point is that
\bm{e}_{i}^{\intercal}(\bm{Y}^{-i}-\bm{P}^{*})=0
.
In particular, following the same row-wise decomposition for
\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t,-i}_{i,\cdot}-\bm{U}^{*}_{i,\cdot}
as in Step 3 of the proof of Theorem
4
, the analogue of the stochastic term
\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger,(U)}
vanishes identically at row
i
. This reduces to the deterministic scheme. Therefore, a similar argument can yield

\|\bar{\bm{U}}_{i,\cdot}^{t+1,-i}-\bm{U}^{*}_{i,\cdot}\|\leq\Big(1-\frac{1}{2}\eta\alpha\sigma_{\min}\Big)\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i,\cdot}^{t,-i}-\bm{U}^{*}_{i,\cdot}\|+C\eta{\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)}\omega_{*}.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
11
, we can show the following: suppose
(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})
and
(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})
lie within the same local neighborhood of the reference
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
, and let their optimal alignments to
(\widetilde{\bm{U}},\widetilde{\bm{V}})
be
\bm{G}_{1}
and
\bm{G}_{2}
. If

\max\left\{n^{-1/2}\|\bm{U}_{1}\bm{G}_{0}-\bm{U}_{2}\bm{H}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{F}},q^{-1/2}\|\bm{V}_{1}\bm{G}_{0}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{V}_{2}\bm{H}_{0}^{-{\intercal}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right\}\leq\delta

for two well-conditioned alignments
\bm{G}_{0},\bm{H}_{0}
, then there is

\|\bm{G}_{1}-\bm{G}_{2}\|\vee\|\bm{G}_{1}^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{G}_{2}^{-{\intercal}}\|\leq C\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}.

Then with
(\bm{U}_{1},\bm{V}_{1})=(\bar{\bm{U}}^{t+1,-i},\bar{\bm{V}}^{t+1,-i})
, and
(\bm{U}_{2},\bm{V}_{2})=(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{t+1},\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t+1})
and we know

\big\|\bm{G}_{t+1}^{-i}-\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger}\big\|\vee\big\|(\bm{G}_{t+1}^{-i})^{-{\intercal}}-(\bm{G}_{t+1}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}\big\|\leq C\frac{\bar{a}_{t+1}^{\mathrm{row}}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}}.

The smallness of
\bar{a}_{t+1}^{\mathrm{row}}
and the induction hypothesis for
\|\bm{G}_{s}^{\dagger}-\bm{R}^{0}\|\vee\|(\bm{G}_{s}^{\dagger})^{-{\intercal}}-\bm{R}^{0}\|
yields (
F.40
).

Now we are ready to bound
\|(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t}\|_{2\to\infty}
and
\|(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})^{\intercal}\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t}\|_{2\to\infty}
for step
t
. We analyze
\bm{e}_{i}^{\intercal}(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t}=\bm{e}_{i}^{\intercal}(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})(\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})
.
We write
\bm{W}=\nu_{\star}(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})
and for each
i\in[n]
, decompose
\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t}=(\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})+(\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}-\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})+(\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}-\bm{V}^{*})+(\bm{V}^{*}-\bar{\bm{V}})+(\bar{\bm{V}}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})
. Then

\displaystyle q^{-1}\big\|\{\bm{W}\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t}\}_{i,}\big\|\leq{}

\displaystyle q^{-1}\|\bm{W}_{i,}(\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})\|+q^{-1}\big\|\bm{W}_{i,}(\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}-\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i})\big\|

(F.52)

\displaystyle+q^{-1}\big\|\bm{W}_{i,}(\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}-\bm{V}^{*})\big\|+q^{-1}\big\|\bm{W}_{i,}(\bm{V}^{*}-\bar{\bm{V}})\big\|+q^{-1}\big\|\bm{W}_{i,}(\bar{\bm{V}}-\widetilde{\bm{V}})\big\|

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle T_{1,t}+T_{2,t}+T_{3,t}+T_{4,t}+T_{5,t}.

The first term is bounded by Cauchy’s inequality and the row-norm concentration for
\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*}
. Thus

T_{1,t}\leq q^{-1}\nu_{\star}\|\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\|\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq Ca_{t}^{\rm row}\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}(q\beta+\nu_{\star}L_{\star})}{q}}.

By the initialization assumptions, we further have

T_{1,t}\leq Ca_{0}^{\rm row}\Big\{\frac{\nu_{\star}(q\beta+\nu_{\star}L_{\star})}{q}\Big\}^{1/2}\leq c_{0}C\alpha\sigma_{\min}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\omega_{*}+C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\omega_{*}.

Here, the second inequality follows from (
F.37
) and the scaling conditions in (
A.2
).

For the second term, (
F.49
) and
\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}-\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}=\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\big\{\check{\bm{G}}_{t}^{-i}(\bm{G}_{t}^{-i})^{-1}-\bm{I}_{r}\big\}^{{\intercal}},
imply

\displaystyle T_{2,i}\leq

\displaystyle\,q^{-1}\nu_{\star}\|(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})_{i,}\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\|\times\|\bm{I}_{r}-\check{\bm{G}}_{t}^{-i}(\bm{G}_{t}^{-i})^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\left\{\tau_{*}\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta L_{\star}}{q}}+\nu_{\star}\omega_{*}\frac{L_{\star}}{q}\right\}\left(\rho^{t}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}+\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\right)\frac{\tau_{*}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\min}}},

where for
q^{-1}\|(\bm{Y}-\bm{P}^{*})_{i,}\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\|
we applied the Bernstein inequality with
q^{-1/2}\|\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C\tau_{*}
and
\|\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq C\omega_{*}
, as
\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}
is
\mathcal{F}_{-i}
-measurable. Using
d_{t}\leq\rho^{t}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}\tau_{*}
,
\tau_{*}\leq\sqrt{r\kappa\sigma_{\min}}
,
\tau_{*}\leq\sqrt{r}\,\omega_{*}
,
\zeta_{r}\phi_{nq}^{\dagger}/\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\leq c_{0}
, and
(
A.2
), we obtain

T_{2,t}\leq c_{0}\alpha\sigma_{\min}\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\omega_{*}+C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\omega_{*}.

For the third term, from the theorem’s scaling condition and (
F.49
), (
F.37
), we use
\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}-\bm{V}^{*}=\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}-\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}+\widetilde{\bm{V}}^{t}-\widetilde{\bm{V}}+\widetilde{\bm{V}}-\bm{V}^{*}
to obtain

\displaystyle T_{3,i}\leq

\displaystyle\,C\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta L_{\star}}{q}}\times q^{-1/2}\|\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+C\frac{\nu_{\star}L_{\star}}{q}\times\|\check{\bm{V}}^{t,-i}-\bm{V}^{*}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{\star}\beta L_{\star}}{q}}\left\{a_{t}^{\rm row}+d_{t}+\frac{\Delta_{2}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\tau_{*}\right\}+C\frac{\nu_{\star}L_{\star}}{q}\omega_{*}.

Since
\sqrt{{\nu_{\star}\beta L_{\star}}/{q}}\leq\big\{(\nu_{\star}q\beta+\nu_{\star}^{2}L_{\star})/q\big\}^{1/2}
under (
A.2
),
a_{t}^{\rm row}
can be controlled by (
F.37
). The
d_{t}
part is controlled by the same argument as in the treatment of
T_{2,t}
, and the remaining terms are absorbed into
C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\omega_{*}
by
(
A.2
). Then we arrive at

T_{3,t}\leq c_{0}\alpha\sigma_{\min}\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\omega_{*}+C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\omega_{*}.

The fourth term is exactly Lemma
13
, because changing the sign of the noise matrix does not affect the norm:

T_{4,t}\leq C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\omega_{*}.

For the fifth term, apply (
E.2
), Bernstein’s row bound, and (
A.2
) to get

\displaystyle T_{5,t}\leq C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\omega_{*}.

Combining the bounds for
T_{1,t}
–
T_{5,t}
and shrinking the constants
in (
A.2
), we obtain uniformly over
i\in[n]
, we know
q^{-1}\|\{\bm{W}\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t}\}_{i,\cdot}\|\leq\alpha\sigma_{\min}\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\omega_{*}/8+C\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\omega_{*}
.
Taking the maximum over
i\in[n]
gives the upper bound for
\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger,U}
in (
E.4
). The bound for
\delta_{3,t}^{\dagger,V}
in (
E.4
) can be obtained by symmetry.

Finally, replacing (
D.76
) in Step 3 by
(
F.41
) gives again

\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t+1},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t+1})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\rho\|(\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{U}^{t},\widetilde{\bm{E}}_{V}^{t})\|_{2,\infty}+C\eta\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)\omega_{*}.

Following the same strategy, one can prove

r_{t}\leq\rho^{t}\psi_{nq}^{\dagger}\omega_{*}+C\frac{\Delta_{\infty}(n,q,\delta)}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}}\omega_{*}.

The derivation of the induction hypothesis

(4)
in Section
D.3
is unchanged, since it only uses the Frobenius norm contraction. This closes the original induction and completes the proof of Lemma
14
.

∎
